# Open issues

Everything known to be open: the design, the prototype, the runtime and measurements, and the documents. The current design is the [references](reference/README.md), with the rationale in [`design.md`](design.md); the decisions, with their origins, are in [`record/decisions.md`](record/decisions.md). The current measurements are `design/evidence.md`; what Julia gained is `design/julia-changes.md`.

Only open items are listed in sections 1–6. Items keep their numbers, so that references such as "§1.10" stay valid; the gaps are closed items, which are listed at the end, under [Closed items](#closed-items), with how and where they were closed. Commits are in the repository named (julia-1.13, TraitWorld, a demonstrator).

**How items are kept** (the user's rule of 2026-10-27, [decisions](record/decisions.md) §8; applied by the clean-up, W-65, [its proposal](plans/2026-10-27-cleanup-issues-and-queue.md)): a principled choice, or one that simplifies at little cost, is adopted; one that working with trait code would inform waits for the ports (§6); one that evidence can settle names its experiment; otherwise it is accepted as it is. Only choices with a well-articulated trade-off wait for the user (§1). Performance work is in the [work queue](record/todo.md), or, where the rule for performance work does not take it up, in [the performance explorations log](record/performance-explorations.md).

## 1. Decisions waiting for the user

**W-141, input to the synthesis:** a membership behaves as a method does ([compiler §5.6](reference/compiler.md#56-the-memberships-method-and-the-edges), [traits §12.2](reference/traits.md#122-pirates)). What the mechanism leaves is [open issue 180](#2-design); the options not taken are in [alternatives](design/alternatives.md#a-membership-as-a-method-options-not-taken-2026-11-12); [the review](reviews/2026-11-12-w141-membership-as-method.md).

**The ports' fresh-ideas round (2026-10-28)** ([SciML](plans/2026-10-28-fresh-ideas-sciml.md), [Makie](plans/2026-10-28-fresh-ideas-makie.md), [DataFrames](plans/2026-10-28-fresh-ideas-dataframes.md)) raised items 82–94; all are decided (closed items, §1).

**The machine-code cache's review (W-120; [the review](reviews/2026-11-07-machine-code-cache-design.md), 2026-11-07), to revisit now** (the user (2026-11-11): "We should probably revisit the cross session cache now." A proposal comes first; it asks the question under G-3, since speed work is stopped. Earlier, the user, 2026-11-07: "Let's revisit this later. Do no regret work now. Building the ports is definitely sensible"): it finds that no cache reaches the goal alone (a perfect one leaves the DataFrames session at 5.5–6× pandas, E-234), while TraitReport's small cores already match pandas and matplotlib (E-233). It proposes, in place of the order the user accepted:
1. W-104 with DataFrames first, then Makie (not SciML);
2. a goal gate, `goal.jl` against `goal.py`, registered and measured on the unified build;
3. package-image workloads for the goal's shapes;
4. C-40 built as the reference says (E-281's 1,070 invalidations per load);
5. validation broken down by the G2 bit;
6. a session cache keyed on the environment, admitting all code by the G2 bit (open issue 95's two layers), with C-65 fixed first;
7. only on measured manifest churn, C-52 and relinking by stable names.

It recommends revisiting: facts-only scope (2026-10-27), the environment-keyed cache's retirement (2026-10-27), relinking as step 3's vehicle (2026-10-28), C-52 first and SciML first (2026-11-07, mine). It keeps the slot (rerun its cost 3×) and no boundary flag on Base.

**Flagged: tensions between the goals** (the user, 2026-11-10: "I want to continue the strategy of recording pressure and tension between the goals (as we did for pushback on the design from speed and port work). The goal is not for me to review this bit by bit but to eventually create a synthesis, and iterate the design from all the evidence collected."; before it, 2026-11-08: "Where a design decision makes speed difficult to achieve, flag that for me to review."; [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md)). Each entry is a rule that sets one goal against another, with its measured cost and a proposal. The index, with each entry's `GT-n` and its axes, is [the goals](goals.md) §3. No entry is worked on for speed (the user, 2026-11-10: "I don't want to claim better steady state speed, just no regression/the possibility to opt into full speed (compiled_for(x)). I want to stop pursuing speed for now. We have gained enough to be satisfied. The IO work also shows the difficulty of reasonable solutions to isolated slowdowns in an existing ecosystem."); the entries are input to the synthesis.
- *GT-1.* **Decided (2026-11-11, the user): a membership invalidates as a method definition does; one by neither owner is a pirate's** ([facts §10](reference/facts.md#10-memberships), [traits §3.4](reference/traits.md#34-open-and-closed); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §4). **Built** (W-140; `unified` a19d937eaa, TraitWorld 4718b6e): no call carries a guard, a negative folds with its edge, a membership's walk invalidates the callers it changes; compiler C-88 for what is left. **Not measured on this build:** the library's load at a REPL (the 3,600 log entries below), open issue 158's two cases, composition and the unrelated loads; the rounds wait for the conceptually complete state (W-140). The entry as it was flagged: **"A membership invalidates nothing"** (the committed guarantee, [facts §10](reference/facts.md#10-memberships); the user, 2026-11-06, open issue 146), with **"negatives never fold"** ([compiler §4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries)). The price is paid in code that never uses traits once the library is loaded:
  - every call that a later membership could change carries a guard, and its result joins the shadowing method's declared result (`Any` where none is declared);
  - a negative test at an open set never folds, so inference keeps the arm it would prune.
  - *Measured so far:* open issue 158's two cases in upstream CSV and DataFrames (144 bytes per cell read; `@inferred` failing). *Counted* (E-319, with 158 (a) built): the forks' steps hold no guarded call; the goal's task, setting D and NetworkDynamics' workload one each, in TraitMakie's image.
  - *Measured, its other side* (E-296, build B1): the guarantee moves the invalidation from the membership to the method at the set (compiler §5.6 of that build: a method at a set invalidates, at its insertion, the callers with a pruned arm at a type that could join). TraitWorld's methods at sets for required operations (`iterate(::Iterable)`, `getindex(::Keyed)`, `length(::HasLength)`, `resize!(::Resizable)`) reach the system image's code wherever a `Nothing` arm was pruned, the compiler's included: about 3,600 log entries at each session's load of TraitWorld. At a REPL the replacements TraitWorld's image carries are rejected (REPL's interpreter methods), and the forks' session takes 9.9–10.4 s against 6.5 s. Open issue 158 (a) (being built, E-297) removes it for required operations; a trait default's method at a set keeps it.
  - *Proposals* ([the review of 2026-11-08](reviews/2026-11-08-issue-158-guarantee.md)): count first (W-124, the counter records what each option would remove); open issue 158 (a), sharpened, with C-36, which keep the guarantee; **(d), finality by ownership, decided opt-in** (the user, 2026-11-09: "Finality should be opt in."): a trait closed after load ([traits §3.4](reference/traits.md#34-open-and-closed)) has its negatives final once their declarers have finished loading, and a membership declared later invalidates; every other trait keeps the guarantee (W-129); (c) only for a hot shape that (a) and (d) leave.

- *GT-2.* **Strict mode infers an image's memberships at its load** ([traits §12.3](reference/traits.md#123-strict-mode); mine, 2026-11-10; E-353). Strict mode only. *Measured:* the load of TraitCSV, TraitTables and TraitMakie under strict mode 0.43 s, of it about 70 ms this inference the first time (68 checked results); 7.6 ms with inference cached; 0.28–0.30 s without strict mode ([evidence](design/evidence.md)). *Proposal:* keep it, since strict mode is the checking mode; if the cost matters, infer only the checked calls whose functions got a method from outside the image's own packages before its load.

- *GT-3.* **"The compiler is compiled plainly"** ([facts §6.4](reference/facts.md#64-the-compilers-own-code)), and Base's code too in the product setting. Plain code commits across abstract calls, so a plain package's ordinary method can invalidate it. On the unified build, loading upstream CSV and DataFrames invalidated 1,998 of the compiler's instances (193 on vanilla), and the session compiled the compiler again (E-292, E-294; mostly untyped spots in the stack's own compiler code, fixed in 28d2334164, E-295 to measure); TraitWorld's image hook, plain code too, recompiles at load (2.7 s). Code compiled by facts is invalidated only by a stakeholder or a winning pirate (G2): under the test bed `all`, a late `using DataFrames` recompiles 0.02 s against plain Julia's 6.1 s (E-177).
  - *What it costs:* the compiler's abstract calls (dispatch over lattice elements and call infos) become run-time sites, so inference runs slower unless those positions get declared results; Base by facts needs its own system image (PE-23: an empty session under `all` takes 24–25 s without one, E-275).
  - *Measured for the library* (E-307): TraitWorld's hooks were invalidated by ForwardDiff's `promote_rule` through ranges over untyped bounds; typing them fixed it. TraitWorld compiled by facts fixed it too but made every session compile the library's facts code. *Measured again* (E-312, no plain image code left): the check code is reached through run-time sites (its arguments are `Any`), and the dependents' images drop it, since it fails G2 by inlining Base's plain code at `Any` values (`push!(::Vector{Any}, …)`); TraitReport (t) 1.01–1.03 s against 0.75–0.78 s. *Measured after C-75* (E-322): the check code is G2 and the dependents' images keep it, but the library's own image lacks the instances its run-time sites reach (`@nospecialize` methods at `Any`, closures over untyped values), so each session compiles them: the goal's task +0.1 s, setting D +0.2 s, (t) +0.15 s. *With a rule for the images* (E-326; [compiler §9](reference/compiler.md#9-images-and-the-machine-code-cache), mine): an image holds the code a facts run-time site reaches whatever the values. With it, and TraitWorld's closures over untyped values written as loops, the sessions compile none of TraitWorld's code and equal E-324's; TraitWorld is compiled by facts (`unified` c9f701e). What is left: the goal's read step reaches Base's methods at concrete types through run-time sites in the check code (17 ms); check code typed throughout would reach them statically ([PE-28](record/performance-explorations.md)).
  - *Measured for a port* (E-302): plain packages loaded after the forks' CSV invalidated 752 of the forks' and TraitMakie's image instances; the CSV reader compiled by facts keeps its image code valid (the goal's read 0.03 s against 0.27 s) and compiles the task closures of a new type in the session instead.
  - *Measured for validation* (E-325): of the calls a load checks by signature, the costliest edges, 732 of 882 are in plain code failing G2 (DataFrames' image: 486), other modules' code compiled at the packages' types. Validation by the G2 bit would skip 80 % of the instances the loads bring but 17 % of those edges.
  - *Proposals:* (1) measure first: the compiler's module compiled by facts, in a system image so built: inference's speed and the invalidations at load; (2) where inference slows, declared results at the compiler's hot abstract positions (a trait for lattice elements, one for call infos), found by the dispatch profiler; (3) TraitWorld's runtime as facts code: done, with compiler §9's rule for run-time sites (E-326); (4) until then, the stack's compiler code held to no dynamic call of Base's generic functions (`experiments/unification/e289/dyncalls.jl` as a gate).

- *GT-4.* **"A cast decides only at an argument typed by a trait"** ([traits §7.6](reference/traits.md#76-casts), step 2 of [§7.5](reference/traits.md#75-what-decides); [open issue 162](#2-design)). A `compiled_for` at a parameter no trait types has no effect, and nothing says so.
  - *Measured* (E-310 (b), E-314): NetworkDynamics' steady state 436–475 ms with its batch call's cast at an untyped parameter, 228–257 ms with the parameter typed by the trait.
  - *Proposals:* the idiom (type the parameter by its trait) with a package's tests checking that no cast is without effect (162 (b), (c)), or the attribute through untyped parameters (162 (a)).

- *GT-5.* **A closed type's contract promises no result** ([compiler §7.1](reference/compiler.md#71-the-rule), [§7.5](reference/compiler.md#75-io); open issue 171, closed; mine, 2026-11-10). `IO`'s members are unchecked, so each operation's result is `Any`: correct for every stream, and Julia's own answer at a receiver typed `IO`.
  - *Measured* (E-340): through the eraser `IOContext{IO}`, `write(io, ::Char)` 4.99× the type known (1.85× with `IO`'s declared `Int` counts, E-337), `print "ab"` 2.63× (2.16×); a display of a vector 1.09×. Base's `write(io, ::Char)` adds the counts of its byte writes, a dynamic `+` when each is `Any`.
  - *Recommended* (mine, 2026-11-10): (a). The rule is principled and (b) is a patch at a case; no gain across the board is in reach here.
  - *Proposals:* (a) leave it: byte-level writes reach the eraser only from code that erased a stream on purpose, and §9b keeps such kernels per type; (b) Base's counting writes (`write(io, ::Char)`, `write(io, ::String)`) annotate each count `::Int` where the stream is erased, raising for a stream whose count is another integer type there only; (c) the eraser's forwarders typed by `IOContext{IO}` return the inner call's result unchanged but infer it from the candidates' joined results (the 171 plan's alternative (d)), which needs the fallback's result too and gains nothing while it is `Any`.

- *GT-6.* **The attribute resolves at the method's declared type, not at the caller's knowledge** ([compiler §6.3](reference/compiler.md#63-the-attribute-at-a-call), step 4 of [traits §7.5](reference/traits.md#75-what-decides); [the performance review](reviews/2026-11-09-performance-day.md) M5). A set whose default is `Dynamic` compiles its methods once, and a caller that knows the member's concrete type still reaches that instance.
  - *Measured:* Base's writers 1.2–24× vanilla's with `IO` a set (E-330, E-333); NetworkDynamics' steady state 2× until its batch kernel was typed by the trait (E-314). `IO` left the sets; TraitDifferentialEquations' and NetworkDynamics' sets have not.
  - *Proposals:* the attribute at a call resolved by what the caller knows where it knows a concrete member (the plan on compiled-once equations, rule 4, Optional); the idiom of open issue 162 meanwhile.

- *GT-7.* **The firewall's pointer per call** ([compiler §7.2](reference/compiler.md#72-the-split); open issue 167 (a), mine; [the performance review](reviews/2026-11-09-performance-day.md) M4, M5): a split's branch calls its candidate's target through a pointer and inlines only a leaf, trading per-call speed for isolation from invalidation.
  - *Measured:* through `IOContext{IO}` a byte or a short string per call 1.5–2.2× the hand-routed proxy (E-337); the benefit (17,035 → 6,687 instances invalidated at the goal's load, E-332) was measured with `IO` a set. Every split of every set pays the pointer, the ports' `Dynamic` paths among them; NetworkDynamics was last measured before it (E-327).
  - *Measured in composition* (E-358; [evidence §1](design/evidence.md#1-the-guarantee)): a member that joins after a loop over the trait is compiled is called through a pointer made at its first use, `Base._split_pointer` for (operation, member type). Making it costs 8.3–8.6 ms per operation and member. No image holds it: at a member's image build it is the only member, and none is made. So each session pays it again (G-3).
  - *Proposals:* E-327 again on the head before the rule is stated in §7.2; the code slot (§3.3) where the pointer's cost shows (167). For the pointer's making: one made without compiling per (operation, member), or made at a member's image build for each operation of its `@implement`.

- *GT-8.* **G2 covers less on the unified build** ([compiler §3.2](reference/compiler.md#32-g2-as-a-predicate), [§3.3](reference/compiler.md#33-the-code-slot)): one CodeInstance per MethodInstance and policy, so facts code that rests on a package's own plain methods is compiled again when they are.
  - *Measured* (E-265, on the earlier build made to lose what the unified rule loses): the forks' session's work after its loads 0.2–0.3 s more with the code slot, 0.7–1.0 s without it. On the unified build, 99.8 % and 99.9 % of the facts instances of two workloads satisfy G2 (E-278).
  - *Measured on the unified build* (E-355; [evidence §1](design/evidence.md#1-the-guarantee)): facts code also keeps an edge to each plain callee in Base, so a load that invalidates one ends its facts callers. Nine unrelated loads end 48 of the forks' 4,796 own instances (1.0 %; plain upstream 9.2 %), 47 of them facts code. They are the reader's entry chain (`CSV.File(::String)`, `CSV.read`, `resolvesource`, `_parse`), so the forks' work after the loads takes 0.96–0.98 s against 0.063 s first. Upstream compiled by facts as it is loses 14 of 3,487, and its work 0.48–0.66 s against 0.67–0.70 s.
  - *The Base callees on the way:* `vcat(::Int, ::Vector{Int})` (SparseArrays' `_cat`), `open` and `write` under `Downloads.download` (FilePathsBase), `promote_rule` (IntervalArithmetic), `Colon` (Unitful), `any` and `reduce_first` (FillArrays), `isempty` (OffsetArrays), `getindex` (StaticArrays), `convert` (GeometryBasics). The tool that lists them for any environment: `experiments/unification/w134/cause.jl`.
  - *Proposals,* for the synthesis: (a) Base compiled by facts in the product setting, so the callees are facts code whose record a load's method at another type does not touch (GT-3 sets this against "the compiler is compiled plainly"); (b) the boundary flag ([compiler §2.2](reference/compiler.md#22-the-boundary)) on the Base methods `cause.jl` names, which makes each such call a run-time site; (c) the code slot ([compiler §3.3](reference/compiler.md#33-the-code-slot)) keeping a facts caller whose callee is compiled again to the same result: the 48 were lost with the slot built, so what it kept here is to be counted. No port is changed to avoid these callees.

- *GT-9.* **The membership machinery costs code that uses no trait** ([compiler §4](reference/compiler.md), [§5.1](reference/compiler.md#51-the-method-table)): memberships are consulted in subtyping and method matching for every type.
  - *Measured:* a non-member's `<:` against a set 1.7–1.8× an unrelated pair's in vanilla (E-270); method matching, on inference and dispatch misses only, 7–25 % more with a method at a set on Base's hottest functions (E-269, PE-21) and 4–8 % more with none (PE-22); Base's test files 2–6 % slower, one run (E-262); with BaseTraits loaded, `arrayops` and `dict` within the noise (E-282).
  - *Measured in a session* (E-356; upstream CSV and DataFrames compiled plainly, `unified` 36b653bb3b against vanilla 45b6d15e34; [evidence §4c](design/evidence.md#4c-the-unifications-trade-offs-measured-on-todays-build)): the first work is not slower (3.02–3.04 s against 3.24 s; 3.14 s with the library loaded first). DataFrames' repeated steps on a small table are 8–18 % slower (`describe` 19.2 against 17.6 µs, `sort` 8.0 against 6.9 µs, `groupby`-`combine` 45.2 against 40.4 µs); the read is within the runs' scatter. The library loaded adds nothing to the repeated steps.
  - *After W-140* (`unified` a19d937eaa): a call carries no guard and its result is its own; whether DataFrames' repeated steps move is not measured (W-140's rounds).
  - *Proposal:* leave it; state it as the design's cost to plain code. Which part of the 8–18 % is dynamic dispatch through the per-call-site and leaf caches is not separated ([PE-24](record/performance-explorations.md)); no work pursues it.

- *GT-11.* **A method at an open set on one of Base's functions ends plain callers when it is added** ([traits §10.1](reference/traits.md#101-basetraits), BaseTraits' operations on `length`, `iterate`, `haskey`; [compiler §5.1](reference/compiler.md#51-the-method-table)): any subtype of a plain abstract type may be a member, so the method meets every call at such a type. Plain code that resolved the call there is invalidated, as by any method at a wide signature.
  - *Measured* (E-356): after upstream CSV's and DataFrames' work, `using TraitWorld` ends 197 of 123,269 plain instances (Base 85, DataFrames 77, Statistics 30), and the work after it takes 0.25–0.26 s against 0.19 ms repeated. `length(::HasLength)` ends 191 log entries, `iterate(::Iterable{T})` 71, `haskey(::Keyed, ::Any)` 10. Ended: a caller at a plain abstract type with few subtypes, whose call was resolved. Kept: callers at concrete types, struct families, Base's abstract types and `Any` (`experiments/unification/w134/probe_insert.jl`). With the library loaded first nothing is ended, and the first work is 3 % slower (3.14 against 3.03 s). E-281's session of array, dictionary and matrix code had no such caller.
  - *After W-140* (`unified` a19d937eaa): a method at a set invalidates the callers at its members, and no longer a caller with a pruned arm at a type that could join; a caller at a plain abstract type that meets the set is still reached, by Julia's rule for a method at a wide signature. The count is not measured again (W-140's rounds).
  - *The tool:* `experiments/unification/w134/cause.jl` with `CAUSE_WORK` and `CAUSE_ROOTS`, for any library and session.
  - *Proposals,* for the synthesis: (a) the sets on Base's functions are Base's own, defined before any package is compiled (the upstream form; the late load is the prototype's); (b) BaseTraits' operations as its own functions, as `LinearOp`'s `apply!` is, at the price of members' own `length` and `iterate` no longer serving them; (c) the guideline: the library is loaded before plain work, by the packages that use it. The library is not changed for this case.

- *GT-12.* **The definition-time check runs at each edit** ([compiler §6.7](reference/compiler.md#67-the-definition-time-check), [traits §11.2](reference/traits.md#112-definition-checking)): a method at a trait is inferred at the trait when it is defined, so its error is raised at the definition. An edit at the REPL defines the method again.
  - *Measured* (E-359; [evidence §1](design/evidence.md#1-the-guarantee)): evaluating TraitTables' `summarize(c::Column, name::String)` again takes 2.82 s the first time in a session and 0.40–0.42 s the second, against 82–94 ms and 8–9 ms for the plain twin's. The first time compiles the check interpreter's own code, which no image holds; each later check infers the method and what it calls afresh. A method with no trait argument is not checked (ODECore's `save_step!`: 106 ms, then 13 ms).
  - *The tool:* `experiments/unification/w134/repl.jl` (the edit of any function of a session's packages, timed) and `probes/probe_edit.jl` (its profile).
  - *Proposals,* for the synthesis: (a) the check interpreter's code in an image (the library's, by a check of one of its own methods at its build); (b) the check's inference results kept between checks, as the native interpreter's are, ended as code is; (c) the check of an edited method deferred to its first call, with the definition's error raised there (it sets the place of the error, G-5, against the edit's time).

- *GT-13.* **Decided (2026-11-11, the user: "Let's consider this resolved."): a closed build compiles generic code for the members it has** ([compiler §7.6](reference/compiler.md#76-a-closed-build), [traits §16](reference/traits.md#16-compiling-ahead-closed-programs); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §6; W-139, E-364). The entry as it was flagged: **a trimmed build refuses the call on a value known only by its trait** ([traits §16](reference/traits.md#16-compiling-ahead-closed-programs), [§7.9](reference/traits.md#79-operations-on-a-value-the-code-knows-only-by-its-trait); [compiler §2.6](reference/compiler.md#26-ahead-of-time-builds)): code compiled once at a trait operates on its value by a call dispatched at run time. `--trim=safe` refuses every such call, though every member is declared and `verify_main` lists every target.
  - *Measured* (E-361; [evidence §4](design/evidence.md#4-builds-and-binaries)): no demonstrator builds under `--trim=safe` (verifier errors: table 8, ODE 122, plot 34, the report 44), and none runs to its end under `--trim=unsafe-warn`. The plain twins do neither (table 10, ODE 14, plot 86, the report 96; vanilla 12, 14, 88, 98), nor does upstream CSV with DataFrames (770; vanilla 766). With `verify_main`'s entry points the table program gets past the members' operations and the cast, and stops at a call on the cast's result, which the report lists.
  - *What it shows:* compiling once (G-1) leaves the calls that a closed build must resolve (G-6). The trait form knows their targets; the build has no use for that knowledge.
  - *The tool:* `experiments/unification/w135/` (`run_safe.sh`, `run_warn.sh`, `summarize.py`: the verifier's errors by module and root; `entrypoints.jl`).
  - *Proposals,* for the synthesis: (a) in a closed build, a call on a value known by its trait is compiled as a split over the declared members; (b) the verifier accepts a dynamic call whose targets are closed and compiled, as `verify_main` lists them; (c) a closed build compiles a method at a trait per member, as plain Julia would.

- *GT-14.* **A membership is seen from the world of its declaration** ([compiler §4.3](reference/compiler.md#43-worlds); [facts §10](reference/facts.md#10-memberships)): a membership has a world, as a method has: code compiled before it keeps its answer in its own world. Code that runs in an earlier world does not see it: a function that evaluates a declaration or loads a package and goes on, a task started before.
  - *Measured* (E-362, case 08; [evidence §4d](design/evidence.md#4d-error-messages-against-plain-julias); found first by E-358's session script): a function declares a member and stores a value of it in a `Vector{Shape}`. The trait form raises `MethodError`: "Cannot `convert` an object of type Circle to an object of type Shape. The applicable method may be too new: running in world age …". The plain twin runs: a subtype is seen at once, in every world.
  - *What it shows:* the guarantee's mechanism (G-1) adds a failure that plain Julia's subtyping does not have, and its message names a method and a world, not the membership (G-5). A program that loads plugins from a running loop meets it on the path (G-7).
  - *The tool:* `experiments/unification/w136/` (case 08, both forms).
  - *Proposals,* for the synthesis: (a) a hint on that `MethodError` and on a `TypeError` at a set: where the value's type has a membership in the set in a later world, the message says so and names `invokelatest`; (b) `intro/` teaches a membership as a definition, with a method's rule for running code; (c) no change to the worlds: a membership seen at once would change the answers of code running in an older world. With W-140 a membership invalidates as a method does, so the world is a method's world in every respect, and (b) is the whole account.

- *GT-15.* **Decided (2026-11-11, the user: "Build members by use."): a family's members in a closed build are the types the program uses as members.** Built (`unified` d8cebc40fe; [compiler §7.6](reference/compiler.md#76-a-closed-build), C-86 for what it does not count): `Column` has 5 members in the table program, which builds trimmed and runs (E-364). The entry as it was flagged: **A closed build has every member of a trait, and a family over another owner's type has hundreds** ([compiler §7.6](reference/compiler.md#76-a-closed-build), C-86; [traits §16](reference/traits.md#16-compiling-ahead-closed-programs)): a trait is open so that a library can say "every `Vector{T}` is a `Column{T}`" ([traits §5](reference/traits.md#5-joining-a-trait)); a closed build branches over the members it has, and for a family it has every instantiation the process made.
  - *Measured* (E-364, `unified` 5d8da002fa; `experiments/unification/w139/README.md`): TraitTables' `Column{T}` with `@implement Column{T} Vector{T} where {T}` has 250 members in a session, past the limit of 64. The table program uses four. Its operations at `Column` are left to run time: 44 of the build's 50 errors. TraitCSV's `CSVColumn`, with five members at concrete types, splits, and `readtable` holds no run-time call. BaseTraits' sets at Base's abstract types (`Indexable{T}` at `AbstractVector{T}`) are not listed at all.
  - *What it shows:* the same declaration that makes a trait useful in a session (G-7: one line covers a family) makes it unbounded for a build (G-6). Rust has the tension too and resolves it by use: a generic is compiled for the types the program instantiates it with, a `dyn Trait` has a vtable for the types the program coerces.
  - *The tool:* `experiments/unification/w139/dbg_table.jl` (the members a closed build lists, and what is left dynamic, from a session); `closed_candidates` for any trait.
  - *Proposals,* for the synthesis: (a) **members by use**, as rapid type analysis does it for a class hierarchy: a family's and an abstract type's members are the concrete types that appear in the signatures of the instances the build compiles, found in the rounds the build already runs; a value of another type raises, which G1 allows; (b) a declaration in the program, `closed_members(Column, Vector{Int}, …)`, checked by the build; (c) the limit of 64 raised with a table built at build time, which keeps every instantiation and grows the binary. (a) needs no new syntax and matches what the two languages named in the goal do.
- *GT-16.* **Fully typed mode raises exactly where a build refuses, or fully typed code pays nothing** ([traits §14](reference/traits.md#14-concreteness-and-fully-typed-mode); [compiler §2.7](reference/compiler.md#27-fully-typed-mode); traits T-100, T-104; found 2026-11-14 with W-142). The mode's check is a prologue the compiler puts only into code where the session's inference found a run-time site; the prologue then asks the build's check. So a call that only the build refuses, inside plain code the method calls, is reported by `fully_typed` and not raised (T-100), and a plain caller that inlines the method runs it without the prologue (T-104). *The measured cost of the other way:* the build's check takes 2 to 5 s for a small program and 4 to 11 s for a demonstrator's (E-369); asked at the first run of every signature of every method in the mode, it would be the mode's price for fully typed code too. *Proposal:* keep the rule (fully typed code pays nothing; the mode raises where the session sees a site and the build agrees), and state the exact question as `fully_typed`'s, asked in a package's tests and before a build, where seconds are acceptable. Mine; for the synthesis.


- *GT-10.* **An isolated slowdown in an existing ecosystem has no reasonable local fix** (the user, 2026-11-10: "The IO work also shows the difficulty of reasonable solutions to isolated slowdowns in an existing ecosystem."; W-129): the forks' `show` step at a REPL was slow because display code is compiled per stream type.
  - *Measured:* `IO` as a set compiled once made the goal's task 15.8–16.1 s against the control's 4.08–4.28 s (E-330); after the lookup, the firewall, the final negatives and the backedges, 3.5 s against 2.5 s (E-336); with `IO` a closed type with a contract and an eraser, 1.76–1.80 s against 2.46–2.57 s (E-337), at 1.5–5× per erased byte-level write (GT-5, GT-7).
  - *What it shows:* a trait laid over a type that the whole ecosystem already specializes on moves every caller. The fix that held is a contract on the closed type, chosen by the author who erases.
  - *Proposal:* the porting guideline says so (W-137): a port does not turn an ecosystem-wide abstract type into a set to cure one slow step.

**The agent's choices, in force: input to the synthesis, not waiting for the user's review** (the user, 2026-11-10: "I don't want to review all decisions made by you."; [the goals](goals.md) §4). Each is in its reference and in [the decision log](record/decisions.md) marked "mine":
- **The check that answers for the build is the build's own code, run in the session** ([compiler §2.7](reference/compiler.md#27-fully-typed-mode); [traits §14](reference/traits.md#14-concreteness-and-fully-typed-mode), [§16](reference/traits.md#16-compiling-ahead-closed-programs); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §6; W-142, E-369): mine (2026-11-12). Not a second walk with the rules written again, and not a child process. Fully typed mode raises only where it lists a call (T-100); `make_executable` runs it first. The check and the build agree on 17 of 18 programs.
- **The check infers against the Base a build compiles, read from the build script's files into an overlay method table** ([traits §16](reference/traits.md#16-compiling-ahead-closed-programs); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §6; [open issue 181](#2-design); traits T-109): mine (2026-11-14). The session's methods are not changed; what the script changes that is no method is not read.
- **`OperationError` for an operation called outside its declared signature; a check's error raised at the user's line** ([traits §11.3](reference/traits.md#113-errors-and-their-messages), [compiler §10.1](reference/compiler.md#101-definition-and-image-hooks); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §2; open issue 177 (a), (b), closed; E-370): mine (2026-11-12).
- **A method is fully bounded or unbounded; `@unbounded`** ([traits §7.3](reference/traits.md#73-fully-bounded-methods); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; open issue 176, closed): mine (2026-11-12). *Open* is kept for traits and types.
- **A `Callable`'s check is decided by the declaration, in every instance** ([compiler §7.4](reference/compiler.md#74-callables-call), [traits §9](reference/traits.md#9-functions-callable); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; compiler C-90): mine (2026-11-14). A function compiled for its type, or a constant of the caller, returned a value outside `S` where the instance compiled once raised: one call, two answers by the attribute. The pattern: a check that the compiler attaches to the type a value is known by is lost where an instance knows more. A trait's members are checked at their membership, so their calls need none; `Callable`'s members are decided by their methods and are never checked. The tool: gate `n8_callable`, each case under `compiled_for`, `with_dynamic` and with the function a constant. A control: the call compiled for the function costs what a plain call costs, 0.79 against 0.80 ns (E-371).
- **A `Callable` absence edge is as narrow as the question** ([compiler §7.4](reference/compiler.md#74-callables-call); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; compiler C-88 (b) closed; E-372): mine (2026-11-14). None for a method the call's other arguments cannot reach; at `A` with the method's variables' values where they can. The pattern: an edge wider than the question fails G2, the image drops the instance, every session compiles it again, and nothing reports it. The tool: `experiments/unification/w120/` (`classify_trace.py`, `image_has.jl`, `g2_why.jl`).
- **The cache across sessions: a proposal that recommends stopping it** ([the proposal](plans/2026-11-14-cache-across-sessions.md); W-120; E-372): for the user. The trade-off it leaves: the session's own code is compiled in every session, or a build step caches it for unchanged scripts.
- **A cast in a call with keyword arguments is lowered through `Core.kwcall`** ([compiler §6.9](reference/compiler.md#69-casts-and-keep); lowering L-56): mine (2026-11-14).
- **`like` and `withfields` name the constructor to define, in an `ArgumentError`** ([lowering §8](reference/lowering.md#8-like-and-withfields); L-57 retired): mine (2026-11-14), as `OperationError`: a `MethodError` thrown by hand prints Julia's sentence for one.
- **`detect_kept_storage(mod)`, and the warning at an extension's method under strict mode** ([traits §7.8](reference/traits.md#78-keeping-an-argument-with-its-attribute), [§12.3](reference/traits.md#123-strict-mode); T-111, T-97 retired): mine (2026-11-14).
- **The triage revised** (2026-11-14, mine; [the schedule](plans/2026-11-11-complete-prototype.md), addendum): out of bin A, with the reason. To B: compiler C-55 (a standard library's type under a trait's default: it waits for [item 19](#1-decisions-waiting-for-the-user), whether the standard libraries are Julia's own, and Julia has no cheap test for one at a load); traits T-105 (an input left open: a missing refusal, the call's result is abstract and no answer is wrong); lowering L-58 (a hint's wording). To C: compiler C-56 (`keep` in code that is not compiled: no program reaches it but under `--compile=min`); traits T-99 (the hints are tested on the library's cases; SciML's cases are a measurement).
- **Kept values, the mechanism** (2026-11-12; W-149; [traits §7.8](reference/traits.md#78-keeping-an-argument-with-its-attribute), [§8.1](reference/traits.md#81-fields-and-containers-typed-by-a-trait)): the user decided that the storage's type decides what is stored, with `Kept{X}`. I had written that assignment into storage typed by the trait unwraps a `Dynamic{X}`. It cannot: Julia stores a member as it is, with no `convert` (`experiments/unification/w149/convert_probe.jl`: the box stays; one such method of `convert` logs 7,005 invalidation entries at its definition). So: a trait argument is assigned there without `keep`, which gives the object whoever calls (`keep_or_not.jl`); `Kept{X}` is storage and no member; a call under the kept attribute is `kept_call`. Nothing yet reports a `keep` into storage typed by the trait.
- **The triage of 2026-11-11** ([the schedule](plans/2026-11-11-complete-prototype.md); W-144): which open question and deviation is fixed before the measurement round (bin A), which goes to the synthesis (B), which is accepted for the prototype (C). Of bin A's four decisions, three are made (2026-11-11: the `where` variable, kept values, the abstract result); W-141 is decided (2026-11-12): a membership behaves as a method, its method is the edges' implementation.
- **A call records no cell; a membership replays the trait's methods at its member and invalidates the callers whose selection changes** ([compiler §5.5](reference/compiler.md#55-a-membership-invalidates-as-a-method-does); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §4; E-365): mine (2026-11-11). The proposal had the call record the pair; the walk reaches the code of an image compiled where no method at the trait existed, which a recorded pair cannot. A membership adds no method, so the walk leaves valid a call that matched the trait's method before, and a method at `Vector{X}`: composition then ends no instance (two orders of E-358).
- **A non-membership is relied on wherever the code is compiled,** while a declarer loads and for a type or trait of `Main`, for a concrete type and for an abstract type whose intersection with the trait no type is in ([compiler §4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries)): mine (2026-11-11), choice (a) of [the proposal](plans/2026-11-11-membership-piracy.md).
- **What counts as a use, for a closed build's members by use** ([compiler §7.6](reference/compiler.md#76-a-closed-build); E-364): a type forgotten at a place typed under the trait (a field, an element, an argument, a result, a join, an assertion, a constant's contents), or at a wider place that a test narrows to the trait: mine (2026-11-11). Every concrete type the build computes was tried first and made every `Vector` of the program a `Column`.
- **An image keeps the member checks that passed while it was built, as a list beside its memberships** ([lowering §11](reference/lowering.md#11-checks-errors-and-what-images-keep); L-55): mine (2026-11-11). A failure is not kept.
- **In a closed build a cast to `Compiled` on a value known by its trait branches over the trait's members; the cast of a value typed by a trait is typed by it** ([compiler §7.6](reference/compiler.md#76-a-closed-build), [§6.9](reference/compiler.md#69-casts-and-keep); E-364): mine (2026-11-11).
- **A call that does not match a method at `Callable{A, S}` holds an edge to the argument's dispatch at `A`** ([compiler §7.4](reference/compiler.md#74-callables-call); gate `n26_callable_late_method`): mine (2026-11-11). Where `A` names a method variable the edge is to every call of the argument (compiler C-88).
- **A function written at the prompt is built from the definitions it depends on, selected by name from the prompt's history** ([traits §16](reference/traits.md#16-compiling-ahead-closed-programs); E-364; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §6): mine (2026-11-11). Every method of a function reached is replayed, not only those called; a global the function reads must be a `const`.
- **`make_executable` and `make_library`: their form** ([traits §16](reference/traits.md#16-compiling-ahead-closed-programs); E-364; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §6): mine (2026-11-11). The build's process loads the function's file or package.
- **In a closed build the split holds the box `Dynamic{X}`, splits over at most 64 members, and code inferred before every member was declared is inferred again** ([compiler §7.6](reference/compiler.md#76-a-closed-build); E-364; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §6): mine (2026-11-11).
- **The form of a message: a signature as a call, a refused method headed as such, a field named, a declaration's code at the declaration** ([traits §11.3](reference/traits.md#113-errors-and-their-messages); E-362; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §2): mine (2026-11-11). `ImplementError` has a field `method`. With it, "outside the declared result" for a result that is no supertype of it, a bare option name at `@trait_module`, and no hint at an operation that is another owner's function ([§11.4](reference/traits.md#114-hints); E-363).
- **A trimmed image marks every module as never compiled by facts; a trimmed build has no code slots** ([compiler §2.6](reference/compiler.md#26-ahead-of-time-builds), [§3.3](reference/compiler.md#33-code-slots); C-53 retired; E-361; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §6): mine (2026-11-10).
- **`verify_main` lists a cast's members and instances, reports `entrypoints` and `complete`; `add_entrypoints(report)` gives them to a build** ([traits §16](reference/traits.md#16-compiling-ahead-closed-programs); T-108; E-361; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §6): mine (2026-11-10).
- **A declared result is asserted, never converted; a closed type's contract promises no result** ([traits §3.4](reference/traits.md#34-open-and-closed), [§7.9](reference/traits.md#79-operations-on-a-value-the-code-knows-only-by-its-trait); [compiler §7.1](reference/compiler.md#71-the-rule); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; open issue 171, closed; E-340): mine (2026-11-10), after [the plan](plans/2026-11-09-171-declared-results-without-conversion.md) and [its review](reviews/2026-11-09-plan-review-171.md). A call through `IO` returns the stream's own result; a call through a trait raises a `TypeError` where a member escaped its check.
- **A contract is declared in the image that defines the type, its candidates fixed with it** ([compiler §7.1](reference/compiler.md#71-the-rule), [§7.2](reference/compiler.md#72-the-split); C-81 retired; E-340): mine (2026-11-10), after [the review](reviews/2026-11-09-plan-review-c57-c81.md). REPL's `LimitIO` can never be `IO`'s candidate (open issue 170).

- **Strict mode refuses a membership that makes methods ambiguous; at an image's activation the ambiguity is reported, not refused** ([traits §6.3](reference/traits.md#63-ambiguity), [§12.3](reference/traits.md#123-strict-mode); [compiler §4.3](reference/compiler.md#43-worlds); E-343): mine (2026-11-10), after [the plan](plans/2026-11-10-t98-strict-membership-ambiguity.md) and [its review](reviews/2026-11-10-plan-review-t98.md). Refusing at activation would invalidate the image's code for a condition its precompile could not see; strict mode reports a pirate loaded earlier the same way. An ambiguity among an image's own memberships and methods is refused at its precompile where strict mode is on (`JULIA_TW_STRICT=1` reaches the precompile process). The error-level report after the load is built (E-349, below).
- **Strict mode refuses the membership that creates an ambiguity, not a later one** ([compiler §5.4](reference/compiler.md#54-the-ambiguity-report-at-a-membership); [traits §6.3](reference/traits.md#63-ambiguity); E-346): mine (2026-11-10), after [the C-78 plan's review](reviews/2026-11-10-plan-review-c78.md).
- **The ambiguity report asks each method a membership makes applicable against the method table at the member** ([compiler §5.4](reference/compiler.md#54-the-ambiguity-report-at-a-membership); [traits §6.3](reference/traits.md#63-ambiguity); W-132, C-78 retired; E-347): mine (2026-11-10), after [the plan](plans/2026-11-10-c78-report-at-declared-supertypes.md) and [its review](reviews/2026-11-10-plan-review-c78.md). Mine in it: a match counts only where it covers the member itself, not through a set the member is not in; one warning per method, with every tied method and their joint signature; a tie that arises only at a type with the member as its parameter (`Vector{S}`) is left to the call. Decided under the user's instruction of 2026-11-10 ("Make decisions where there is a principled option."): a tie with a method at `Any` is reported and refused like any other, at the membership ([alternatives](design/alternatives.md)). `Test.detect_ambiguities` over the trait modules finds no such tie (open issue 174).
- **A member by its definition declares its entry, and its parameters come from the definition** ([traits §5.2](reference/traits.md#52-with-the-types-definition), [§6.3](reference/traits.md#63-ambiguity); [lowering §3](reference/lowering.md#3-implement); W-131; E-345): mine (2026-11-10), after [the plan](plans/2026-11-10-w131-declared-subtype-entry.md) and [its review](reviews/2026-11-10-plan-review-w131.md). The order of a member's `@implement`s does not decide which membership strict mode refuses (E-347).
- **Activation ends an older entry in force for the same member, set and inputs** ([compiler §4.6](reference/compiler.md#46-one-instance-per-member), [§9](reference/compiler.md#9-images-and-the-machine-code-cache); C-84 retired; E-348): mine (2026-11-10). Two images that declare one membership leave one entry in force, the later image's, as two declarations in a session do.
- **Under strict mode an ambiguity at an image's activation is reported at error level after the load and kept** ([traits §12.3](reference/traits.md#123-strict-mode); [compiler §10.2](reference/compiler.md#102-hooks-for-strict-mode); T-98 retired; E-349): mine (2026-11-10). The runtime hands the findings to the library in place of its warning; the library reports them as it reports a pirate loaded earlier.
- **A member by its definition used before its `@implement` raises `ImplementError`** ([traits §5.2](reference/traits.md#52-with-the-types-definition); [lowering §3](reference/lowering.md#3-implement); [compiler §10.4](reference/compiler.md#104-the-member-hook); E-350): mine (2026-11-10). Built at a use that dispatch resolves at run time and at a use compiled with the type known (the next item).
- **The member hook in the compile queue: a failed use is left to dispatch, and compiled again when the check passes** ([compiler §10.4](reference/compiler.md#104-the-member-hook), C-31 narrowed; lowering L-52 retired; E-352): mine (2026-11-10), under the user's "Implement 1. 2. and 3. But no plan and review required." Mine in it: inference runs no check, it notes the member and the queue asks the hook before the code runs; the runtime keeps the answer per member and set; a use whose check failed stays a dynamic call, so the error is raised at that use and a branch not taken raises nothing; callers compiled while a check failed are compiled again once it passes. Left: a call by `invoke` at the set's method asks no hook, and with threads another thread may run the code before the queue's check ends (C-31).
- **Strict mode infers an image's memberships at its load and reports, never refuses** ([traits §12.3](reference/traits.md#123-strict-mode); W-133 done; E-353): mine (2026-11-10), under the same words. The report is at error level, as a pirate loaded earlier is. To confirm with it: a method defined after the load that changes a checked call's result only through a callee is accepted and not reported; the call through the trait asserts the declared result ([traits §5.10](reference/traits.md#510-checks) refuses only a method that would serve the checked call). Its cost is flagged above.
- **Two traits' operations of one function: a joint method where one answer is right for every common member, else the pair is named in the package's tests** ([traits §6.3](reference/traits.md#63-ambiguity); open issue 174, closed; E-354): mine (2026-11-10), under the same words. BaseTraits' `getindex` of `Indexable` and `Keyed` stays a named pair: each common member defines its own, as `Dict` does.
- **A membership loaded from an image keeps its check, from records made again at load** ([traits §5.10](reference/traits.md#510-checks); [lowering §11](reference/lowering.md#11-checks-errors-and-what-images-keep); W-130; E-344): mine (2026-11-10), after [the plan](plans/2026-11-10-w130-records-at-load.md) and [its review](reviews/2026-11-10-plan-review-w130.md). The records are the build's check, not the session's state: a method already in the session when the image loads is not judged. Decided under the user's instruction of 2026-11-10 ("Make decisions where there is a principled option."): strict mode infers each checked call of an image's membership at its load and reports a method that breaks it, as it reports a pirate loaded earlier ([traits §12.3](reference/traits.md#123-strict-mode); Planned, W-133).
- **A member's check holds while it is a member** ([traits §5.10](reference/traits.md#510-checks), [§5.11](reference/traits.md#511-overrides-and-redeclaration); open issue 168; E-342): mine (2026-11-10), after [the plan](plans/2026-11-10-recheck-at-redefinition.md) and [its review](reviews/2026-11-10-plan-review-recheck.md). A method defined after the check that would serve a checked call with a result outside the declared one, make the operation ambiguous at the member, or take a call a default or the forwarding serves is refused before it exists, an `ImplementError`; an image's such method is left inactive with a warning. The rule is the trait's, not strict mode's: a pirate in another module is refused too. Covered: memberships declared in the session, members checked at their first use in it, and memberships loaded from an image (E-344), members by their definition included (W-131, E-345).

Choices marked mine since the references' adoption (2026-11-06), input to the synthesis as the ones above:
- **What strict mode's check before a pirate's membership misses is reported, not undone,** until a membership can be taken back; at an image's load a family's membership is checked for the folded answers only (traits T-112; open issue 180 (c); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §4).
- **A trait package's front end belongs to a trait module** (`intro/porting.md` §0.5; E-357; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §8).
- **The library on the unified build is TraitWorld on an orphan branch `unified`** ([decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **The trait's method types only its receiver** (and arguments that bind parameters), so a member's own method wins (lowering §2; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **E-269's failed gate answered in the runtime** (disjointness by reachability, the typemap's world range) rather than BaseTraits' operations off Base's functions, and the exact-type call-cache entries dropped (compiler §4.7, §8.2; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **`All{X, Y}` encoded as the abstract type `Core.All{Tuple{X, Y}}`,** with branches at every site that reads a type's name, supertype or parameters (compiler §4.9; reviewed by Fable, [the review](reviews/2026-11-07-all-representation.md); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **An open set meets an abstract type as `All{X, T}`** (compiler §4.7, §5.3; C-33): a query at an abstract type sees the methods at sets, so inference at such a query is less precise where no closed method covers it ([decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **The library exports `All`; `Core` does not** (open issue 148's recommendation (b); [traits §3.6](reference/traits.md#36-intersections); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **Method matching asks a recorded interference between two methods at open sets again, in the query's world,** so a later set-in-set membership orders them (compiler §5.2; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **An image's memberships run the stopgap's walk and the ambiguity report at load,** as a declaration does (compiler §9; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **BaseTraits' contracts** after the prior art: `Iterable` repeatable, `Indexable` with `firstindex`, `MutableIndexable` with `BitVector` and views, `Resizable{T}` by `push!` and shrinking `resize!`, `Keyed`'s `get` with any default, sets and strings members ([traits §10.1](reference/traits.md#101-basetraits); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **`Solvable` by LinearAlgebra's `ldiv!`; `LinearOp` by BaseTraits' `apply!`** (E-281); **`BoundedText` a constant per type, with `textbound(T)`** (open issue 109, closed by it).
- **The trait's method types a further argument only where it binds an input**, and **the check refuses a serving method that takes any receiver** (lowering §2, traits §5.10; open issue 150).
- **Method matching asks a pair with a method at a set in the query's world** (compiler §5.2).
- **Fully typed mode in the compiler, explained by the library** (compiler §2.7; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9), and `AbstractTypeError` for an abstract result (traits §14).
- **Compiling ahead asks for the instance a static call at the member runs** (lowering §12; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **W-101 (the code slot) after W-113 and W-114:** the language first, the slot's optimization last ([decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **The code slot as built** (compiler §3.3; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; [the plan](plans/2026-11-07-w101-the-code-slot.md)): a slot per call site checked against the task's world; the result edges on the CodeInstance's analysis results; a mark as a lowered `max_world` with a flag, made valid again where code is looked up in the latest world.
- **No method of Base carries the boundary flag** in the product setting, by E-279: the plain code facts code rests on satisfies G2 itself (compiler §2.2; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **W-100 developed on the stack's top** (branch `w100`), its commits confined to the facts mode for a later rebase onto vanilla ([decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **The query world is the task's,** restored by exception handlers as the world age is (compiler §4.11; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; the audit's A4 proposed the subtyping environment).
- **Which of `trait-world-1.13`'s changes the stack on branch `unified` carries now** ([julia-changes §7.1](design/julia-changes.md#71-the-stack); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §8): the eleven useful without traits, one commit each; `known_type` and the registry of type printers wait for a stage that uses them; the per-call-site cache's exact-type entries wait for W-109 (they slowed sites that see many types, [PE-20](record/performance-explorations.md)); the dispatch profiler is carried without its split by owner, the reflection fix and the two inliner changes without their facts halves.


- **C-32 as built** (compiler §4.6, traits §4.3; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9): a set's inputs a byte on its `TypeName`; a field named by an input declares a property, so `HasProperty{N, R}` is `@const N::R` with `@input N`.
- **A slot's miss with no code to make gets a dispatching entry** (compiler §3.3; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; the review's H1).
- **A family's variables are in scope in its block's methods** (traits §5.8; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **The profiling campaign's plan, and slice 3's sessions inside it** ([the plan](plans/2026-11-08-w121-profiling-campaign.md); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **Open issue 159: `Dynamic{Callable}` without a signature** (proposal (a), mine), so that a record holding a function before its signature is known (SciML's `ODEFunction`, callbacks) keeps the caller's choice of attribute. The port takes (c) until then.
- **The cast names are bound, not reserved** ([traits §7.6](reference/traits.md#76-casts), [compiler §6.9](reference/compiler.md#69-casts-and-keep); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; open issue 160, closed): mine (2026-11-12). A call's argument `compiled_for(x)` is a cast where the name is bound to the cast; a plain package's own function of the name is called as any other.
- **Open issue 158: a required operation's method is no shadow, with forwarding as methods at the member** (proposal (a), mine): the `Nothing` arm of a call to one of BaseTraits' operations prunes as in plain Julia (found by CSV's suite, 2026-11-08). Compiler §5.6 and traits §5.5 changed (W-128): the runtime's half on `unified` 07c0bee2a2 and 40401f67d8 (a `serves_no_member` mark in the body, read before the insertion), the library's in TraitWorld; E-297 measures it. `Dynamic{X}` keeps being served by the trait's method rather than by methods of its own: it joins at the trait's declaration, so the method still serves no type that joins later.
- **`keep` of a `Dynamic` holds its object anew** (compiler §6.9; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9): never a `Dynamic` of a `Dynamic`, as the constructor does (found by TDE's suite, 2026-11-08).
- **Open issue 161: a type is its own package's only with its parameters** (proposal (a), mine): `@default_dynamic` applies to a library's own type that carries a user's type, such as NetworkDynamics' `FunctionDynamics{typeof(f)}` (traits §7.5, compiler §6.3; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; E-310).
- **Lowering's record of `f(…)::RT` covers a type at the definition only** (C-36, mine): constants, a module's constant bindings, literals and the method's static parameters; an `RT` computed from the arguments records none ([compiler §6.8](reference/compiler.md#68-a-declared-result-on-method); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
- **Open issue 162: a parameter that passes a trait's value on is typed by its trait, and a package's tests check that `detect_unused_casts` is empty** (proposals (b) and (c), mine; [traits §7.6](reference/traits.md#76-casts); NetworkDynamics' port does (b)).
- **An image holds the code a facts run-time site reaches whatever the values, for all modules** (compiler §9; E-326; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9): TraitWorld's precompile grew from 4.8 to 8.4 s, the ports' did not; the registered 10 % clause read as aimed at growth in the ports.
- **An operation declared `::Self` declares the set at the receiver's parameters** (compiler §6.8; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9), so the definition-time check classifies the code after such a call.
- **Traits closed after load: opted in per trait, by `@closed_after_load` in the trait's body** ([traits §3.4](reference/traits.md#34-open-and-closed); [compiler §4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9): the user decided that finality is opt-in (2026-11-09); the granularity, the spelling, the declarers with their joint owners, and the final negative as an edge on the pair's cell are mine.
- **`IO`'s contract** ([traits §9b](reference/traits.md#9b-streams-io); [compiler §7.1](reference/compiler.md#71-the-rule), [§7.5](reference/compiler.md#75-io); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9): `write(io, ::UInt8)` and `unsafe_write` `::Int` (a stream's other integer type converted at the call, E-333), `read(io, ::Type{UInt8})` `::UInt8`, `haskey` `::Bool`, the rest undeclared; `eof`, `isopen` and `close`, which have no method at `::IO`, are not operations; the candidates `IOBuffer`, `IOStream`, `TTY`, `PipeEndpoint`, registered by Base first; the contract written on the type's `TypeName` contract slot, as its operations' methods (`jl_declare_contract(T, ops)`).
- **Sets in Base's build: Core and Base loaded at the end of Base's build, the system image's indices of sets, a split's branch `Compiled`, ambiguity reflection in the query's world** ([compiler §4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries), [§5.4](reference/compiler.md#54-the-ambiguity-report-at-a-membership), [§7.2](reference/compiler.md#72-the-split), [§7.5](reference/compiler.md#75-io), [§9](reference/compiler.md#9-images-and-the-machine-code-cache); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; E-330): no `__init__` runs while an image is generated, so Base's own types count as loaded once Base's last definition has run; a branch of the split runs the serving method compiled for its candidate, since a default at `::IO` compiled once made the branch's case cover every stream (`get(io, :compact, false)` folded to `false`); `detect_ambiguities` reports a pair at a set and a type only once a membership makes them meet (with `IO` a set, `write(::AbstractString, …)` against `write(::IO, …)` made 66 in Base, E-330).
- **Open issue 165: a trait argument passed on to plain code, or captured by a closure** (recommendation (a) with (b), and (d), mine; not built): the classification follows `keep`, so `sum(keep(t))` is open; for `Callable`, `map(keep(f), xs)` is in the contract; a closure captures a trait argument as `keep` stores it, so a generator over it is fully bounded where its body is (found by the docs examples, 2026-11-09).
- **Dispatch's lookup keeps a match met only at an intersection no type is in at an open set, and drops it at a final pair with its cells; a `Callable` component and a `Vararg` tail are met** ([compiler §5.4](reference/compiler.md#54-the-ambiguity-report-at-a-membership); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; [the performance review](reviews/2026-11-09-performance-day.md) H2; [the plan](plans/2026-11-09-c82-unmet-match-lookup.md) and [its review](reviews/2026-11-09-plan-review-c82.md)): mine; built on `unified` 999777ab77 (C-82 retired, E-341). C-78: the report does not yet name a member's declared supertype.
- **A call through a trait asserts the declared result, never converts it** ([traits §7.9](reference/traits.md#79-operations-on-a-value-the-code-knows-only-by-its-trait); [compiler §7.2](reference/compiler.md#72-the-split); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; open issue 171; E-340): mine (2026-11-10), replacing the conversion of E-333; with `handle1`'s join of a const result taken for its effects.
- **An intersection no type is in, at a set closed after load, is a final negative with a cell** ([compiler §4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries), [§5.4](reference/compiler.md#54-the-ambiguity-report-at-a-membership); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; E-334; open issue 169 (a)): `t isa AbstractString` folds to `false` for `t::X` at a set `X` closed after load, and a match met only at `All{AbstractString, X}` is dropped even where it would win, with the cell (`AbstractString`, `X`) as an edge; a membership of a type under `AbstractString` in `X` opens it and invalidates. Code compiled at such an intersection while Base is built is invalidated once Core and Base count as loaded.
- **A method whose signature mentions a set is listed unindexed only at the set's position** ([compiler §5.1](reference/compiler.md#51-the-method-table); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; E-335): elsewhere it is indexed as any other, so a lookup of another function does not walk it.
- **A dispatch edge keeps the call's signature where the call reached an instance wider than its own match** ([compiler §3.1](reference/compiler.md#31-the-record); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; E-336): a method defined later invalidates that caller only where it meets the call's signature; for methods compiled once at a set and `@nospecialize` methods alike.

Waiting for evidence, not for the user:

19. **Should standard libraries be separate owners?** Today Julia (Core, Base, the standard libraries) is one owner ([facts §1](reference/facts.md#term-owner); `TraitWorld/src/ownership.jl` `owner(m)` returns `:Julia` for every standard library, `strict.jl` skips `:Julia`), so a standard library's method on Base types (SparseArrays' `vcat` for `Number` and `Vector`, loaded by StatsBase) is a stakeholder's change: followed, never refused. TraitWorld's `_type_root` already treats a standard library's types as its own. Found by the writeup review of 2026-10-19 (finding 1). **To settle by E-189** (W-65; with strict mode's intended design, W-66): a census of the standard-library methods that are pirates by the letter if each library is its own owner, and which of them win at a fact of trait code in the demonstrators and the port's core. Outside strict mode the choice changes nothing, since a winning pirate is followed by default. Only late-loaded libraries win (SparseArrays' `vcat`): each library its own owner. A library in the system image wins (LinearAlgebra's methods of Base's functions): Julia stays one owner.
65. **Who owns a method defined by `@eval Owner …`** (facts review M1): ownership is the module a method is evaluated in, so a package that evaluates a method into Base defines it as Julia's own, and strict mode does not see it as a pirate; [traits §10.3](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#103-strict-mode) recommends `@eval Owner` to define as the owner. **Recommendation:** a method belongs to the package whose code defines it (an image's methods to the image's package); sharing ownership is declared, with `@joint_owners` and `delegate!`. **To settle by E-189** (W-65; with item 19): the methods in package images whose module is neither the image's package nor a joint owner of it. Few, all between split packages: a method in an image belongs to the image's package, and the module rule at run time is a named exception. Common and legitimate: the module rule stays, named in facts §2.2's exceptions. Until decided, the references state the rule as it is.


## 2. Design

**From the decision on keys (2026-11-06):**

147. **Keys for traits with operations only** (W-110; not urgent). The user (2026-11-06): "A usecase for keys: Operation only traits. That just guarantee functions of a certain signature exist. Keep them optional and build them late. Needs more investigation but is probably not urgent." Such a trait guarantees that functions of given signatures exist and declares no fields. Under [the draft's key](reference/traits.md#72-the-key), a member that serves an operation by its own method is its own receiver, so each member is its own key and keys share nothing; only members served wholly by the trait's defaults share one. **Deferred (2026-11-11):** the user (2026-11-11): "Keys should be considered purely a performance optimization. They can stay deferred." ([PE-33](record/performance-explorations.md)).
   - **To investigate:** a key that names what the operations resolve to rather than the member's type, so that members whose operations select the same methods (a generic method over a common supertype, the defaults) share a fully bounded method's code. Which guarantee that gives, what invalidates it, and how it relates to `Dynamic{X}` (compiled once, a dispatch per operation).
   - Before W-110 is built.
148. **The name `All` collides with DataAPI's `All`** (W-107; found 2026-11-07). The user's spelling for intersections is `All{X, Y, Z, …}` (Q5 (a); [traits §3.6](reference/traits.md#36-intersections)). DataAPI exports a type `All` (a column selector, `select(df, All())`), and DataFrames re-exports it; the DataFrames port uses it. If `Core` exported `All`, every module that uses DataFrames would see two `All`s and have to qualify one. The build keeps `Core.All` unexported for now (W-107's probe); trait code would reach it through the library.
   - **Options:** (a) `Core` exports `All`, and DataFrames code qualifies `DataAPI.All`; (b) the library exports `All` (= `Core.All`), so only modules that use both qualify; (c) another name, such as `Both`, `AllOf` or `Meet`.
   - **Recommendation:** (b): trait code gets the user's spelling, plain Julia keeps its names, and the clash is local to modules that use both.
   - **Taken for now:** (b), mine (2026-11-07): TraitWorld `unified` ae4d250 exports `All` ([traits §3.6](reference/traits.md#36-intersections)); input to the synthesis (§1).

**Parameters and types**

8. Numeric traits: numbers that join a trait after their definition or forward to a field, and promotion across members' types. Definition checking of code that computes with an element type needs a numeric trait for it (solvers.md §11.6). *Revisit once experience with large-scale ports is at hand (W-65; §6): the SciML port shows which numeric traits definition checking needs.*
9. Units (solvers.md §11.11). Automatic-differentiation tags are decided: labelled by the primal signature ([decisions](record/decisions.md)). *Revisit once experience with large-scale ports is at hand (W-65; §6): the ports show whether units need more than a parameter.*
10. `Callable` bounds whose argument types are chosen by the callee, a type-level function of the algorithm's configuration (solvers.md §11.9). *Revisit once experience with large-scale ports is at hand (W-65; §6): the SciML port's algorithm packages (W-34, X4) show whether partial evaluation (W-63) leaves any.*

**Bounds, dispatch, arguments**

12. Bounds over several arguments at once, `lines(x, y)`, where today a conversion builds one value from both (the Makie agent of 2026-10-28 proposes no design change: the argument tuple conforms as a family over `Tuple`, [its §3](plans/2026-10-28-fresh-ideas-makie.md)); this ties to promotion (makie-rewrite.md §8.1). *Revisit once experience with large-scale ports is at hand (W-65; §6): the Makie port.*

**From [the annotation audit](reviews/2026-10-28-ports-annotation-workarounds.md) (2026-10-28):** port code that makes up with an annotation or an escape hatch for something the design should provide. Each item: the case, the rule that blocks it, the audit's proposal. What to decide and build for each: [the plan](plans/2026-10-28-annotation-audit-decisions.md) (proposal; 101 decided by the user, closed items §2).

99. **Run-time sites where plain Julia would infer a union of results** (candidate 2). The CSV fork's reader is compiled by facts (a trait module); every call at abstract types is typed `Any` where plain Julia would infer the union of the methods' results. The port removed upstream's `@nospecializeinfer` from seven functions, wrapped a type in `Val` and asserted seven results. Blocked by [facts §5](reference/facts.md#5-run-time-sites) and the policy rule ([facts §6.2](reference/facts.md#62-the-policy-rule)). *Proposals:* (a) a porting rule: enter trait code at the kernels and keep plain front ends plain (the lean-core memo §3.1 says so for CSV); (b) in the compiler, split a run-time site on a result type that all matching methods declare or infer, as union splitting does.
112. **The old API's untyped node graph undoes the late-load protection** (W-70, E-226). It concerns the old recipe API's recipe children only: the workload's path makes the core's primitives directly and recompiles nothing after a late load (E-227). A child recipe's arguments are nodes of its parent's graph, which resolve to `Any` in plain code, as upstream's ComputePipeline does. The child's creation then compiles abstract instances (among them `hist.jl`'s `Histogram` conversion), which DataFrames' `AbstractVector` subtypes invalidate at a late load: 41 CodeInstances, 126 ms. No rule blocks a fix. *Proposal:* typed derived values in the `PlotCore` slice of the workload's path ([the fresh-ideas memo](plans/2026-10-28-fresh-ideas-makie.md): inputs kept, derived values declared), not a patch to the compatibility layer. It waits for unification (W-83), by the plan's rule.
115. **A member's check does not cover inference deeper inside an operation's body** (W-84, E-231; re-read 2026-11-14). A member's check infers each operation's result at the member, and the library's records name the methods dispatch selects there ([lowering §3](reference/lowering.md#3-implement), step 7). A method defined later *inside* an operation's body (not the operation's own method) that widens the result is not refused at its definition, and an image keeps the check that passed ([lowering §11](reference/lowering.md#11-checks-errors-and-what-images-keep)). The answer stays Julia's or an error: the call through the trait asserts the declared result and raises a `TypeError` ([traits §7.9](reference/traits.md#79-operations-on-a-value-the-code-knows-only-by-its-trait)). *Proposal,* for the synthesis: the check's inferred code kept with its edges, so that Julia's validation ends the check when a method below it changes.
116. **A member first used while Julia writes an image is checked again at load** (W-84; re-read 2026-11-14): code compiled at output runs after the build's `atexit` hooks, so the list of checks an image keeps ([lowering §11](reference/lowering.md#11-checks-errors-and-what-images-keep)) misses a member whose first use is there; 1 of the forks' 27 members (`Vector{Union{Missing,String15}}`). A cost at load, no wrong answer. *Proposal:* a Julia hook after code is compiled at output, or the member used in the package's workload before output.
122. **Two types for a value that changes: `Observable` and the core's `Live`** (W-32, E-240). `Observable{T}` cannot conform to `Positions` or `Sample` by forwarding without forwarding for a whole family (traits T-7, Planned) and an Observables dependency in the core, so MakieCompat mirrors an `Observable` into a `Live`. *Proposal:* build T-7, then let `Observable{T}` conform directly (in an extension) and drop the mirror.
129. **`DiffEqArray` over any state type** (W-32, E-242). TraitArrayTools' `DiffEqArray` holds vector states only, so `sol(ts; idxs)` returns a plain `Vector` and upstream's `sol(ts; idxs).u` does not run. *Proposal:* `DiffEqArray` over any state type; it changes the type's parameter, so it is decided before it is built.
134. **A constant per implementation** (W-32, E-247; P4-5 of the meta plan). NetworkDynamics' `ND.pdim(::Swing) = 3` and a record's `psym` are two sources of one fact, checked equal when the record is built. *Proposal:* an associated constant in the trait (a value declared per implementation, like an associated type), so the record reads it.
125. **No facts copies of Base in the product setting** (W-92, E-238). Trait-module code that reaches Base compiles Base by facts in the session, since the product system image keeps plain code only: DataFrames' public layer as a trait module costs 0.24–0.34 s more than plain (display at the REPL's I/O type, `describe`, `ByRow` paths, about 340 Base methods), where under `all` the same reach gains 0.31–0.34 s. *Proposal:* a plain CodeInstance inferred fully typed (every edge a fact) also serves the facts owner; or the product system image keeps facts copies of Base. Relevant to W-83 (what unification costs plain code that becomes trait code).

**From adopting BaseTraits in the ports (2026-10-28):**

110. **Two traits for reading items by position: `Column` and `Indexable`.** The DataFrames port's `CellVector` is `asvector` for a `Column`, written again: `Column`'s `cell(c, i)` reads row `i` counted from 1, `Indexable`'s `c[i]` is Base's index, which for an `AbstractVector` may start elsewhere (OffsetArrays). So a column is not `Indexable` by its own operations, and `asvector` cannot serve the kernels. Blocked by [traits §8.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#81-basetraits-traits-for-bases-concepts) (the index's meaning is Base's) and T-8 (a generic implementation of a trait with parameters, which `@implement Indexable C where {C: Column}` would be). *Proposal:* `Indexable`'s index means a position counted from 1, Base's types conforming through `firstindex`, as `Column`'s family does; then `Column{T} <: Indexable{T}` with `cell` its `getindex`, and `CellVector` is `asvector`. Until then `CellVector` stays.
   - **Now answerable without a design change** (2026-11-07, [the review](reviews/2026-11-07-basetraits-choices.md) M8): `Indexable` has `firstindex` ([traits §10.1](reference/traits.md#101-basetraits)), so the port's `Column` joins by a block, `@implement Indexable{T} Column{T} where {T} begin getindex(c, i) = cell(c, i); firstindex(c) = 1 end` (§5.9), and `CellVector` becomes `asvector`. To take when the DataFrames port is rebuilt (W-104).

**From the code reviews of the references (2026-10-19):**


**From BaseTraits (W-114, 2026-11-07)**, its prior art ([the research](record/collection-and-linalg-traits-research-2026-11-07.md)) and [the review](reviews/2026-11-07-basetraits-choices.md):

150. **A trait's method hides a member's own method that takes any receiver** (found 2026-11-07). An operation that is an existing function, served at a member by the function's untyped fallback (LinearAlgebra's `mul!(C, A, B)`, Base's `show(io::IO, x)`): the trait's method is more specific at the receiver (a set beats `Any`, [compiler §5.2](reference/compiler.md#52-specificity)), so once the type is a member every call goes to the trait's method, which finds no forwarding and no default. With `mul!` as an operation, loading the library broke matrix–vector `mul!`, and its method invalidated 14 instances at each load (E-281).
   - **Options:** (a) the trait's method passes such a call on, by `invoke` with the receiver's position at `Any` (the error path's `MethodError` then names the `invoke`, and the invalidation stays); (b) the runtime lets a member's method untyped at the receiver win over the trait's method for members; (c) refuse such a member at its check and give such operations a function of the trait's own (BaseTraits' `apply!`).
   - **Taken for now:** (c), mine ([traits §5.10](reference/traits.md#510-checks); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9).
151. **Linear algebra's further operations:** the adjoint and transposed solves (faer's `SolveCore`, ndarray-linalg's `Solve`), several right-hand sides, the five-argument `mul!(y, A, x, α, β)` and `*` as a default ([the research](record/collection-and-linalg-traits-research-2026-11-07.md) §3.2, §3.4). SciML's adjoint sensitivities and Krylov paths need them. *Proposal:* `AdjointSolvable{T} <: Solvable{T}` by `ldiv!` at `adjoint(F)`, and `LinearOp`'s defaults, once a port asks.
