152. **Mixed precision:** `T = eltype(Self)` ties a solver's or operator's vectors to its element type, so an `LU{Float32}` cannot solve for `Float64` vectors, as iterative refinement does ([the research](record/collection-and-linalg-traits-research-2026-11-07.md) §3.6; Mojo's kernels take one element type per operand). *Proposal:* the vectors' element type a `where` variable of the operation.
153. **Defaults defined in terms of each other:** a trait with two defaults that call each other passes a member that serves neither, which then recurses (ndarray-linalg's `LinearOperator`, [the research](record/collection-and-linalg-traits-research-2026-11-07.md) §3.2). *Proposal:* a minimal complete definition declared in the trait and checked at each membership.
154. **Keys of another type:** `haskey(d::Keyed{String}, k::SubString)` is outside the contract, though Base's dictionaries take an equal key of another type (Rust's `Borrow<Q>`, [the research](record/collection-and-linalg-traits-research-2026-11-07.md) §1.2).
155. **Single-pass iteration:** generators, `eachline` and `Iterators`' adapters are no `Iterable` (it promises repeatable iteration). *Proposal:* `IterableOnce{T}` as `Iterable`'s supertrait ([the research](record/collection-and-linalg-traits-research-2026-11-07.md) §1.1).
156. **The iteration state is `Any`:** a loop over a value known only as `Dynamic{Iterable{T}}` boxes the state at each step ([the review](reviews/2026-11-07-basetraits-choices.md) L1). *To measure:* items per second against a `Vector`, before a port's hot loop runs on such a value.

157. **In code compiled by facts, a function barrier loses its callee's declared result** (found 2026-11-07 by W-104's first slice).
   - *The case:* TraitMakie's recipes keep their data in fields typed by a trait (`pos::Positions`), and call kernels compiled per type (`limits(@specialize(pos::Positions))::NTuple{4,Float64}`). Then `datalimits(p::Lines) = limits(p.pos)` is a run-time site, and facts types its result `Any`. So the operation's declared result fails the membership check, at the library's recipes and at a user's recipe (`Ribbon`) that calls them.
   - *The rules that block it:* a run-time site's result is `Any` under facts ([facts §5](reference/facts.md#5-run-time-sites)). A declared result is read only for a trait's operation: lowering records `f(…)::RT` ([compiler §6.8](reference/compiler.md#68-a-declared-result-on-method)), but a run-time site does not read it. And a dispatch made when the code runs could select a later method that declares nothing.
   - *Also:* the membership check infers its top frame plainly but its callees under their own policy, so a member is checked by a mix of the two.
   - *Proposals:*
     - (a) A run-time site in facts code types its result as the declared result `RT` of every method it could select now, asserted at the call, as an operation's is (§7.9). A later method returning something else raises `TypeError` there.
     - (b) An idiom, no rule: a kernel whose result callers rely on becomes an operation of the trait, with a default.
     - (c) The check infers under the serving method's own policy, whatever (a) or (b) gives.

     *Measured for (c)* (2026-11-08, E-311's first build, `unified` 917e767d73, which inferred reflection's top frame under the method's policy): TraitMakie fails to load, `MappedColors` refused as a `ColorSpec` because `rgba` infers `Any` at a function barrier. So (c) needs (a) or (b) first. The build in use infers the top frame plainly (facts F-24).

     The port asserts the result at each such barrier until this is decided (TraitMakie `unified` e95da97; TraitDifferentialEquations' `at` on a solution, two sites, `unified` 893a434, found once TraitWorld bd9a721 checked a package's family members).

159. **A function stored before its signature is known cannot keep the caller's choice of attribute** (found 2026-11-08 by W-104's third slice, TraitDifferentialEquations' front end).
   - *The case:* SciML's `ODEFunction(f; jac)` and `DiscreteCallback(condition, affect!)` store the user's functions before the problem's types are known; `ODEProblem` and a solver's `init` enter them at a signature computed from the state and the parameters (`CoreRHSSig{T}`, `CondSig{U, T, P}`). TDE's tests choose per function: `ODEFunction(compiled_for(f); jac = compiled_for(j))`, `DiscreteCallback(with_dynamic(c), a)`. The earlier design kept such a function with its attribute (`Kept{Callable}`) and wrapped it again at the signature (`rewrap`).
   - *The rules that block it:*
     - a cast decides only the call it is an argument of, and a plain front end passes none on ([traits §7.6](reference/traits.md#76-casts)); a cast in a call with keyword arguments is not lowered yet (lowering L-56);
     - a value carries an attribute only as a `Dynamic{X}` ([traits §7.5](reference/traits.md#75-what-decides));
     - `Dynamic{X}` needs `X`'s parameters, so `Dynamic{Callable}` is refused ([traits §7.7](reference/traits.md#77-dynamicx)).
     So a record that holds a function before its signature is known holds a plain value, and the library's default decides when the function is entered: `Dynamic`, `Callable`'s. The caller cannot choose `Compiled` for a function inside an `ODEFunction` or a callback.
   - `ODEProblem(compiled_for(f), u0, tspan, p)` itself is expressible: a constructor with a trait argument whose signature its `where` variables take from `u0` and `p`, and `keep` ([traits §8.2](reference/traits.md#82-a-struct-parameter-bounded-by-a-trait)).
   - *Proposals:*
     - (a) **`Dynamic{Callable}` without a signature:** a function kept `Dynamic` before its signature is known, called only after it is wrapped again at a signature (`Dynamic{Callable{A, S}}(unwrap(d))`, which a library writes where it enters the function). `keep(f)` at an argument typed by the bare `Callable` gives it under `Dynamic`, and `f` itself under `Compiled`. A record then stores `keep(f)`, and the caller's cast at the record's constructor decides. Only for `Callable`: its members are functions, which Julia already calls without a signature.
     - (b) A library's own carrier for the choice (`TDE.Compiled(f)`), unwrapped at entry: a convention per library, not the language's.
     - (c) No per-function choice inside a record: the library's default decides, and a caller who wants `Compiled` builds the problem with the function itself (`ODEProblem(compiled_for(f), …)`).
   - *Recommendation:* (a), mine: one rule for a value that is not yet at its signature, the earlier design's `Kept{Callable}` as a `Dynamic` box.
   - *A second case* (closed item 111): `Tables.ByRow{F}` stores the user's function at its own type, so `ByRow`'s method and DataFrames' mini-language are compiled per closure (E-216: a new closure's whole `transform` 24–25 ms against the kernel's 6.1–6.8 ms). DataFrames' plain pipeline reads the function (`nameof` for column names, `ComposedFunction`'s fast path): `unwrap(d)` serves it under (a).
   - The port takes (c) until this is decided: `ODEFunction` and the callbacks store the functions as given; `ODEProblem`'s constructor takes the function as a trait argument. The tests that cast inside a record are marked with this issue.


161. **Does a trait's `@default_dynamic` apply to a library's own type that wraps a user's function?** (found 2026-11-08 by E-309, NetworkDynamics' new equations on the unified build).
   - *The case:* NetworkDynamics' batches keep each component's model, a `FunctionDynamics{typeof(swing!), …}` that wraps the user's function. `VertexDynamics` and `EdgeDynamics` declare `@default_dynamic`, so a solver's models are compiled once. `FunctionDynamics` is NetworkDynamics' own type, so under the exception for the trait's own package the default does not apply: the model is kept as itself, `Network`'s type names the user's functions, and new equations compile the code over the network again (6.6 s against the earlier port's 1.1 s, E-309).
   - *The rule in question:* "`@default_dynamic` does not apply to a value whose type Julia itself or the trait's own package defines: those types are few and fixed" ([traits §7.5](reference/traits.md#75-what-decides) step 4; [compiler §6.3](reference/compiler.md#63-the-attribute-at-a-call) step 4). A parametric type whose parameters carry a user's type is neither few nor fixed.
   - *Proposals:*
     - (a) **Own with its parameters:** the exception holds where the type and every type among its parameters, recursively, are Julia's or the trait's package's; a value parameter is the type's own. `FunctionDynamics{typeof(swing!)}` and `Base.Fix1{typeof(f), Int}` take the trait's default; `Vector{Int}` and `FunctionDynamics{typeof(sin)}` stay `Compiled`.
     - (b) No exception: `@default_dynamic` applies to every member. Simple, but Julia's own collections at a trait with a dynamic default would lose their per-type code.
     - (c) Keep the rule; the library wraps its records in `Dynamic{X}` itself. That is a port built around the rule, and it overrides a caller's `compiled_for` at construction.
   - *Recommendation:* (a), mine, built (`unified` 7fb94ae02b, gate `n6_attribute`): the port keeps its model with `keep` at an argument typed by the trait, and the batch call's `compiled_for` compiles the kernel per model. Measured (E-310, E-314): new equations 0.59–0.66 s against 6.6 s, the steady state 228–257 ms, once the port's kernel types its model by the trait ([open issue 162](#2-design)).

162. **A cast, or a carried `Compiled`, stops silently at a parameter no trait types** (found 2026-11-09 by E-314, NetworkDynamics' steady state on the unified build).
   - *The case:* NetworkDynamics' kernel calls `_run_batch!(…, compiled_for(batch.model), …)`; the batch holds its model as `Dynamic{VertexDynamics}` (open issue 161). `_run_batch!(::Type{VertexModel}, m, batches, k, args)` does not type `m`. So:
     - the cast has no effect there ([traits §7.6](reference/traits.md#76-casts)), and the box is passed as itself;
     - Julia compiles `_run_batch!` once, for `Dynamic{VertexDynamics}`, and every operation in the solver's hot loop is dispatched when it runs.
     - The steady state takes 436–475 ms against 228–257 ms with `m::VertexDynamics` (E-310 (b), E-314). Nothing reports it.
   - *The same for a carried attribute:* code compiled per key passes `Compiled` on only at an argument typed by a trait ([traits §7.5](reference/traits.md#75-what-decides) step 2). Through an untyped helper the value is plain again, and the next trait-typed call applies the trait's default. Typing only the inner helpers (`_vertex!(m::VertexDynamics, …)`) made the steady state slower (1.10–1.13 s): the kernel was still compiled once.
   - *The rules that block it:* a cast decides the argument at that call only where the selected method types it by a trait (traits §7.6, [compiler §6.9](reference/compiler.md#69-casts-and-keep)); step 2 carries the attribute only through trait-typed arguments. Julia's habit is the untyped helper.
   - *Proposals:*
     - (a) **The attribute passes through a parameter no trait types:** a cast or a carried attribute at such a parameter is part of the callee's key (as `Core.attributed_call` keys a trait argument), so `compiled_for(d)` passes the held object, compiled per its type, and the callee passes `Compiled` on. A plain value is unchanged: no attribute, the trait's default at the next trait-typed call. Needs the attribute bits at any argument; a key Julia's specialization does not show.
     - (b) **The idiom:** a parameter that passes a trait's value on is typed by the trait, as a Rust generic names its bound to keep static dispatch. What the port does (NetworkDynamics 7e8997d). No new rule.
     - (c) **Make the silent case loud:** a package's tests check that `detect_unused_casts(mod)` is empty, as they check `detect_ambiguities`, so a cast at an untyped parameter fails a test. The ports' tests run it: TraitDifferentialEquations, TraitMakie, TraitTables, TraitCSV, the forks of Tables, CSV and DataFrames, NetworkDynamics' quality tests and ndport's subset; none has a cast without effect (E-320).
   - *Recommendation:* (b) with (c), mine: no hidden key, and the trap becomes a test failure. (a) only if the ports show the idiom is too much to ask.


165. **A trait argument passed on to plain code, or captured by a closure: a function to `map`, any value through `keep`, a generator** (found 2026-11-09 by the docs examples, W-117).
   - *The case:* [traits §9](reference/traits.md#9-functions-callable)'s own example, `colmap(f::Callable{Tuple{Float64}, Float64}, t::ColTable, i::Int) = map(f, col(t, i))`, is refused in a trait module: "TraitUseError: colmap is declared bounded, and is open: map(Callable{Tuple{Float64}, Float64}, Vector{Float64}) … is not in the contract of Callable{Tuple{Float64}, Float64}". Passing a function to a higher-order function (`map`, `filter`, `sum(f, xs)`, `foreach`, `sort(xs; by = f)`) is Julia's habit, and each is a call of another function with it.
   - *The rule is right about the effect:* where `f` is `Dynamic`, `map(f, …)` is a run-time site, and `map` is compiled per closure, which `Callable` is meant to avoid.
   - *What does work, and the hole beside it:*
     - `map(keep(f), col(t, i))` is accepted, since `keep(t)` keeps a method fully bounded ([traits §7.3](reference/traits.md#73-fully-bounded-methods)). It does what is wanted: under `Dynamic`, `map` is compiled once, at `Dynamic{Callable{Tuple{Float64}, Float64}}`, for two closures; under `Compiled`, per function (`unified` 6767b03893, TraitWorld dbce5f7; a scratch test, 2026-11-09).
     - The same rule accepts `colsum(t::ColTable) = sum(keep(t))`. Its result depends on the attribute: under `Dynamic`, `MethodError` (`iterate(::Dynamic{ColTable})`); under `Compiled`, the member's own `sum`. That contradicts "the results are the same; only the speed differs" ([traits §7.4](reference/traits.md#74-the-attribute-compiled-and-dynamic)).
   - *A second case, a closure:* `dotp(a::Samples{T}, b::Samples{T}) where {T} = sum(sample(a, i) * sample(b, i) for i in 1:nsamples(a); init = zero(T))` is refused: "_typeof_captured_variable(Samples) … is not in the contract". A generator or a comprehension over a trait argument captures it, and capturing is open (traits §7.3), since the closure's field takes the member's type. Traits §6.5's own `total(t::ColTable)::Float64 = sum(sum(col(t, i)) for i in 1:ncols(t))` has the same shape.
   - *The rules:* traits §7.3 (a call of another function with the argument is open; wrapping it as `keep(t)` or `Dynamic{X}(t)` keeps the method fully bounded), §7.4. A closure: §7.3 (capturing it in a closure is open).
   - *Proposals:*
     - (a) **The classification follows `keep` and `Dynamic{X}(…)`:** their result counts as the argument. Passed on into an argument or slot typed by the trait, the method stays fully bounded; passed to anything else, it is open. This closes the hole.
     - (b) **For `Callable` only, `keep(f)` passed to plain code is in the contract:** a member is a function, and the idiom is to hand it to code that calls it. Under `Dynamic` that code gets the wrapper, which answers only calls at `A`, with the result checked; under `Compiled`, the function. The results differ only where the plain code does more than call it at `A` (`nameof(f)`; an empty `map`'s element type where `S` is abstract), and then `Dynamic` is the stricter. The hint of the `TraitUseError` at a `Callable` argument names `keep(f)`.
     - (c) **BaseTraits' higher-order algorithms at `Callable`,** with names of their own, as `retain!` ([traits §10.3](reference/traits.md#103-algorithms)): fully bounded by the current rules, but a growing list beside Julia's own.
     - (d) **A closure captures a trait argument as `keep` stores it:** under `Dynamic` its field holds `Dynamic{X}(t)`, so the closure has one type for all members; under `Compiled`, `t` itself. The classification follows the closure's body as a function of the module, so a generator whose body uses only the contract stays fully bounded.
   - *Recommendation (mine):* (a) with (b), and (d). `map(keep(f), xs)` is the idiom for passing a function on; any other trait's `keep(t)` passed beyond the contract is open; a generator over a trait argument is fully bounded where its body is. Not built. Until it is decided, the documentation's examples write such a method as a loop over the elements, which is fully bounded under the current rules.

166. **Code compiled once at a stream typed `IO` reaches its targets by dynamic calls** (found 2026-11-09 by Base's `precompile` test, W-129 (b); counted by E-330 on a build where Base's methods typed `io::IO` were compiled once). Base's methods are compiled per stream type ([compiler §7.5](reference/compiler.md#75-io)); the case stands for code an author erases (`IOContext{IO}`) and for any set compiled once.
   - *The case:* a call on a stream typed `IO` that is not one of `IO`'s operations infers `Any` and dispatches when it runs where it has more matching methods than inference unions (`read(io)`, `read(f, Int32)`), where its lookup is flagged ambiguous, or where its receiver is a wrapper built from the stream. The instance it reaches is in the image only if Base's build ran it. Compiled per stream type, these calls were static.
   - *Counted* (the erasure report, `experiments/unification/audit/erased_calls.jl`, `unified` d554855579): Base's 931 methods typed `IO` hold 139 split fallbacks and 2,018 other dynamic sites on a value of `IO`, in 164 functions: `write` 949, `print` 145, `show` 110, keyword calls 94, `IOContext` 89, `read` 75; receivers `IO` 1,886, `IOContext` 118. Most are calls whose lookup is flagged ambiguous at `All{AbstractString, IO}`, an intersection no type is in ([compiler §5.4](reference/compiler.md#54-the-ambiguity-report-at-a-membership): fixed by E-331). A small method inlined (`print(io::IO, s::String)`) carries its dynamic call into callers that know the stream. After E-331 (`unified` 43c222c751): 1,093 sites. Tested with an `IOBuffer` in place of the erased stream, 128 are dynamic because of the stream (`read` 41, keyword calls 13, `displaysize` 11, `position` 11), 953 because of another argument, as Base compiled per stream type would be.
   - *Wrappers over a stream* (the same report): `IOContext{IO_t}`, `EachLine{IOT}`, `ReadEachIterator{T, IOT}` and JuliaSyntax's `Lexer{IO_t}` take the stream's type as a parameter. Built in code compiled once, `IOContext`'s constructor typed `io::IO` included, they are abstract even where the caller knows the stream: `IOContext(::IOBuffer, …)` infers `IOContext`, a loop over `eachline(::IOBuffer)` infers `Any`.
   - *Effects:* per call (E-330: E-328's workloads on an `IOBuffer` 2–24× vanilla's); values that lose their types downstream (the cache header's reader: its results `Any`, so `check_clone_targets` was reached dynamically); a session without the compiler (`JULIA_LOAD_CODEGEN_LIB=0`) interprets an instance the image lacks and fails at its first `ccall`: Base's `precompile` test, the child that loads a package without LLVM, fails at `read(::GenericIOBuffer)` (438 of 457).
   - *The rules:* code compiled once at a stream typed `IO` (erased by its author, [traits §9b](reference/traits.md#9b-streams-io)); [compiler §7.2](reference/compiler.md#72-the-split) splits only an operation's call; §9's rule that an image holds what a run-time site reaches applies to facts code.
   - *The first failure:* the readers of a package cache's header, compiled once at their stream. Compiled per stream type, as Base's methods are, they read typed values.
   - *Since E-332* (`unified` 7bd8b6e512, the battery `w332`): the same child fails one more check (437 of 457). It stops while printing a tuple. The likely cause, not traced: a split's branch through a `:caller_world` pointer, whose converter is compiled ahead of time or by the JIT, and this child has no JIT. The table's fallback takes the dynamic call there (compiler §7.3); a branch's pointer has no fallback. Proposals: an image holds the converters of its branches' pointers (as §7.3's "ahead of time" for the table's targets, C-39); without the compiler, a branch's thunk dispatches.
   - *With Base's methods per stream type* (E-337, `unified` b05ae361e0, the battery `w337`): Base's `precompile` test passes, 457 of 457. The case stands for code an author erases.
   - *Proposals:*
     - (a) **An image holds what a call at a set's candidates reaches:** while an image is built, a dynamic call in code compiled once whose receiver is typed by a set with candidates gets the instance each of the first candidates selects, as §9's rule for facts run-time sites. The split's branches for an operation already do this.
     - (b) **Split every call at a receiver typed by a set over its first candidates,** not only an operation's, with the dynamic call as the fallback: the branches are static calls, so their targets are compiled with the caller and the image holds them. The result is the join, `Any` past the candidates.
     - (c) **More of `IO`'s interface as operations with declared results:** `read(io, ::Type{T})::T` for Julia's bits types, `write(io, ::String)::Int`; and `write`'s and `unsafe_write`'s declared result `Int`, not `Integer` (with `Integer`, `n += write(io, b)` in `write(::IO, ::Char)` infers `Any`). The values keep their types; the calls split.
     - (d) *Withdrawn with the decision on `IO`:* the erased wrapper is made by its own constructor, `IOContext{IO}(io)`, where the author erases.
   - *Recommendation (mine):* (c) for `write`'s and `unsafe_write`'s `Int` (E-333, built), then for `read` of Julia's bits types, `displaysize` and `position`, the 128 sites the stream decides; (a) for the rest.



170. **A split's table targets at a type no image saw are compiled at the first call** (found 2026-11-09 by E-337's traced forks session).
   - *The case:* DataFrames' erasing entry `show(@nospecialize(io::IO), df; allrows = !get(io, :limit, false), …)` reads the stream's properties in its keyword defaults, at the stream typed `IO`. `get` is an operation of `IO`'s contract, so the call splits. The REPL's stream, `IOContext{REPL.LimitIO{TTYTerminal}}`, is past the candidates, and the table's miss compiles a target for it: 34.6 ms. The eraser's writes reach `unsafe_write` at `LimitIO{TTYTerminal}`: a pointer, 5.5 ms. The `TTY` branch's target in Base's image was invalidated and compiled again: 8.2 ms (the cause not traced). In all 53.5 ms of the `show` step's 81–110 ms (`experiments/unification/e323/session-traced-w337.out`).
   - *The rules:* [compiler §7.3](reference/compiler.md#73-the-table-of-a-calls-targets) (a miss makes the target at the call; ahead of time, an image compiles the targets for the members a package declares: C-39, not built); [§7.2](reference/compiler.md#72-the-split) (a branch's pointer to its candidate's target).
   - *Proposals:*
     - (a) **C-39 for a closed type's subtypes:** the image of the package that defines a subtype of a type with a contract (REPL's `LimitIO`) holds the targets of the contract's operations at the concrete types its workload makes.
     - (b) **An erasing entry reads the stream through the eraser:** it erases first and reads `:limit` from `IOContext{IO}(io)`, whose `get` reads its own properties and splits nothing. A port's idiom; `show`'s keyword defaults make it awkward.
     - (c) **The target for a type whose method's instance is in an image is a dispatching thunk,** compiled once per call signature: past the candidates the call dispatches in the caller's world, a dynamic call in place of a compile.
   - *Measured* (E-338): with the REPL's display erasing, DataFrames' keyword defaults read the erased stream, and the `get` target is gone; the `show` step compiles 44.4 ms, a candidate's invalidated target 7.0 ms of it.
   - *Recommendation (mine):* (a), the design's own rule (C-39); the invalidated candidate's target with open issue 167.
   - *Since C-81's fix* (E-340): `IO`'s candidates are fixed with its contract, so REPL's `LimitIO` can never be one; (a) stays the way.



178. **What the guards' removal leaves to simplify, and what it leaves unmeasured** (found 2026-11-11 while building W-140; G-8, G-1, G-4).
   - *The cases:* (a) a forwarding member has methods of its own for the required operations (W-128, open issue 158 (a)), written so that the trait's method was no shadow of a guarded call; no call is guarded now, and the trait's method could serve a forwarding member again. (b) The trait's method for a required operation raises at a member that no method serves; with no guard its result needs no special rule, but the mistake's message is still Julia's sentence for a thrown `MethodError` (open issue 177 (a)). (c) A membership's walk runs at every declaration and now invalidates: its cost at a port's load, at a REPL and in composition is not measured on this build.
   - *The rules:* [compiler §5.5](reference/compiler.md#55-a-membership-invalidates-as-a-method-does); [traits §5.5](reference/traits.md#55-forwarding-with-by); [lowering §2](reference/lowering.md#2-trait).
   - *The tools:* `experiments/unification/w140/` (the small cases, `load_invalidations.jl` with its control `nowalk`); `experiments/unification/audit/set_index.jl` (the index against the signatures); `experiments/unification/w134/cause.jl` for a session.
   - *Proposals,* for the synthesis: (a) remove the member's own forwarding methods once the rounds show the trait's method costs nothing there; (b) with 177 (a); (c) W-140's rounds, at the conceptually complete state.

177. **Where the trait form's messages fall short of plain Julia's** (found 2026-11-11 by E-362, the same mistakes in both forms; G-5). Items (a) and (b) are closed (below).
   - *The cases:* (c) A pirate is silent unless strict mode is on, as in plain Julia. (d) A port's run-time failures (E-363, SciML's three error cases): none is a mistake against a contract, so the port's messages are those of its plain twin, 1.2 to 1.7 times as long, and upstream's hand-written explanations say more in two of the three.
   - *The rules:* strict mode is off unless turned on ([traits §12.3](reference/traits.md#123-strict-mode)).
   - *The tool:* `experiments/unification/w136/` (`run.sh`, `summarize.py`): each mistake in both forms, the message's lines, bytes and frames, and two counts of form: signatures printed as tuple types (12 on TraitWorld 05991fc, 0 on 158d000) and the library's frames above the user's line (25, 24; 2 on TraitWorld 78a78af, E-370).
   - *Proposals,* for the synthesis: (c) is the choice of strict mode's default, which GT-2 prices; (d) a `Callable` whose result infers `Union{}` at its declared arguments (it always throws: `p[1]` at `p::Nothing`) is refused where the function enters, with the declared signature, so the first of SciML's cases is caught at `ODEProblem(…)` or `solve` ([traits §9](reference/traits.md#9-functions-callable)); and a port writes its explanations as upstream does, by a method of its own error type, which no rule of the design gives or prevents.

179. **A fully typed Base surface for trait code** (W-143; G-6, G-1, G-5; desirable, the user, 2026-11-11: "Record \"fully typed Base surface for trait code\" as a desirable together with your plan.).
   - *The case:* fully typed code is exactly what compiles ahead ([traits §14](reference/traits.md#14-concreteness-and-fully-typed-mode)), so a hole in Base below a trait program fails its build, where the user cannot fix it (Base's `repeat(::Char, ::Int)` under TraitTables' table program, E-364). Trait code also keeps an edge to each plain callee in Base (E-355, under §1's tensions).
   - *Three parts of Base:* fully typed already at the signatures typed code calls; holes by accident (an untyped container, a missing declared result), each a small patch; dynamic by purpose (`show` and display, `IO` at its abstract type, the REPL, Pkg, loading, `eval`, reflection, the compiler).
   - *The plan:* (1) the claim: Base is fully typed at the signatures typed code calls it with; a hole there is a defect of Base, fixed in Base and proposed upstream. (2) A tool: `fully_typed` over Base's and the standard libraries' exported functions at broad typical signatures (`String`, `Int`, `Float64`, their vectors), listing the holes with counts; kept in the experiments repository. (3) The part that is dynamic by purpose is not patched: `show` and `IO` as traits with declared results are a port of Base, recorded here as pressure for the synthesis.
   - *Measured* (E-368, the session's report, `experiments/unification/w143/`): of 6,179 signatures of Base's 837 exported functions over twelve broad types, 71.4 % are fully typed and 20.1 % have a hole. The holes sit in 106 functions; five hold 73 % of the sites (`typejoin`, `ntuple`, `setproperty!`, `rehash!`, `_growend_internal!`), twenty 91 %. The kinds: paths that build an error (the exception stack, from growing an array or rehashing), a field set by a name that is no constant, reflection, `IO`, logging. The report over-counts calls at a union (166 signatures) and misses holes a build finds; the build's verdict per signature is next, with W-142.
180. **What the membership's method leaves** ([compiler §5.6](reference/compiler.md#56-the-memberships-method-and-the-edges); W-141, E-367; found 2026-11-12; G-8, G-1, G-4). The findings table: `experiments/unification/w141/README.md`. Items (b), (e), (g), (h), (i) are closed (below).
   - *(a) One list for every absence edge.* Julia keeps such edges under the function's type. Each membership scans all of them: 293 µs with 2,000 folded in the session, where a membership cell cost 7.5 µs. Sessions hold 0 to 6 (TraitTables, TraitDifferentialEquations). *Proposal:* index these edges by the trait's name, as Julia indexes keyword calls by another argument; or one function per trait.
   - *(c) Taking a membership back.* A method can be deleted; a membership cannot. Code that called a trait's method at the member holds an edge to that method only, so a removal needs the walk over the trait's methods (compiler §5.5), run for the removal. *Proposal:* build the walk for a removal; then Revise works on memberships.
   - *(d) Tools that list ambiguities see the runtime's methods.* A member of two subtraits is in their common supertrait twice; `Test.detect_ambiguities` reports 12 such pairs of `Core.implements` in the library, and Aqua would. No call ever selects among them. *Proposal:* the tools leave `Core.implements` out; the library's test does.
   - *(f) An overlapping membership drops the dispatch cache,* where its method meets one that code folded. Not measured.
   - *(j) The member as an argument type:* `(::Type{X}, ::S)` as the method's signature. On plain functions, 2,000 abstract members insert at 0.52 to 0.57 ms each, where `Type{<:A}` grows to 12.5 ms each (`w141/probe_valuesig.jl`). Not tried in the runtime. *Proposal:* try it if abstract members of one trait come to cost each other.
181. **The build's Base is not the session's** (found 2026-11-14 by W-142's check of TraitTables' table program; G-6, G-8).
   - *The case:* Julia's build script replaces 28 methods of Base and the standard libraries before a build compiles (printing a type, `sprint`, logging and deprecation warnings off, some `__init__`s), and changes 12 things that are no method: the binding `Base.invokelatest`, eleven functions' `max_args`. The table program prints a type's name: the session's check listed 25 calls inside Base's `show`; the build has none of them.
   - *The pattern:* the check and the build compile one program against two Bases. "Fully typed is exactly what compiles ahead" holds only against one.
   - *The rule:* [traits §16](reference/traits.md#16-compiling-ahead-closed-programs) (the build's Base, read by the check), [compiler §2.7](reference/compiler.md#27-fully-typed-mode) (the check's overlay). Built (mine): the library reads the build script's files into an overlay method table, one list read by both. With traits T-109: what is no method is not read (`bb_invokelatest` differs), and three functions the files add are defined in the session's Base.
   - *The tool:* `TraitWorld.build_base_report()` (what was laid over, what was left out); `experiments/unification/w142/agree.sh` with the apps `bb_*`.
   - *Proposals,* for the synthesis:
     - (a) **The list goes when Base is fully typed there.** Each replacement marks a place where Base is not fully typed at a signature typed code calls ([open issue 179](#2-design), W-143): printing a type is the first. A patch to Base that makes the replacement unneeded stands without traits (W-19).
     - (b) **A closed build's rule in place of the rebinding:** in a closed build nothing is defined later, so a call in the latest world is the call. The compiler infers `invokelatest(f, args…)` as `f(args…)` under the closed rules, and the check agrees with the build without the binding.
     - (c) The build compiles through the same overlay, so that the script changes nothing in its process either.
   - *Recommendation (mine):* (a) as the direction, (b) for the binding; (c) only if the two lists drift.

## 4. Runtime and measurements


66. **W-52 costs the folds of Base's type-introspection helpers in code compiled by facts** (E-162, 2026-10-27). `array_subpadding` (`@assume_effects :total`) calls `padding(T::DataType)` (`:foldable`), whose calls on `DataType` are run-time sites under the facts mode. Before W-52 the caller's assertion still applied (the G1 hole of the facts review's H2) and `array_subpadding(Dual{…}, Float64)` folded; now it runs at every `reinterpret` of dual numbers: in the port (setting P4, julia-1.13 0c589fd385) a Rodas5P solve takes 311–339 ms against Tsit5's 4.5 ms, and step d's steady state 267.7 ms against 33–47 ms before and upstream's 18.6 ms (`ndport/logs/p4-regress-*.log`). Results stay bit-equal. **Taken (mine, 2026-10-27): W-57,** Base's asserted helpers made fully typed, so they fold under the rule ([the W-56 review](reviews/2026-10-27-w56-recorded-concrete-evaluation.md) found a dispatch recorder unsound as drafted). **Deferred, W-56:** keep W-52, and let concrete evaluation fold a call over run-time sites while recording the dispatches it performs as edges: inside a call on constant types every dispatch is at a concrete signature (`fieldoffset(::Type{Dual{…}}, ::Int)`), a fact, so the fold rests on facts again. Needs a dispatch recorder in the runtime (`gf.c`), so a full build. The alternative, reverting W-52's reach through calls until then, reopens G1. **Next** (W-65): E-167 (2), step d after W-57's part 1 (julia-1.13 252edca933). Back near 33–47 ms: this item closes, and W-57's parts 2–3 continue only where the withdrawn-assertion log finds a gap of 20% or more. Still far: part 3 by the log's list. *2026-10-29 (E-235, setting M on julia-1.13 bfca89decc):* step d's steady state is 29.1–29.6 ms, not 267.7 ms; W-57's part 1 likely fixed it. E-167 (2) in setting P4 confirms or not; then this item closes.

160. **Closed (2026-11-12, mine; `unified` 3f33d9e684, gate `n6_attribute`):** proposal (a) built: the cast names are bound, not reserved ([traits §7.6](reference/traits.md#76-casts), [compiler §6.9](reference/compiler.md#69-casts-and-keep)). Lowering emits the cast and the ordinary call, chosen by what the name is bound to; a plain package with a function `compiled_for` loads (the ODE port's plain twin). A cast with keyword arguments or a splat is still not lowered (lowering L-56). *As it stood:* **The cast names are reserved for all code, so a plain package that uses them does not load** (found 2026-11-08 by [the review of the unification](reviews/2026-11-08-unification-review.md), H3). - *The case:* `PlainODE`, TraitDifferentialEquations' plain twin, defines `compiled_for(f) = Compiled(f)` (`TraitDifferentialEquations-unified/baselines/PlainODE/src/PlainODE.jl:111`). On the unified build its precompilation fails: "syntax: "compiled_for" is a cast, reserved as ccall is: it cannot be defined or assigned". Any registered package, script or notebook that names a function or a variable `compiled_for` or `with_dynamic` fails the same way, whether or not it uses traits. - *The rule that blocks it:* the cast names are reserved as `ccall` is ([traits §7.6](reference/traits.md#76-casts), [compiler §6.9](reference/compiler.md#69-casts-and-keep); P-23, mine). Lowering recognizes them by name (`julia-unified/src/julia-syntax.scm`, `cast-names`). - *Why it matters:* a new reserved word is a breaking change to the language. It contradicts the stack's aim that the changes are proposable upstream (W-19) and that plain code runs unchanged. - *Proposals:* - (a) **Casts recognized by binding, not by name:** lowering emits the cast where the name resolves to `Core.compiled_for` or `Core.with_dynamic` (exported by TraitWorld), and leaves a local or module binding of the same name alone; as `Core.Intrinsics` and `GlobalRef` calls are resolved, not reserved. A module that defines its own `compiled_for` simply does not use the cast. - (b) **Macro forms:** `@compiled_for(x)` and `@with_dynamic(x)` at the argument, lowered to the same `Core.attributed_call`. Macros cannot clash with plain code. - (c) Keep the reservation, and rename the twin's function. - *Recommendation:* (a), mine: no reserved names, the same spelling at the call. (b) if lowering cannot see the binding where it needs it.

176. **Closed (2026-11-12, mine; TraitWorld 0dad2e6, TraitDifferentialEquations cecf2c3):** the proposal built: *open* and *closed* are for traits and types; a method is *fully bounded* or *unbounded*, marked `@unbounded` ([traits §7.3](reference/traits.md#73-fully-bounded-methods); the glossary, `intro/`, the ports, the docs examples). *As it stood:* **"Open" names three things** (found 2026-11-11 by the same review, M5; G-8). - *The case:* a trait is *open* (any type may join; [traits §3.4](reference/traits.md#34-open-and-closed)); a method is *open* at an argument (it uses the argument beyond the contract; `@open`, [§7.3](reference/traits.md#73-fully-bounded-methods)); and the pages write of positions "where types are open". A reader meets "an open method at an open trait" in the first hour. The first sense is about who may join, the second about what a method may do: `@open` on a method does the opposite of what an open trait suggests, since it gives up the contract's bound. - *The rule:* the names of [traits §7.3](reference/traits.md#73-fully-bounded-methods) (*fully bounded*, *open*, `@open`, `@bounded`, the option `bounded`). - *Proposal:* keep *open* and *closed* for traits and types. Name a method by what it does with its argument: *fully bounded* against *unbounded*, with `@unbounded`. One sense per word; a rename of one macro and one word in the references, the library and the ports.

167. **Closed (2026-11-14, mine, on re-reading):** proposal (a) is the rule of [compiler §7.2](reference/compiler.md#72-the-split): a branch is inlined only where its body is a leaf, and every other branch calls its candidate's target through a `:caller_world` pointer, so the caller holds no edge to the candidate's instance (`unified` 0c836f3ef6, 7bd8b6e512; E-332). What a split with no table and a constant-folded branch keep is measured with the load's invalidations at the conceptually complete state (W-145). *As it stood:* **Invalidation passes through a split's branches** (found 2026-11-09 by E-330's load report). - *The case:* a split's branch calls its candidate's instance directly, so the caller holds an edge to it. An instance compiled once at a set serves every caller of the operation, and through its branches it depends on each candidate's whole implementation. An invalidation anywhere below one candidate's operation reaches every instance compiled once that calls the operation, and their callers. - *Counted* (the load report, `experiments/unification/audit/load_invalidations.jl`, `unified` d554855579, the goal's packages): 18,781 instances invalidated at the load. TraitWorld's `length(::HasLength)` invalidates 11,994 of them under one pruned arm, `length(::LinearAlgebra.Factorization)` in Base's code that reads the terminal's description. Its callers reach `get(::TTY, :color, …)`, then, through the split's `TTY` branch, `ioproperties(::IO)`, `IOContext(::IO)`, all printing and the parser (JuliaSyntax 6,198). The goal's task: imports 10.5 s, its read 3.6 s (E-330). - *The rules:* [compiler §7.2](reference/compiler.md#72-the-split) (a branch is a static call to the candidate's target); §5.6 (a method at a set invalidates the pruned arms it reaches: correct here); an edge to an instance carries that instance's invalidation. - *Proposals:* - (a) **A declared result is a firewall:** a branch not inlined calls its candidate's target through a pointer valid in the caller's world (the table's `:caller_world` pointer, §7.3), so the caller holds no edge to the candidate's instance; the result is the operation's declared result, asserted, as now. An invalidation stops at the candidate. An inlined branch keeps its edge, since it holds the body. - (b) The same through the table only: the candidates past those inlined are left to the table, with no branch. - (c) Base's code under `get(::TTY, …)` typed, so that no pruned arm sits there: a patch at one place; the pattern stays. - *Built* (E-332, mine): (a) on `unified` 0c836f3ef6, then only a leaf inlined on 7bd8b6e512 (an inlined branch, `get(::TTY, :color, false)` after constant propagation, brought its body's edges). At the goal's load: 17,035 then 6,687 instances invalidated, 22,791 then 2,110 under split edges, 7,422 then 121 under `length(::HasLength)`. - *Left* ([the Fable plan](plans/2026-11-09-compiled-once-equations.md) §4.1): a split with no table still inlines and calls directly; a constant-folded branch keeps its edge. The plan's proposal 1 states the firewall as a rule of §7.2 (a branch depends on its candidate by a dispatch edge, the declared result, and a code edge to a leaf only), with the code slot (§3.3) and a contract edge as a cheaper mechanism than the pointer. - *Measured* (E-337, `unified` b05ae361e0): through the eraser `IOContext{IO}`, a byte or a short string per call 1.5–2.2× the hand-routed proxy, whose branches are static calls; formatted output 1.01–1.11×. - *Recommendation (mine):* (a) as built, stated as the plan's rule; the slot where E-328's short writes show the pointer's cost.

168. **Closed (2026-11-14, mine, on re-reading):** closed by open issue 171's rule: a declared result is asserted, never converted, and a closed type's contract promises no result, so `IO`'s unchecked streams are no case ([traits §7.9](reference/traits.md#79-operations-on-a-value-the-code-knows-only-by-its-trait), [§5.10](reference/traits.md#510-checks)). A member keeps its check while it is a member (TraitWorld 5b3c427, E-342). A test helper for a package's suite (proposal (b)) is not built: no port needs it. *As it stood:* **Members that join by a nominal supertype are not checked against the declared results** (found 2026-11-09 by E-333's build: Core's `unsafe_write` returned a `UInt` where `IO` declares `Int`). - *The case:* `struct S <: IO` makes `S` a member of `IO` before any of its methods exist, so [traits §5.10](reference/traits.md#510-checks)'s check of each serving method's result never runs. A method whose result is outside the declared result meets the declaration only at a call through the trait. With the result asserted, that call raised a `TypeError`: the system image's build failed in `show(::Float64)` writing to Core's `stdout`. - *Counted* (the declared-result report, `experiments/unification/audit/declared_results.jl`, `unified` 43c222c751, against `Int` for `write` and `unsafe_write`): Base 2 streams whose count is a `UInt` (`Filesystem.File`, Core's `stdout` and `stderr`), and 4 forwarding methods that follow the declaration; with the standard libraries, REPL's `LimitIO` (`Union{Int, UInt}`, a forwarder); a stream in CSV's tests (`PartitionSink`) returns its count `n::UInt`. The other operations of `IO`: none outside. - *The rules:* traits §5.10 (checked at a membership); [compiler §7.5](reference/compiler.md#75-io) (`IO`'s members by their declared supertype); traits §7.9 (the result at a call through the trait). - *Proposals:* - (a) **The call through the trait converts** a member's result to the declared result, as a return annotation `f(…)::R` converts, then asserts (traits §7.9). A checked member returns `R` already. Built (E-333, mine). - (b) **A test helper for a package's suite,** as `detect_ambiguities`: the report's check over a module's methods serving a set's operations, reporting a result outside the declaration (after (a), one that does not convert). - (c) Refuse a member outside the declaration at its package's load: breaks working packages (CSV's test stream). - *Recommendation (mine):* (a) was built (E-333) and replaced by an assertion (open issue 171, E-340): a call through the trait raises a `TypeError` where a member escaped its check, and a closed type promises no result, so `IO`'s unchecked streams are no case. (b) with W-129's remaining work. A member keeps its check while it is a member ([traits §5.10](reference/traits.md#510-checks)): a later serving method is inferred at the checked call and refused outside the declared result, TraitWorld `unified` 5b3c427 (E-342), mine; for a membership loaded from an image too (TraitWorld 6871ea9, E-344).

173. **Closed (2026-11-14, mine, on re-reading):** the rule it names is gone. No set is closed after load: a non-membership is relied on wherever the code is compiled, and a membership invalidates as a method does ([compiler §4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries), [§5.5](reference/compiler.md#55-a-membership-invalidates-as-a-method-does); W-140). An extension's membership is a late membership as an extension's method is a late method; what its load invalidates is counted with the loads (W-145). *As it stood:* **A membership declared in a package extension is late at a set closed after load** (found 2026-11-09 by [the performance review](reviews/2026-11-09-performance-day.md), M4). - *The case:* `run_package_callbacks` marks a package loaded before its extensions run, so a membership an extension declares (CSV's InlineStrings extension declares 20) comes after the mark, and at a set closed after load it invalidates whatever relied on the negative, at every load. - *The rule:* [compiler §4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries) (a module's mark once its package has loaded); strict mode counts an extension as its parent ([facts §11.2](reference/facts.md#112-strict-mode)). - *Proposal:* a package is marked loaded after the extensions whose triggers are already loaded have run; an extension whose trigger loads later stays a late declarer. No port opts in yet, so nothing pays for it now.

177 (a), (b). **Closed (2026-11-12, mine; TraitWorld 399f389 with `unified` 8f42ba7ed7; E-370):** (a) an operation called outside its declared signature raises `OperationError`, which names the call, the declared signature and the member's methods; (b) a check's error is raised at the user's line: the library's frames above it are 2 in E-362's trait runs, where they were 24 ([traits §11.3](reference/traits.md#113-errors-and-their-messages); [compiler §10.1](reference/compiler.md#101-definition-and-image-hooks)). *As it stood:* (a) an operation called outside its declared signature (`scale(Circle(1.0), 2)` for `scale(s::Scalable, k::Float64)::Self`): the trait's own method takes the call and throws a `MethodError`, so Julia prints "This error has been manually thrown, explicitly, so the method may exist but be intentionally marked as unimplemented", the trait's method is the first candidate and the first frame, and the user's method, with the argument that differs, comes second. (b) The library's own frames stand above the user's line in every check's error: three at an `@implement`, two at a method's definition, four at the `bounded` check; 24 in E-362's 16 trait runs.


**Closed (2026-11-12, the user: "Pirate method rule is approved. That was the point of \"behaves as a method\"."):** strict mode's rule for a third party's membership (W-141's rest; §1). A pirate's membership follows the pirate method's rule ([traits §12.2](reference/traits.md#122-pirates), [§12.3](reference/traits.md#123-strict-mode)); built in TraitWorld a2ca7be with `unified` 7fee959383, with traits T-112. The one case where the method's own ownership rule and the membership's differ (the library's membership of Core's `Dynamic` in `Callable`, E-367) needs nothing: the membership's rule, by the trait's owner or the type's, is what is applied.

180 (b), (e), (g), (h), (i). **Closed (2026-11-12, the user: "Conceptual parsimony for the user only requires implementation to behave like a method. The implementation can be whatever is simplest/cleanest/fastest."; `unified` 8b4fde6e84):** a membership is not defined as a method; its method is the runtime's, the implementation of the edges ([compiler §5.6](reference/compiler.md#56-the-memberships-method-and-the-edges)). (b) The library's membership rule beside the method's: the choice of strict mode's rule is in §1. (e) A membership an image's load refuses: its method is taken out of force (gate `n12_image_instances`). (g) A membership written by hand is not checked, and (h) a refused one leaves its method in the table: a program defines no method of `Core.implements`, and the runtime adds its method after the checks (gate `n29_membership_method`). (i) What stays a rule of the membership: one instance per member, the same at an image's load, the reach to callers of the trait's methods and the contract's check are the membership's own rules in the references; the options not taken are in [alternatives](design/alternatives.md#a-membership-as-a-method-options-not-taken-2026-11-12).

174. **Closed (2026-11-10, mine; TraitDifferentialEquations f903e40, TraitWorld 048c44e, E-354):** proposal (a) and (b) built. TraitIndexing's `_read` has a joint method for every type in both `SeriesProvider` and `ValueProvider`. BaseTraits' `getindex` of `Indexable` and `Keyed` stays: no one answer is right for every common member, each defines its own (as `Dict`). Each package's tests assert the list: TraitWorld's `test/basetraits.jl` names the one pair, TraitDifferentialEquations' `test/runtests.jl` asserts none. The tool: `experiments/unification/e289/e347_detect.jl`. *As it stood:* **Two traits' methods of one function with no joint method, in the libraries** (found 2026-11-10 by E-347's tool, `experiments/unification/e289/e347_detect.jl`: `Test.detect_ambiguities` over the trait modules of an environment). *The pattern:* two traits each declare an operation of one function, and a type in both needs the joint method (traits §6.3). Reflection names the pair as soon as some type is in both; the report at a membership names it for each new member without a more specific method. *The counts* (`unified` 53d28eea68; `e289/logs/e347-detect-*.log`): 2 pairs. BaseTraits' `getindex(::Indexable{T}, i)` against `getindex(::Keyed{K, V}, k)`; TraitIndexing's `_read(::ValueProvider, f)` against `_read(::SeriesProvider, f)`. No tie with a method at `Any`. Two plain ambiguities in TraitMakie (`convert(::Type{ColorSpec}, x)` and `convert(::Type{MarkerShape}, x)` against Base's `convert(::Type{T}, x::T)`, raised by `convert(ColorSpec, c)` at a member) are fixed by their joint methods (TraitMakie c9e294b). *The rule that produces it:* a trait's operation is a method at the trait (lowering §2), so two traits with an operation of one name always tie at a common member that brings no method of its own. *Proposal:* (a) a package's tests assert the tool's list is empty or names each pair with its reason, as they check `detect_unused_casts`; (b) for the two pairs, the joint method where one answer is right for every common member, else the pair stays and each common member defines its own (as `Dict` does for `getindex`).

172. **Closed (2026-11-10, mine; `unified` 999777ab77, E-341):** proposal (a) built: the match stays at an open set, the lookup drops it only at a final pair and reports the cells, a `Callable` component and a `Vararg` tail are met. (b), a guarded drop, not needed: the unmet-pair census (`experiments/unification/audit/unmet_open_pairs.jl`) finds no pair in the goal's, the forks' or the ODE port's environments. *As it stood:* A match met only at an intersection no type is in, at an open set: dispatched, or left out under a guard. - *The case:* E-331 built dispatch's lookup to leave out such a match where a covering match exists, at every set, reading the intersection's members from entries only. `Callable`'s members are methods (compiler §7.4), so `All{AbstractString, Callable}` was judged unmet while a string type with a call method is in it: `f(MyS(), 1)` raises the ambiguity and code compiled at `Callable` returns the covering method's result (`experiments/unification/audit/probe_unmet_callable.jl`; G1 fails). At an ordinary open set the drop records no cell: after a membership that meets the intersection, compiled code runs the covering method while dispatch is ambiguous, a folded negative §4.8 forbids. - *The rules:* [compiler §5.4](reference/compiler.md#54-the-ambiguity-report-at-a-membership) (now: the match stays at an open set; C-82, the implementation still drops it); [§4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries) (negatives never fold at an open set; a final pair drops with its cell, E-334); [§5.6](reference/compiler.md#56-cells) (guards on cells). - *What the drop bought:* a static call at `describe(::ColTable)` against `describe(::AbstractString)`, unmeasured on any port; it was built for `All{AbstractString, IO}` with `IO` a set, which `IO` no longer is. - *Proposals:* - (a) **Keep the match** at an open set: the call dispatches; the reference's rule now. Small: `drop_unmet_matches` restricted to final pairs, `Callable` read by its methods; gate `n20_unmet_lookup` (2) flips, and gains the probe and a membership declared after compilation. - (b) **Leave it out under a guard on the pair's cell** ([the plan on compiled-once equations](plans/2026-11-09-compiled-once-equations.md), proposal 4): the call stays static, and a membership that meets the intersection fails the guard, so the call dispatches. Needs a cell per pair at open sets. - *Recommendation (mine):* (a) now; (b) only if a port's session shows the dynamic call.

171. **Closed (2026-11-10, mine, [the plan](plans/2026-11-09-171-declared-results-without-conversion.md) after [its review](reviews/2026-11-09-plan-review-171.md); `unified` ef1233e79e, E-340):** a declared result is asserted, never converted, and a closed type's contract promises no result (`IO`'s operations `Any`), so no value depends on inference; `handle1` joins a const result taken for its effects, which had made compiled code return a value its type excluded. *As it stood:* At a value typed by a closed type with a contract, a result's type depends on what inference knows. - *The case* (`unified` b05ae361e0; `experiments/unification/audit/probe_result_by_inference.jl`): `struct S <: IO end` with `Base.write(::S, ::UInt8) = UInt(7)`. `write(v[1], 0x01)` returns an `Int64` for `v::Vector{IO}` and a `UInt64` for `v::Vector{Any}`; under `--compile=min` both return a `UInt64`. A stream whose `write` returns a `String` raises a `MethodError` through `Vector{IO}` and returns the string through `Vector{Any}`. On vanilla Julia every call returns what the method returns. - *The pattern:* a rule applied only where inference applies it makes a result depend on inference. The conversion lives in the split, and inference makes the split; dispatch, the interpreter and a call at `Any` never convert. The same rule at a set has the same consequence for a member whose result is outside the declaration (168: members by their declared supertype); not probed at a set. - *The rules:* [traits §7.9](reference/traits.md#79-operations-on-a-value-the-code-knows-only-by-its-trait) and [compiler §7.2](reference/compiler.md#72-the-split) (the call converts; 168 (a), mine, E-333); [compiler §7.1](reference/compiler.md#71-the-rule) (the rule at a closed type with a contract, in either policy). Against [facts §2.1](reference/facts.md#21-g1-identical-or-error) (G1: the same result, or raises) and [§13.1](reference/facts.md#131-the-product-setting) (plain code keeps Julia's behaviour), which state the exception as Open. - *Counted* (the declared-result report, `experiments/unification/audit/declared_results.jl`, issue 168): the members outside `IO`'s declarations are Base's `Filesystem.File` and Core's `stdout` and `stderr` (`UInt` counts), REPL's `LimitIO` and CSV's test stream `PartitionSink`. Every other result through `IO` is the declared type already, so the conversion changes nothing there. - *Proposals:* - (a) **Declared results only where every method gives them:** at `IO`, `write` and `unsafe_write` declare no result (`Any`), as vanilla infers at `::IO`; the split keeps its branches, and no result changes. The cost: code that sums counts at a value typed `IO` infers `Any` there, as on vanilla. To measure first with E-328's workloads and the forks' `show` step. - (b) **Base's own counts made `Int`** (`Filesystem.File`, Core's streams, `LimitIO`), as Julia documents `write`'s count, and the call asserting instead of converting: Base's streams give the same result in every mode; a user's stream outside the declaration still raises through `IO` only. - (c) **The conversion in the member, not at the call:** a method serving an operation with a declared result returns the converted result wherever it is called, as a return annotation on the method. The same result through dispatch, the interpreter and the split; a plain method's result changes where nothing uses traits. - *Recommendation (mine):* (a) at `IO`, measured before it is built; at sets, (c) with [traits §5.10](reference/traits.md#510-checks)'s check, so that a declared result is a property of the member, not of the call site.

- **Closed (2026-11-09, the user: "That said, b-display is adopted."):** the REPL's display erases the stream, proposal (a): REPL's `show_limited` and Base's `TextDisplay` pass `IOContext{IO}`; the ports' display entries plain, with a workload line ([decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; [traits §9b](reference/traits.md#9b-streams-io)). *As it stood:* **Whether the REPL's display erases the stream** (the user, 2026-11-09: "Measure the B-display arm"; [the plan](plans/2026-11-09-io-alternatives.md) §7 question 3; E-338, `unified` branch `b-display`). REPL's `show_limited` passes `IOContext{IO}` to `show`, so every value's display at the REPL runs at one stream type.   - *Measured* (E-338): with DataFrames' code unchanged and one workload line, `show(IOContext{IO}(IOBuffer()), MIME"text/plain"(), df)`, the forks' `show` step 0.087 s, as fast as DataFrames' own erasing entries (0.065–0.115 s, E-337) and against 0.205–0.211 s; without the line 0.159–0.198 s. The output equal; REPL's suite as on vanilla.   - *Measured too* ([the performance review](reviews/2026-11-09-performance-day.md) M2, from E-338's logs): the forks' `write` step 0.121–0.157 s in every arm against 0.505–0.515 s on the head (E-337), since the REPL's display of the returned path, `show(::IOContext{LimitIO{TTYTerminal}}, ::MIME"text/plain", ::String)`, 134.6 ms on the head, is no longer compiled per session. The arm removes a class (PE-25's REPL half: a value's display at the REPL's stream type), not one package's case; W-126's gate for the forks' steps (≤ 1 s) is met on the arm (0.87 s) and not on the head (1.24 s).   - *What it changes:* `show` at the REPL receives `IOContext{IO}`, not `IOContext{LimitIO{TTYTerminal}}`: a method that dispatches on the REPL's stream type no longer sees it. Its properties (`:limit`, `:color`, `:displaysize`) and the stream's interface pass through.   - *Proposals:* (a) adopt it, with Base's `TextDisplay` erasing too, and the ports' display entries back to plain code with the workload line; (b) adopt it, with a convention that makes a package's display workload run at the erased type without the line (a helper in Base, `Base.precompile_display(x)`, that displays at `IOContext{IO}(IOBuffer())`); (c) keep erasing at each port's entries (E-337).   - *Recommendation (mine):* (a), then (b) if more than the forks need the line: the erasure moves to the one place every display passes, and a port's code stays plain.

Closed on 2026-10-29:

136. **Closed (2026-10-29, the user: "Okay, given the evidence, we pursue (e). The exploratory note had an everything from 4 components framing. I think the evidence backs this up now. Sealed AbstractTypes make sense but are an optimisation for later (the spelling of this will be tricky)"):** option (e) is the direction of level 2 of unification, one dispatch ([compiler §7.1](../attic/writeup/reference/compiler.md#71-memberships-and-dispatch), Planned; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §5). A negative membership is never folded, in inference or in dispatch. A call that a later membership could change carries a guard; in the ports' sessions every guard is where plain code calls trait code (E-259). The guard reads a cell per (type, set) in the caller's world. Base's abstract types are closed; Core's kinds are never members. Sealed membership is deferred as an optimisation ([PE-19](record/performance-explorations.md)). Next: the unified build, W-97 to W-104 ([the synthesis plan](plans/2026-11-05-unification-synthesis.md), 2026-11-06). Options (a) to (d): [`design/alternatives.md`](design/alternatives.md), "Level 2 of unification: options not taken". *As it stood:* **Level 2 of unification after U6: may a membership invalidate compiled code?** (W-83, E-252; [the plan](plans/2026-10-29-unification-evidence.md#6-results-of-the-quick-runs) §6; [the exploration](explorations/abstract-types-as-traits.md) §11.1, §11.2 C3/C4, §16). It decides gate A for level 2, one dispatch. No reference states level 2 yet.
    - **The case** (U6, E-252, every precompile mode; a plain method stands in for the membership `Ty ∈ X`):
      - R6: the member's own package `Sp` compiles code at `Ty` before the set's extension that `Sp` triggers (`XsSpExt`) loads;
      - R7: `Sp` compiles before its own extension `SpXsExt`, though the trigger `Xs` is in its tree;
      - R8: an extension (`FoSpExt`, triggers `{Fo, Sp}`) compiles before `SpXsExt` (`{Sp, Xs}`); without images the outcome depends on the load order;
      - R12: an image written before an extension existed is reused, and the extension loads after the package.
      - In each, the late definition invalidated 3 MethodInstances. In R6–R8 the bounded method is `f`'s owner's in its package, so C2 repairs none.
      - The loader (julia-1.13 00dcc9994c): a package's triggers are inserted after its own load (`base/loading.jl:2615-2631`) and never in its precompile child (`:3226-3229`); an extension's precompile child loads only extensions whose trigger set is a strict subset of its own (`:3306-3308`, `base/precompilation.jl:843-846`); an image lists the modules loaded while it was written (`src/staticdata.c:3400`), and a newly applicable extension does not make it stale.
    - **The rule it breaks:** Lemma M (exploration §11.2, C4; its premise §11.1): a membership by the member's owner, or a method by the function's owner, loads before any code at the member's signatures compiles, so only the combination C3 refuses can win at a compiled fact. What the loader guarantees instead: an extension is loaded before `P`'s code compiles when `P` is a package (not an extension), neither the extension's parent nor one of its triggers, with all its triggers in its tree; when `P` is an extension, only extensions with a strictly smaller trigger set; for an image, only as of the environment it was written in. So memberships that C3 and C4 allow can win at compiled facts.
    - **Options:**
      - (a) **Memberships are definitions in full:** dispatch facts with method-like invalidation. A membership invalidates what a method at the member's signatures would; image edges are verified against memberships at load. The coherence rule decides what is a pirate; G2 holds as for methods. Cost: "an implementation invalidates nothing" ([design.md](design.md), [facts §2.2](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#g2), [glossary](glossary.md)) weakens to "an implementation invalidates only what a stakeholder's method would"; memberships need edges in Julia (exploration §13.3, candidate (b)).
      - (b) **No membership in a package extension;** memberships only in a package proper or at a type's definition. Every failing arrangement of U6 (R6, R7, R8, R12) declares the membership in an extension, so (b) excludes them; it also excludes the passing ones (R1–R5, R10), whose membership is in an extension too. What remains is a membership in a package proper: the member's owner's, in the package every caller at its signatures depends on, or the set's owner's, which C3 governs (R9, R9b). Cost: the 20 memberships in extensions that E-253 counts (the CSV fork's 18 `InlineString` columns in `CSVInlineStringsExt`, TraitCSV's `String15` in `TraitCSVInlineStringsExt`, Tables' `PooledVector` in `TablesPooledArraysExt`), Tables' `CategoricalVector` in `TablesCategoricalArraysExt`, and the pattern of [traits §10.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#101-who-may-implement) ("an implementation for a foreign trait and a foreign type belongs in the trait owner's package extension"), which would need a dependency or a newtype (C8).
      - (c) **Memberships in packages are dispatch facts; memberships declared in extensions stay late:** decided at run time, never folded negatively in dispatch. Cost: two kinds of membership again, for extensions only.
      - (d) **Today's two tiers for dispatch** (an entry at `Any`, membership decided at run time), and level 1 only. Cost: level 2's simplifications stay out of reach (lowering L-3, L-47, L-50; open issue 135).
      - (e) **Never fold a negative membership in dispatch either:** §13.4's rule applied to dispatch, as U7 supports it for `isa` (E-255).
        - A compiled call `f(s::S)` may fold a positive membership: memberships only grow.
        - Where a method of `f` on an open set `Z` that `S` is not (yet) a member of would beat the statically selected method `m₀`, the call carries a guard: a membership read in the caller's world, then `f`'s bounded method, else `m₀`.
        - **The registry is a cell per (type, set),** holding the membership's `min_world`, fixed when the guard compiles. The guard is a load and a compare against the caller's world, which LLVM hoists out of a loop. It is never a lookup in a table (E-259).
        - No membership invalidates anything. The coherence rule is not needed for soundness, Lemma M not at all, and "an implementation invalidates nothing" keeps holding. Ownership ([traits §10.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#101-who-may-implement), pirates, strict mode) still decides who may declare a membership, as a matter of meaning, as today.
        - Negatives stay folded where nothing can join late: memberships declared with a type's definition (`struct S <: X`, so every abstract type as today: no guards in plain Julia), sets declared `closed`, Core's kinds (E-255).
        - **Base's abstract types are closed:** their members come only from `struct S <: A`. The open sets are the traits, BaseTraits' among them (E-259).
        - C7's tie order (a type's own supertypes first) means a later membership never beats a method on the type's own supertypes. So guards arise only where `m₀` is a fallback: `f(::Any)`, a method on a less specific set, or a `MethodError`.
        - Method additions stay under Julia's ordinary invalidation. This is today's two-tier guarantee (the entry at `Any` decides membership at run time) inside Julia's one dispatch.
        - Cost: a guard at those call sites; the guarded branch is a run-time site whose result is the bounded method's declared result, or `Any`, joined with `m₀`'s. Today's lowering asserts a bounded function's declared result on its untyped fallback too ([traits T-95](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#17-deviations)), so this join is a change from today.
        - *A note, not a rule:* a capability predicate written as dispatch (`f(::X) = true; f(x) = false`, as `is_nonlinear_algorithm` and [traits §5.5](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#55-dispatch-types-first-then-traits)'s `has_mass`) is a membership test. Under (e) its guard is a run-time membership read, which is what the predicate means. The references have no word for this pattern.
    - **The user (2026-10-29), on (e):** "Yes. This is the version we want to investigate."
    - **U9's result (E-259, [`experiments/w83/u9/README.md`](../experiments/w83/u9/README.md)): (e) stands.**
      - 7 guard sites over five sessions, 3 distinct (TDE's, TraitMakie's, TraitReport's, the forks', NetworkDynamics' on the ports). All at the plain-to-trait boundary. None in a loop, none inside bounded code: there an argument's membership is positive by construction.
      - 3 fall on a `MethodError` path: `add_lines!` at a `Vector{Float64}` for `Positions`. A path that throws costs nothing.
      - 4 fall on a real fallback: the capability predicate `is_nonlinear_algorithm(alg::NonlinearAlgorithm) = true; is_nonlinear_algorithm(alg) = false`, at `Tsit5` and `Rodas5`. Its result widens to `Bool ⊔ Any`, where today's inferred result is `Any` already. A declared `::Bool` on the bounded method removes the widening.
      - The sealed-membership variant folds 4 of the 7: a set whose memberships are all declared at the member type's definition or in the member's own package proper folds its negatives. The 3 left are `add_lines!` at `Positions`, an open set that takes Base's vectors.
      - The guard's cost ([evidence §2](design/evidence.md#2-run-time)): a cell per (type, set) holding the `min_world` has no measurable cost, hoisted out of loops (+0.2 to +0.6 ns per call where it cannot be). A table lookup costs 27–28 ns per call. A widened result in a loop costs 24–25 ns and 32 B per call; the ports have no such site.
      - The conditions (e) rests on, stated in it above: (i) Base's abstract types stay closed. Open, 1,326–4,619 specializations per session would carry guards (`axes`, `eltype`, `hash`, `==`, `broadcasted`), 173–1,122 where a package could declare the membership. (ii) The registry is a cell per (type, set), fixed when the guard compiles.
    - **Proposal (mine, the main session's recommendation): (e),** with conditions (i) and (ii). **The user decides:** adopting (e) for level 2 (gate A); and whether sealed membership is adopted as an optimization license (it folds 4 of U9's 7 guard sites; open sets that take Base's types keep their guards). **(a) is the alternative should guards appear in hot code that a declared result does not cover:** C4 already calls a membership a definition, and G2 already lets a stakeholder's definition invalidate ([facts §2.2](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#g2): "a stakeholder's package extension" is a stated exception). Under (a) the cost is the weaker slogan; a stakeholder's membership in an extension invalidates what a method in the same extension at the member's signatures would (3 MethodInstances in each of U6's failures).
    - **Level 1 does not depend on the answer:** membership in subtyping with negatives never folded in inference (E-254, E-255) holds under each option.

Closed on 2026-10-28:

97. **Closed (2026-10-28, the user: "This type of Base Trait extension counts as the principled solution by extending capabilities. Please implement the four TraitBase features you mentioned as soon as your current work on TraitMakie is done."):** option (d), property access as a BaseTraits trait, `HasProperty{N, R}` ([traits §8.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#81-basetraits-traits-for-bases-concepts); built, TraitWorld 2215188). *As it stood:* **A computed property on a value of the trait world** (SciML's `integ.ps[:σ]`; found by move 4, E-160). *The case:* SciML's users write `integ.ps[:σ]` and `integ[:x]` in callbacks. A callback receives the integrator as `Dynamic{IntegratorView{U, T, P}}`. *The rule that blocks it:* computed properties are not declared fields ([traits §3.3](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#33-fields)), and a kept value's `getproperty` reads declared fields only. *What works today:* `getparam(integ, :σ)` and `getstate(integ, :x)`, getters written once against `IndexProvider` and `ValueProvider`, reached through the traits the integrator's object conforms to ([§6.5](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#65-values-carry)). The options:
    - (a) **property syntax for operations:** a trait declares `@property ps(i::X)::R`, an operation read as `x.ps` on trait objects and kept values. It is never a stored field, so it is neither written nor mapped.
    - (b) **functions only:** `getparam(integ, :σ)`; the sugar stays on concrete types, in plain modules (move 4's principle).
    - (c) **a trait's owner adds Base methods for its kept forms** (`Base.getproperty(::Dynamic{IntegratorView}, …)`), overriding TraitWorld's field access for that trait.
    - (d) **property access as a trait of BaseTraits** (the user's question, 2026-10-28: "Should field access be available as a trait on Trait base?"): a BaseTraits trait whose operation is Base's own `getproperty` at a name (`HasProperty{:ps, R}`, read as `getproperty(x, ::Val{:ps})::R`); a trait that wants `x.ps` names it as a supertrait or bound; a trait object or kept value reading a name it has not declared as a field calls that operation. Declared fields stay the language's: stored data, read and written at their offsets, `@const`, keys compiled for the layout.

   *The recommendation was (d), over (a).* It is (a) without new syntax: Base's function stays the one property function, as `Indexable` uses `getindex`, and a computed property is an ordinary operation, checked at `@implement`, its result declared, compiled per key or erased by the attribute. No representation is computed or stored, so the decision of 2026-10-16 against computed projections stands; it touches it closely enough to be yours. To check: the name reaches the operation as a constant from a plain caller (facts §6.3), so `integ.ps` stays typed. (c) breaks the rule that a kept value's properties are its declared fields.

96. **Closed (2026-10-28, mine, by the user's rule of the day; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §1; built, E-214):** a struct parameter named only as a parameter of a bound is read from the kept value ([traits §7.4](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#74-a-struct-parameter-bounded-by-a-trait)). *As it stood:* **A view of a trait object as a Base type** (the DataFrames port, D3). The case: the kernels hand a column's cells to plain code that reads a vector (upstream's `groupreduce`, Statistics). Trait code may not pass the trait object bare, so the port copied the cells into a `Vector{T}`: 2× upstream's time for the aggregates on 10⁶ rows. The view `struct CellVector{T, C <: Column{T}} <: AbstractVector{T}; c::C; end` reads in place, but §7.4 refused it: "Every other parameter is named by a field outside a bound". The proposal: such a parameter is read from the kept value, as a `where` variable in a bound is.

94. **Closed (2026-10-28, mine, by the user's rule of the day; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §1; flagged: after a probe (W-80)):** A position whose attribute is `Dynamic` has a known key for compiling ahead, the erased one (traits §13). *As it stood:* **Keys of positions that default to `Dynamic`, for compiling ahead** (SciML weak point 5). [Traits §13](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#13-compiling-ahead) treats another trait position as unknown, so compiling ahead never reaches the solver's driver; W-60's memo needs workloads for it. Proposal: a position whose bound defaults to `Dynamic` has a known key, the erased one, so compiling ahead covers it. **My recommendation: state it so,** after a probe.

93. **Closed (2026-10-28, mine, by the user's rule of the day; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §1; E-158: 270–280 ns against 12.7 ns for `Dynamic`):** A kept `Compiled` function is called through an entry compiled for its type (traits §7.5; W-17). *As it stood:* **A kept `Compiled` function called through an entry compiled for its type** (SciML weak point 4). [Traits §7.5](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#75-functions-kept) dispatches a kept `Compiled` function at run time; E-158 measured `compiled_for` callbacks at 270–280 ns against 12.7 ns for `Dynamic`. A slowdown where trait code should be faster (the rule for performance work). Proposal: call it through an entry compiled for its type. **My recommendation: take it up,** as a design statement and in W-17.

Proposal: check each member at its first use, as a family's members are checked; then a live source (`Live{T}`, an `Observable{T}`) conforms by forwarding, which closes §2 item 13 without a new construct. **My recommendation: per-member checking.**

84. **Closed (2026-10-28, mine, by the user's rule of the day; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §1):** A `Dynamic` annotation may fix a parameter by a `where` variable, `c::Dynamic{Column{T}} where {T}`, compiled once per `T` (traits §5.1). *As it stood:* **Leaving an associated parameter open at a `Dynamic` position** (DataFrames weak point 1). A kernel over stored columns of any cell type wants `c::Dynamic{Column{T}} where {T}`: compiled per `T`, read from the value. Blocked by [traits §5.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#51-bounds): a `Dynamic` annotation must fix every parameter its operations' result types use.
