81. **Closed (2026-10-28, mine, by the user's rule of the day; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §1; flagged: needs a module-end hook in the patched Julia; if that is not feasible, a Revise extension instead):** A trait module as a plain module that declares itself one (`module M; @trait_module; … end`), its later statements those of the trait module, its end where Julia finishes the module; `@trait_module module M … end` stays a spelling (traits §2; T-81). *As it stood:* **Revise and trait modules** (found by W-78, 2026-10-28; traits T-81). Revise tracks a file by its top-level statements. It cannot load a file whose top level is `@trait_module module … end` ("module expression not at top level"), and it sees a `@trait_module begin … end` block as one statement: an edit of a bounded function inside it does not take effect (`experiments/w78/`). Options:
   - (a) **a trait module as a plain module that declares itself one:** `module M; @trait_module; … end`, with every later statement evaluated into it transformed by TraitWorld's front before Julia's lowering (built for W-78), and the module's end run where Julia finishes the module. Revise then sees, and re-evaluates, the statements one by one. `@trait_module module M … end` can stay as a spelling of the same. It needs a place where Julia tells the library that a module's body is evaluated (a small Julia change, or the package callback for a package's top module);
   - (b) **a Revise extension in TraitWorld** that hands Revise the statements of a `@trait_module` block one by one;
   - (c) leave it: edits by `eval` work; a file edit needs a restart.

   **My recommendation: (a).** It makes a trait module an ordinary module to every tool that reads files (Revise, a language server, `include` with its own transform), and it uses the mechanism W-78 built. It changes the surface syntax, so it is yours to decide.

95. **Closed (2026-10-28, the user: "Pause the store, move for (b) when we pick it up again."):** the store is paused; when it is taken up again, its vehicle is machine code keyed on facts, relinked by stable names at load (option (b)). *As it stood:* **The facts-keyed cache's vehicle: inferred code or machine code** (W-66; E-205, 2026-10-28). The store's revised note keeps optimized IR, so that an entry can be reused across environments; code generation stays in the session. E-205 measured the ceiling on the current build: in C off its workload, the entries the store would admit cost 1.5 s of inference and optimization, in a 14.5 s session with 10.7 s of compilation, of which code generation is 7.56 s (facts code 6.25 s); in D, 0.02 s. The store's own costs (reading, relinking, validation) come out of the 1.5 s. The environment-keyed cache (E-180, retired 2026-10-27 for this direction) kept machine code and took C off its workload from 19.4 to 6.1 s. Options:
   - (a) build the IR store as revised: reuse across environments, a ceiling of about 1.5 s in C and nothing in idiomatic trait code;
   - (b) a store of machine code keyed on facts: Julia loads machine code only from package images, which link to their dependencies by build id, the cascade E-194 measured; storing machine code per facts key needs a Julia change, native code relinked by stable names at load, as the IR store's reader relinks IR;
   - (c) two layers: the environment-keyed machine-code cache (E-180) for an unchanged environment, and the facts-keyed store across environments;
   - (d) pause the store: keep what W-66 built (fingerprints, strict mode on facts code, images that keep only running owners, the checks' records), record the bound, and take first calls up through W-7.

   **My recommendation: (d) now, with a short feasibility study of (b)** (how package images link native code, and what relinking by stable names would take in Julia's loader) before anything is built. The measured ceiling of (a) is small against its complexity (the rule for performance work), and machine code is where the time goes; (b) is the principled form of the user's direction, if Julia's loader can carry it. Yours to decide: it revisits the direction of 2026-10-27.

Making the trait/type system more expressive is a principled change."):** option (a): a `Callable`'s argument types may follow the other arguments ([traits §5.8](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#58-callable-functions-at-a-declared-signature); W-81). *As it stood:* **A `Callable` at signatures that follow another value's parameters** (SciML weak point 1; DataFrames weak point 3; related: §2 item 10). Cases: one right-hand side at plain, dual and nested-dual element types and any chunk (TraitDifferentialEquations keeps two bounded parameters and marker functions); `retain!(f::Callable{Tuple{T}, Bool}, c)` for any item type; `ByRow(f)` at a column's cell type. Blocked by [traits §5.8](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#58-callable-functions-at-a-declared-signature) ("the argument types `A` are a constant type") and §8.4. Proposal: a `Callable` bound may name the method's `where` variables or the other bounds' parameters; the entry enters the other arguments first, then the function at that signature, one entry per signature at its first call (TraitWorld's `_erased_at` does this today, T-54). Options: (a) allow it; (b) keep one bounded parameter per signature, with tuples of `Callable`s (W-29, W-30). **My recommendation: (a)**: two ports need it independently, and the mechanism exists; W-31 would remove that mechanism and waits.

82. **Closed (2026-10-28, the user: "82 go with a) 83 we want that, go for it. Making the trait/type system more expressive is a principled change."):** option (a): a kept value is seen through every trait its held object conforms to, checked at the bound ([traits §6.5](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#65-values-carry); T-53, W-81). *As it stood:* **Selecting a capability of a stored value** (found by W-79's port design and [its review](reviews/2026-10-28-dataframes-lean-core.md), B1; the user, 2026-10-28: "Remember that one purpose of the ports is to identify weak points in the design. If the design prevents capability selection by subtraits then that might qualify. On the other hand, capabilities might be cleaner to express by a column conforming to multiple traits, without a subtrait relationship."). A frame stores its columns as `Vector{Dynamic{Column}}`. Some columns are pooled, most are not. A kernel wants the pooled path where the column has a pool: `groupcodes(c::(Column, PooledColumn))` beside `groupcodes(c::Column)`, with `PooledColumn` a trait of its own, not a subtrait. For a plain value, dispatch selects by conformance today ([traits §5.5](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#55-dispatch-types-first-then-traits)). For a stored value it cannot: a value is seen through the traits it entered with ([traits §6.5](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#65-values-carry); decisions §1, 2026-10-21), and a tuple of traits cannot enter a container whose items conform to different sets. So the capabilities of heterogeneous stored values are not selectable. The only route today is an operation with a default on the stored trait (Rust's idiom, `fn as_pooled(&self) -> Option<&dyn Pooled>`), which closes the set of capabilities to what the trait's owner declares. Options:
   - (a) **a kept value is seen through every trait its held object conforms to, checked at the bound:** dispatch among bounds then selects the most capable method, as for plain values. For kept values that hold the user's whole object only; a trait object inside bounded code stays as it entered. T-53's behaviour becomes the design, with `TraitBoundError` where the object does not conform; W-31 changes to that;
   - (b) **an explicit, checked trait cast** ([traits §6.9](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#69-trait-casts), Optional today; the user, 2026-10-21: "I think we should allow such casts, and check on casting"): `conforms(c, PooledColumn)` on a kept value tests its held object, and `Kept{PooledColumn}(c)` sees it through the other trait, refused if it does not conform. A kernel branches, as Swift's `as?` to a protocol does;
   - (c) leave it: capabilities as operations with defaults on the stored trait; a third package cannot add a capability.

   **My recommendation: (a),** with (b) as its spelled form where code wants to test first. Selecting a method by what a value can do is Julia's dispatch; (a) gives it to stored values without a new construct, and keeps the cost where `Dynamic` already pays it (a conformance check over the candidates at the entry). It reverses part of the 2026-10-21 decision, so it is yours to decide. **The fresh-ideas round (below) found it in every port:** SciML's callbacks need integrator capabilities only some integrators provide (`get_tmp_cache`, `get_du`); Makie's legend, data inspector and a backend's native drawing are capabilities owned by third packages (`legend.jl:733–898`, `inspector.jl:782`, CairoMakie's `overrides.jl:12–33`), where option (c) cannot work; DataFrames' GPU, lazy and sorted columns. All three agents support (a), with the conformance check cached per held type.

79. **Closed (2026-10-28, the user: "Accept both suggestions."):** option (b). A bounded function defined again after its trait module is complete goes through the module's end for that function: its worker replaced, its checks run, the facts the end fixes per position recomputed; callers are invalidated as after any redefinition by an owner ([traits §2](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#2-trait-modules); T-81 until built, W-78). *As it stood:* **A bounded function redefined after its trait module is complete** (found by E-202 (c), 2026-10-28). The references say what a redeclared implementation does ([traits §4](reference/traits.md), "Redeclaration") but not what a redefinition of a bounded function does once its trait module is complete, by `eval` at the prompt or by Revise. Today `Core.eval(M, :(sq(v::Values) = …))` is accepted: it defines a method at the trait type, and the entry keeps calling the worker the module's end generated, so the call still gives the old result, silently (`experiments/w66/e202/edit_case.jl`). Options:
   - (a) refuse such a definition, with a message naming the way to redefine (re-evaluating the trait module);
   - (b) lower it as the module's end lowers a bounded function: the worker is replaced, the definition check runs, and the first-call checks' records for that worker are reset (the facts-first review §2.4);
   - (c) leave it, as a deviation.
   
   **My recommendation: (b),** since Revise-style editing is how Julia users work, and the checked-record of W-66 (§2.4) then has a clear rule for edits. Until decided, the edit case of §2.4's check is open.
80. **Closed (2026-10-28, the user: "Accept both suggestions."):** option (b). A stored entry is valid when Julia's validation of loaded code holds for its edges in the session; fingerprints cover only content: the lowered code of its method and of the methods its edges name, the layouts it reads, the values its optimized code embeds ([facts §11](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#11-package-images); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §5). *As it stood:* **How the facts-keyed cache validates a stored entry** (W-66; [the store's review](reviews/2026-10-28-facts-cache-store.md), M1). Decided on 2026-10-27: fingerprints scoped by the facts rule (F1, F2 per fact the stakeholders' methods of the fact's function, F3) plus a candidate index ([decisions](record/decisions.md) §5). The review shows that Julia's own validation of loaded code (`verify_method_graph`, `Compiler/src/reinfer.jl`) already checks every edge's dispatch exactly in the session's world, recursive groups as one unit, with folds, bindings, `invoke` signatures and deleted methods, and hands the code to ordinary invalidation. Options:
   - (a) keep the decided form: F1–F3 and the candidate index decide validity (E-195);
   - (b) validate a stored entry's edges with Julia's validation, and use fingerprints only for content that edges do not cover: F1 of the methods the edges name, F3, and the values the optimized code embeds (the review's B2). F2 and the candidate index are not needed for validity.
   
   **My recommendation: (b).** It reuses Julia's exact check (the fast path covers most facts edges), drops the costliest fingerprint, and answers cycles. E-195's results stay as evidence for the content fingerprints. Either way, an experiment by construction (the review's E-0) comes before the store is built.

Closed by the clean-up of 2026-10-27 (W-65; [the proposal](plans/2026-10-27-cleanup-issues-and-queue.md); the user: "Take up strict mode with W-66, confirm W-70 and flag the 8 worth a look for a quick review session later (add that as a Todo), then implement the clean up."):

75. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; mine, adopted by the clean-up rule):** W-60's answer: heavy features go behind package extensions for keys and behind loading stubs for functions, so `hist(x)` stays as upstream calls it; each lean measurement names the features it drops (option (i)); loading implementations on demand (option (ii)) stays an exploration ([`explorations/load-on-demand.md`](explorations/load-on-demand.md)). No reference changes. E-182: a stub's load invalidates none of the core's code. [decisions](record/decisions.md) §8; [the porting guide](intro/porting.md). *As it stood:* **A lean tree against upstream's API** ([the direction review](reviews/2026-10-27-w58-direction.md) 2; W-58). The load follows the dependency tree (E-176: CairoMakie 2.7 s, 261 packages), and a lean plotting core loads its dependencies in 0.5–0.7 s. But Makie's own recipes use the heavy packages: `hist`, `density`, `boxplot`, `violin`, `ecdf` (StatsBase, KernelDensity, Distributions), units (Unitful). Moving them to extensions changes how they are called (`hist(x)` would need `using StatsBase` first), which the user's "called like the upstream packages are" excludes. **Options:** (i) the lean port drops or moves these features, and says so; (ii) the design gains loading on demand: an implementation of a trait loaded at its key's first use, so the API stays and the tree loads lazily. The user (2026-10-27): "I think SciML Problem and Algorithm are more tolerable to require a change by library authors. On everything else I basically agree." (W-60's architect works under it.) **Recommendation:** (i) for the measurements now (each lean measurement states the features it drops), (ii) as the design question it raises: [`explorations/load-on-demand.md`](explorations/load-on-demand.md) sketches it (a trait's declared signature and result let callers compile before the implementation is loaded; providers registered by key, loaded at the key's first call). **W-60's memo (revision 2, §1.4)** answers with extensions for keys and loading stubs for functions, and names the key extensions miss: a `LaTeXString` label, whose package is light and loaded (Makie re-exports `L"…"`) while its implementation needs MathTeXEngine; a loading stub reached through a `Dynamic{Callable}` covers it.
76. **Closed (2026-10-27, the user: the environment-keyed cache retired):** moot; what carries over is W-66's ([decisions](record/decisions.md) §5). *As it stood:* **The cache across sessions: its shape** ([the cache's design review](reviews/2026-10-27-cache-design.md) §8; W-62, open issue 72). Built as an experiment (E-180); these choices are the user's: **Moot (2026-10-27): the environment-keyed cache is retired (the user); questions that carry over (strict mode's refusals with a cache, the checks kept, budgets, the vehicle) are W-66's.**
    1. Adopt it into the design now (Planned), or keep it an experiment until the load (open issue 73) has a lever? It adds to the load by construction (C: 45 parts, 0.45 s). **Decided (2026-10-27, the user: "Keep the cache experimental").**
    2. Its lifetime: an empty cache at every change of the environment (as built), or one that survives changes (the store replayed into the new environment, or parts keyed by their own dependencies and shared across environments)?
    3. Strict mode with a cache (the earlier review's M1): is cached code evidence (a pirate that breaks it discards it, no error) or a contract (refusals depend on what earlier sessions compiled)?
    4. First-call checks: may a check that warned be kept, so that its warning shows once per environment? Does a stricter fully-typed setting in a later session check the cached signatures again?
    5. Budgets and the default: the load (C 0.45 s), the disk per environment (C about 96 MB), the builder's time (C 174 s the first time); on by default or opt-in.
    6. Where it lives: in TraitWorld, or as a general mechanism (in Julia or its own package) with trait hooks.
    7. Whether to invest in a Julia change for the vehicle (a sidecar image Julia manages), which would share work with open issue 73's loader levers.
    My recommendation: keep it an experiment (1); coarser parts, eviction and a trigger for stale parts are choices an agent makes and records (W-62).
77. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; settled by E-182):** option (a): user-facing entries that can reach code loaded on demand (render, `save`, `display`) call the core through `invokelatest`. E-182 shows it works at no measurable cost, and that world age hits plain code alike: it is Julia's rule. Option (b) is not taken ([alternatives](design/alternatives.md)). [decisions](record/decisions.md) §8; [the porting guide](intro/porting.md). *As it stood:* **World age at loads on demand** ([the memo's review](reviews/2026-10-27-lean-core-memo.md) B1; W-60). A call runs in the world it started in ([facts §2.1](reference/facts.md#21-g1-identical-or-error)): in `function f(x); hist(x); save("h.png", current_figure()); end`, code that `hist` loads on demand is invisible to `save`. Python has no world age, so "called like upstream" meets a Julia property. **Options:** (a) every user-facing entry that can reach code loaded on demand (render, `save`, `display`) calls into the core through `invokelatest`: upstream's calls kept, one dynamic call per entry; (b) an on-demand load below top level raises an error that names the `using`: the API changes. **Recommendation:** (a) for Makie, and SciML loads no code on demand that a returned value needs (the memo, §1.3–§1.4); X7 (i) tests it. *(E-182: (a) works, at no measurable cost per `save`; world age hits plain code alike.)*

The keyword case is T-39. [decisions](record/decisions.md) §3. *As it stood:* **The implicit crossing's finer rules** (built by the BaseTraits agent, TraitWorld branch `basetraits` 8799aea, 9fd18db; [traits §8.2–8.3](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#83-a-users-container-taken-as-it-is), [lowering §13.1](reference/lowering.md)). To confirm:
    - a vector of a concrete type that conforms crosses too (`Vector{Circle}` into `Iterable{Shape}`), through `CompiledItems`: its items are `Compiled{Shape}` ([traits §8.2](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#82-item-positions));
    - a container whose item type cannot hold a conforming value (`Vector{Float64}` into `Iterable{Shape}`, `Dynamic` items of another trait) is refused at the call, not at the first read;
    - a bound the container meets as it is wins over crossing (`f(xs::Iterable{Int})` beside `f(xs::Iterable{Cell})`: a `Vector{Int}` takes the first);
    - a bounded keyword does not cross (T-39, with a bug that predates it: a keyword bound that fixes a parameter fails with `MethodError`);
    - an untyped caller at a crossing position passes the container as `Dynamic` ([traits §6](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#6-values-in-trait-code-compiled-and-dynamic));
    - `DynamicItems` and `DynamicValues` stay exported as explicit tools (the copy that checks every item first, and code that is not bounded).

Why: [the note](plans/2026-10-26-w40-member-builder-fold.md) §5. [lowering §4.6](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#46-families); compiler §4.4 (Planned). 63. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; mine, confirmed by the clean-up rule):** principled by load order; T-28 is the gap ([decisions](record/decisions.md) §2). *As it stood:* **An override of a family member is accepted only in its parameter type's owner's own package, not in its extension** (the W-40 note, question 3; traits review M11; the W-40 review's B4): any code that names `S{MyT}` when it is compiled depends on that package, so the override is loaded first in every load order; an extension loads after its triggers, so it is excluded.
64. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; mine, adopted by the clean-up rule):** where an operation's declared argument type is abstract, the member check counts only the stakeholders' methods there (the owners of the operation, of the type and of its parameters' types), so a member's implementation does not depend on load order ([traits §4.7](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#47-families); T-77; W-67, with the acceptance case of closed item §4b F3). *As it stood:* **The member check at abstract declared arguments** (the W-40 note, question 2): where an operation's declared argument type is abstract, the check asks only whether some method exists at `(U, declared types…)` (`conformance_problems`, `_has_op_method`). A method added there by the owner of any other type (`op(::U, ::MyVec)`) turns the operation from served by the default to served by the type's own method. So a family member's implementation depends on whether that package loaded before the member's first use, and a single type's on whether it loaded before the declaration. **Recommendation:** count only the methods of the type's stakeholders there (the owners of the operation, of the type and of its parameters' types).

34. **Closed (2026-10-20, the user):** the open questions of the `Dynamic`/`Compiled` implementation plan (Q1, Q3b, Q6, Q8, Q9, Q10), answered "go with your recommendations", with Q1 explained by the user and Q3/Q4 narrowed: [the plan §11](plans/2026-10-20-dynamic-compiled-implementation.md#11-questions-for-the-user); decision log, the rows of 2026-10-20.

Raised in session 26 (new trait code after big loads; analysis in session note 26 and §4.5); decided by the user in session 26: §1.3 the policy in the platform, as a prototype; §1.4 option (a), with (b) recorded as an alternative design and an experiment to run later (`decisions.md`):
3. **Closed (s29):** superseded. The facts policy is a mode of Julia's own compiler, parts (A)–(C) (julia-1.13 880ae49dad, c3415abde6). TraitWorld's own policy and its interpreter code are deleted (TraitWorld c533f3a). The checks use Julia's `NativeInterpreter`, run in the compiler's world (julia-1.13 bb82a82bbf, TraitWorld f79727c). So no package defines the interpreter that REPL's could break. Recorded in `design/julia-changes.md` §1–2. *(Prototype built in session 26, `experiments/sysimage`: TraitWorld in the patched system image. REPL first, a new key's first call 0.22 s instead of 5.9 s. TraitReport's work after OffsetArrays, StatsBase and Unitful 1.7 s instead of 5.4 s. Startup 0.125 s against 0.10 s. Open: the demonstrators' rows on it, and the cleaner form of the policy in Julia's Compiler.)* **Where the policy's compiler code lives.** Julia's Compiler keeps the interpreter in an abstract field (`InferenceState.interp::AbstractInterpreter`), so the compiler code TraitWorld's image holds for `FactsInterp` has abstract edges such as `cache_owner(::AbstractInterpreter)`. Any package that defines its own interpreter breaks them. REPL defines one (`REPLCompletions.REPLInterpreter`), and in an interactive session REPL loads before TraitWorld, so the image's compiler code fails validation and the first new key costs 5.9 s instead of 0.13 s. GPUCompiler, Enzyme, JET and Cthulhu define interpreters too. Julia's own compiler avoids this by running in `jl_typeinf_world`, frozen at the system image. **Recommendation:** the future state, with the facts policy as part of the platform, compiled into the system image and run in a frozen compiler world like Julia's own. A prototype is a system image of the patched build with TraitWorld in it, measured against the REPL case. A workaround, not recommended: TraitWorld depends on REPL, which fixes REPL but not the other interpreters.
4. **Closed (s29):** option (a) was dropped in session 27 (item 7). Option (b) is built as part (C): run-time sites in facts code run facts code (julia-1.13 c3415abde6). The machinery's slow paths are compiled by facts (`@machinery`, TraitWorld 624f742). A new model type's `@implement` now takes 142–164 ms in every load order (`design/evidence.md` §3). **Run-time sites in the machinery's code.** Under the policy, a call at abstract argument types dispatches at run time into *native* code. For the machinery's own internal functions (`_analyze_keeps`, `_implement_oneline`, …), that native code is invalidated by large loads, so `@implement` still takes 1.2–1.5 s after CSV, DataFrames and CairoMakie (0.64 s without). Options:
   - *(Adopted and implemented in session 26, scoped to calls within the caller's own package: TraitWorld 6bcca9f. `@implement` 0.09–0.21 s without loads, about 0.52 s after.)*
   - **(a) a rule refinement:** a call at abstract argument types of a function that is not `public` in its owner's module is a fact, since only the owner adds methods to its internal functions, and extending another package's non-public function counts as piracy. This uses Julia's own `public` declarations. Recommended: it reflects how an ecosystem with declared public APIs would work, and it is small.
   - **(b) run-time dispatch under the policy:** a run-time site looks up and invokes the policy's code for the run-time types (a Julia piece: dispatch under a cache owner). It would also protect ordinary code reached dynamically, Makie's for example, but it changes "run-time sites behave as plain Julia" and costs per call.

Raised in session 27, from the fresh-eye consistency review (`reviews/2026-10-17-consistency-fresh-eye.md`; the user asked for the review and for work to continue without questions, so these waited):
5. *(Decided in session 27: opt-in per trait, `@concrete_only` / `@warn_on_abstract`; implemented, decisions.md.)* **Concreteness: opt-in or required?** The notes and the register say opt-in per trait; the prototype requires it for every trait and checks every field of the implementing type, declared or not (review §1). Options: (a) required by default with a per-trait opt-out, matching the prototype and pay-off 3 as stated; (b) opt-in as written, then implemented, with the check narrowed to the key. Either way the sentence "a `DataFrame` cannot conform, since it is not deeply concrete" (TraitReport README, decisions "TraitTables and TraitCSV") states a prototype limit; session 27 measured the independent rationale: column code on `Vector{AbstractVector}` loses compiled code to loads (PlainTables loses 17 of 49 methods), `Vector{Dynamic{Column}}` loses none (TraitReport README, "Form (t)"). *Recommendation:* (b), since nothing in the facts proofs needs concreteness and abstract keys are sound as run-time sites; pay-off 3 then reads "for traits that require it".
6. *(Decided in session 27, the text corrected; decisions.md.)* **Stakeholders and Lemma O** (review §2): the notes define stakeholders so that methods on abstract types count as pirates; strict mode and the prototype use method-signature ownership. Restate Lemma O and Theorem 2 with supertype owners as stakeholders, and name the exception: package extensions triggered by unrelated packages.
7. *(Decided in session 27: dropped; acceptance 16/16; decisions.md.)* **Facts at non-public functions** (review §3; also item 3 of session 26's "Next"): the rule is in no note, breaks Theorem 2's premise as stated, and the scoping to the caller's package still records Base-internal calls from Base code as facts. Options: amend rule 3 (extending another package's non-public function is piracy) and exclude Julia's own modules from the rule; or confine the rule to TraitWorld's machinery. *Evidence (session 27):* acceptance check G2.1 fails with the rule on and passes with it off (`JULIA_TW_INTERNAL_FACTS=0`): a module that owns a type extends another module's non-public function called on type values, and the trait code is invalidated. Under the decision's amended rule 3 that module is a pirate, and the invalidation is the pirate being followed; under rule 3 as the notes state it, it is a G2 failure.
8. *(Decided in session 27: refused, the keys design; family members fixed at first use; restart for interactive changes; decisions.md.)* **Overrides after a conformance** (review §4): "refused" (4-details "Declaring a trait", the keys design, `check_overrides`) against "a fact that recompiles to the new method" (rule P, wrapping by facts). Options: drop the refusal under rule P, or keep it as a declared-API rule with a corrected justification.

The review's minor findings (stale lines, numbers that disagree, "the policy is package code" against the adopted "policy in the platform") are document fixes, listed in its "Minor" section and in §5.

The user first rejected the explicit view ("3. no … I think it is good that Base's containers conform"), then chose: **implicitly, by the bound** (a function bounded by `Iterable{Shape}` accepts a `Vector{Any}` directly; its entry translates it by its bound, so its items are seen as `Dynamic{Shape}`; no copy, writes reach the user's vector), and **an item that does not implement the trait fails when it is read** (as Swift checks a bridged `NSArray` of class elements). Base's containers conform through Base's own methods (v0). To build on TraitWorld's branch `basetraits`; [traits §8.3](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#83-a-users-container-taken-as-it-is).
### §2

158. **Closed (2026-11-11, the user's rule, W-140; `unified` a19d937eaa):** no call carries a guard, so a call's result is its selected method's ([compiler §5.5](reference/compiler.md#55-a-membership-invalidates-as-a-method-does); gates `n7_guards` (6), `n16_declared_result`). The two cases in upstream CSV and DataFrames are not measured again (W-140's rounds). What (a) built stays and is listed for the synthesis (open issue 178 (a)). *As it stood:* **A guarded call at a type with no method takes the trait's declared result** (found 2026-11-08 by W-104's second slice, CSV's upstream suite).
   - *The case:* CSV's `_rowopts(r, j) = r.colopts === nothing ? r.opts : @inbounds(r.colopts[j])` (`forks-unified/CSV/src/tables.jl:250`) reads the field again. So the second call is `getindex(::Union{Nothing, Vector{ValueOpts}}, ::Int)`.
     - Plain Julia finds no method for the `Nothing` arm and infers `ValueOpts`.
     - With BaseTraits loaded, `Nothing` could join `Indexable` and `Keyed`. Their `getindex` methods shadow the arm. The guarded call's result joins their declared results, `T` and `V`, which are unbound at `Nothing`: `Any`.
     - Each cell read then boxes a 128-byte `ValueOpts`: 144 bytes per cell. Upstream's test "Rows cell access allocations do not grow with row count" fails (6 of 330,545 tests; `forks-unified/logs/w104/suite-CSV.log`, unified 9dab7b6983). The code slot plays no part (the same with `JULIA_CODE_SLOT=0`). The values are correct.
   - *How common:* a `Union{Nothing, T}` that reaches one of BaseTraits' operations without narrowing (a field read again after its test, a result of `findfirst`, `tryparse` or `get(d, k, nothing)` used in both arms). Plain Julia prunes the `Nothing` arm; the unified build widens the call to `Any`.
   - *The rules that block it:*
     - the guarded call's result is the join of `m₀`'s and the shadowing method's declared result ([compiler §5.6](reference/compiler.md#56-cells));
     - a non-member is final only as a closed abstract type or with the `never_member` bit ([compiler §4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries)).
   - *What a required operation's method does:* the trait's method for an operation without a default either forwards ([traits §5.5](reference/traits.md#55-forwarding-with-by), and every `Dynamic{X}` value) or raises `MethodError` (`TraitWorld-unified/src/trait.jl`, `_trait_method`). The check accepts a member only with a serving method other than the trait's own, or a forwarding (`src/check.jl`). So at a type that does not forward, the method raises `MethodError`, as plain dispatch would without it.
   - *Proposals:*
     - (a) **Forwarding defines methods at the member** (or the family's pattern), as an `@implement` block does, and at `Dynamic{X}` for each operation when the trait is declared. Then the trait's method for a required operation only raises `MethodError` and is no shadow (one more "No guard arises" case in §5.6). A member's own method, forwarding included, is a method definition and invalidates as in Julia. Effect: the arm prunes as in plain Julia, and most of BaseTraits' guards go (all its operations are required). Defaults keep their guards. It revises P-1 for forwarding ([decisions](record/decisions.md) §9: defaults and forwarding are served by the trait's method).
     - (b) **Negative memberships:** the owner of a set or of a type declares a pair that never joins (BaseTraits: `Nothing` and `Missing` at its container traits). A final non-member by declaration (§4.8). Exact, but by hand and per pair.
     - (c) **A membership invalidates the code that pruned an arm with no method at the member,** as Julia does at a method definition. Changes the committed guarantee that a membership invalidates nothing ([facts §10](reference/facts.md#10-memberships); open issue 146).
   - *Recommendation:* (a), mine. It keeps the guarantee, needs no declaration, and leaves fewer guards for W-121's profile to find.
   - *A second case, through a method at a set* (DataFrames' suite, `test/tables.jl:86`): the fork's `DataFrame(x)` tests `typeof(src) <: TableSource`, which never folds to `false` (§4.8), so inference also infers `fromsource(src::DataFrameColumns, …)`. `DataFrameColumns` has no method there but could join `TableSource`; the shadow is `fromsource(t::TableSource, copycols::Bool)::DataFrame`, a method at the set, whose declared result `::_DF.DataFrame` lowering records ([compiler §6.8](reference/compiler.md#68-a-declared-result-on-method)). So the join is `DataFrame`, and upstream's `@inferred` passes (E-315). Proposal (a) does not cover this case: the shadow is real; (c) would prune the arm.
   - *With the forks' DataFrames compiled by facts* (E-305): four of upstream's `@inferred` tests see `Any` (`sort`, `dropmissing`, `first` with `view`, `Tables.subset`): the result passes a run-time site in facts code, typed `Any` ([facts §5](reference/facts.md#5-run-time-sites)). Lowering's record does not reach them; they need open issue 157 (a) (E-315).
   - The fork keeps upstream's code; the tests' failures stand, listed in the forks' README, until this is decided.
   - *Finality, decided opt-in* (the user, 2026-11-09): a trait such as the fork's `TableSource` can opt in with `@closed_after_load` ([traits §3.4](reference/traits.md#34-open-and-closed)), so that `typeof(src) <: TableSource` folds once the declarers have loaded (W-129).
   - **[The review of 2026-11-08](reviews/2026-11-08-issue-158-guarantee.md) (Fable, at the user's request):**
     - the widened result is intrinsic to a guard whose slow branch is a dynamic call; it bites only where the shadow's declared result names the set's parameters or is undeclared (H2);
     - **(a), sharpened:** a method at a set that can only raise at a member (a required operation's, once forwarding and `Dynamic{X}` have methods of their own) is no shadow; it removes every guard BaseTraits puts into plain code;
     - **(d), new: finality by ownership.** Only the trait's owner or the type's (and their joint owners and extensions) may declare `S <: X` ([traits §12.1](reference/traits.md#121-who-may-declare-a-membership)), while they load. Once all have finished loading, the negative is final: no guard, and `isa` and `<:` fold. It covers the DataFrames case, which (a) does not, and subsumes (b). **The narrowing:** a membership declared after its declarers loaded (`eval`, Revise, the REPL into a package's module) invalidates the code that folded its absence, as a late method definition does;
     - (c) not, unless the counter names a hot shape that (a) and (d) leave;
     - also: C-36 regardless (built: [compiler §6.8](reference/compiler.md#68-a-declared-result-on-method)); a guarded call's effects as the join of `m₀`'s and the shadow's (H3, W-124); a soundness gap at a method's insertion (H1, compiler C-73).

169. **Closed (2026-11-09, mine):** proposal (a) built: at a set closed after load, an intersection no type is in is a final negative with a cell (`AbstractString`, `IO`); inference folds `isa` there and drops a match met only there, and the code compiled there while Base is built is invalidated once Core and Base count as loaded ([compiler §4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries), [§5.4](reference/compiler.md#54-the-ambiguity-report-at-a-membership); C-79; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; `unified` 1906196731, E-334). Instances with valid code at an unmet intersection 58 → 0; the goal's imports 8.0 → 6.4 s. The load hooks still recompiled come through other chains (open issue 167's pruned arm; plain invalidations at `AbstractString`). *As it stood:* **Instances compiled at an intersection no type is in carry invalidation** (found 2026-11-09 by E-332's goal's task: the imports stayed at 8 s after the fan-in through splits was removed).
164. **Closed (2026-11-09, the user: "Go with the Fable plans recommendations"):** `IO` stays Julia's closed abstract type, with a contract: operations with declared results and the split's candidates. The erased stream is Base's `IOContext{IO}` (proposal (a), Base's eraser named as (c) names it), whose operations on its stream split. Erasing is the author's choice: a display entry declares `@nospecialize(io::IO)` and passes `IOContext{IO}(io)` on ([traits §3.4](reference/traits.md#34-open-and-closed), [§9b](reference/traits.md#9b-streams-io); [compiler §7.5](reference/compiler.md#75-io); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9; W-129; [the Fable plan](plans/2026-11-09-io-alternatives.md) §4.2). Proposal (d), built first and measured, is in [alternatives](design/alternatives.md). *As it stood:* **A value's type that plain code specializes on: no attribute erases it there** (found 2026-11-09 with E-318's traced session, the forks' `show` step at a REPL).
   - *The case:* DataFrames' `show(io, df)` passes the stream to PrettyTables, plain code compiled per stream type. At a REPL the stream is `IOContext{REPL.LimitIO{TTYTerminal}}`, a type the image's workload (an `IOBuffer`) did not see, so the step compiles DataFrames' keyword body and PrettyTables' code there: 0.12 s of the step's 0.18 s (E-318). Each new stream type (a file's, a notebook's) compiles them again.
   - *The rule that leaves it open:* the attribute chooses a compilation signature only at an argument typed by a set ([compiler §6.2](reference/compiler.md#62-compilation-signatures)); `IO` is a plain abstract type. `Dynamic{X}` erases a value's type, but it is a member of `X` only ([traits §7.7](reference/traits.md#77-dynamicx)), not an `IO`, so plain code typed `io::IO` cannot take it.
   - *Proposals:*
     - (a) The port erases with the plain type's own eraser, where Julia has one: for streams `IOContext{IO}`, one type for every stream. One compiled `show` then serves every stream, and the image's workload covers it. *Measured* (E-323, the forks' branch `e323`): the step 0.081 s against 0.175 s; a display after its first call within 9 % (0.25 ms at most); what the step still compiles is the REPL's display path at its stream (about 30 ms) and Base's code at the cells' types (about 50 ms).
     - (b) A workload at the REPL's stream, in an extension of the port on REPL. It covers the REPL only.
     - (c) In the design: a trait that models a plain abstract type names that type's eraser, and `Dynamic{X}` of such a trait is the eraser's value (for streams, `IOContext{IO}`), so the attribute `Dynamic` erases into plain code too.
     - (d) **`IO` a trait in Base, compiled once by default** (the user, 2026-11-09: "The real point here is that Base should support and default to Dynamic{IO} right?"). `IO` becomes an open set, as `Core.Callable` is ([compiler §7.4](reference/compiler.md#74-callables-call)), with `@default_dynamic`. This is (c) at its root: the plain abstract type is itself the trait.
       - *What it gives:* `Dynamic{IO}` is an `IO`, so plain code typed `io::IO` takes it. Every method typed `io::IO`, Base's and a plain package's alike, is compiled once by default, so an image's workload at an `IOBuffer` covers every stream: the REPL's, a file's, a notebook's. Code that writes to a stream in a hot loop declares `@specialize io::IO`, or its caller passes `compiled_for(io)`. (a) is then not needed.
       - *Step 4's exception* ([traits §7.5](reference/traits.md#75-what-decides)): `@default_dynamic` does not apply to a type that Julia defines with all its parameters. Every stream of Base and the standard libraries is such a type, the REPL's `IOContext{REPL.LimitIO{TTYTerminal}}` included, so as the rule stands the default does not reach this issue's case. The exception bounds the number of instances per type; here the problem is the instance an image lacks, not their number. A decision for (d): the exception does not apply to `IO`, or a trait's default says whether it covers Julia's types.
       - *Run time:* a method compiled once calls the stream's operations (`write(io, ::UInt8)`, `unsafe_write`, `lock`) through run-time sites, or through the split over `IO`'s first members ([compiler §7](reference/compiler.md#7-operations-on-a-value-typed-by-its-trait)). *Measured* by a proxy in plain Julia (E-328), against the stream's type known: through the split, formatted output and strings built 0.85–1.19×, a display and CSV's `write` 0.92–0.99×; one byte or one short string per call 1.6–2.2×, about 4 ns per call, where `@specialize io::IO` restores it. Without the split (erased as `IOContext{IO}`), 4.2–5.3× on such calls. So the split's candidates for `IO` must include `IOBuffer`.
       - *Negatives at an open set* ([compiler §4.8](reference/compiler.md#48-negatives-fold-with-an-edge-positives-through-entries)): `x isa IO` no longer folds for a type that is not a stream, and a call of `print`, `write` or `show` where a method at `::IO` would win, should the argument's type join `IO`, carries a guard. The flagged item's proposal (d), finality by ownership (§1, "A membership invalidates nothing"), makes these answers final: Julia owns `IO` and its own types, and a package's type is final once its package has loaded. So (d) here rests on that proposal.
       - *`IO`'s contract:* Julia's interface for streams is informal. The trait declares the operations `Dynamic{IO}` forwards: `unsafe_write`, `write(::IO, ::UInt8)`, `unsafe_read`, `read(::IO, ::Type{UInt8})`, `eof`, `isopen`, `close`, `flush`, `lock`, `unlock`, and `get(io, key, default)` and `haskey`, so that an `IOContext`'s properties (`:compact`, `:limit`, `:color`) reach `show`.
   - *Recommendation (mine):* (d), with the decisions it needs: step 4's exception not applied to `IO`; finality by ownership (the flagged item's proposal (d)); `IO`'s contract, with `IOBuffer` among its first four candidates. Its run-time cost through the split is small (E-328), except in a loop that writes one byte or one short string per call, which declares `@specialize io::IO`. (a) is not merged: it builds the port around the gap, and its eraser without the split costs 4.2–5.3× on such loops (E-328).

Closed by W-118 (2026-11-09): items that named the earlier design's mechanisms ([todo](record/todo.md) W-118).

135. **Closed (2026-11-09, W-118, moot under the current references, mine):** a method at a trait is a method: `S(x::X)` beside Julia's `S(x)` is the more specific and wins ([traits §6.2](reference/traits.md#62-specificity)). Nothing is taken out of the method table, so Julia's precompilation checks are not met. *As it stood:* **A package cannot take its own constructor out of the method table while Julia writes its image** (W-95, E-251; [lowering L-50](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#18-deviations)). A bounded method of a type's constructor at the signature of the type's default constructor (`S(x::Bounded)` beside Julia's `S(x)`) takes that constructor as its fallback ([lowering §8.1](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#81-the-rewrite)). In a session TraitWorld takes the constructor out of the method table and calls it by `invoke` with its method. In a package it cannot: while Julia writes a package image it refuses to delete a method (`Method deletion is not possible during Module precompile`, `julia-1.13/src/gf.c:2931`) and to overwrite one (`gf.c:2297`). So in a package such a bounded method is refused at its definition; the struct declares inner constructors with typed arguments instead. Blocked by Julia's precompilation checks, not by a rule of the references. *Proposal:* relax those two checks for methods whose module is in the worklist (the package being precompiled defined them in this process); the image writer already writes a disabled method entry as dead (`staticdata.c`). *Deferred (the user, 2026-10-29: "135 should wait."):* it waits for unification (W-83): under its level 2 a bounded constructor is a method at its bound, not at the default constructor's signature, so nothing has to be taken out ([the plan](plans/2026-10-29-unification-evidence.md) §5).

139. **Closed (2026-11-09, W-118, answered by the current references):** `ImplementError` names each missing operation with its declared signature at the type, and the type's methods of that function that do not cover it, with the argument that differs ([traits §11.3](reference/traits.md#113-errors-and-their-messages)). *As it stood:* **A refusal for a missing operation names neither the declared signature nor the near-miss method** (E-259, finding 6; TraitMakie 1af318c). The session's `@implement Plot Lollipop` was refused with "Lollipop has methods for some operations of Plot but not for setcolor!: define them", although `setcolor!(l::Lollipop, c::Dynamic{ColorSpec})` was defined. The trait declares `setcolor!(p::Plot, c::Kept{ColorSpec})::Nothing`, and that method does not cover a `Kept{ColorSpec}`. The reference asks only that the message name the operations ([traits §4.2](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#42-one-line), [§4.9](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#49-checks-at-the-declaration)); §4.9 names the signature for a result, not for a missing method. The message is built in `TraitWorld/src/implement.jl:1818` at 33dd55e (`_missing_ops`). *Proposal:* for each missing operation the message names the declared signature at the type (`setcolor!(::Lollipop, ::Kept{ColorSpec})`) and the type's methods of that function that do not cover it, with the argument that differs (`setcolor!(::Lollipop, ::Dynamic{ColorSpec})` at its file and line: argument 2 is `Dynamic{ColorSpec}`, the operation receives `Kept{ColorSpec}`). A rule in traits §4.2 and §4.9, then the message.

103. **Closed (2026-11-09, W-118, moot under the current references, mine):** `Kept` is gone. `Dynamic{X}(x)` takes its argument unspecialized (the constructor in Julia's `Core`), so making one compiles once per trait, not per value type ([traits §7.7](reference/traits.md#77-dynamicx); [compiler §6.9](reference/compiler.md#69-casts-and-keep)). *As it stood:* **Kept-value constructors compiled per value type** (candidate 6). `Kept{Callable{A, S}}(f)` compiles once per closure from plain code (4 specializations for 3 closures), so TDE wraps every keep in `@nospecializeinfer`. Blocked by `TraitWorld/src/core.jl` (`@inline function Kept{X}(x)`); traits §7 does not say what making a kept value compiles. *Proposal:* making a `Dynamic` value compiles nothing per value type: the constructors are entered unspecialized from plain code, as an entry's trait positions are ([lowering §8.2](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#82-the-entry)); stated in traits §7.

111. **Closed (2026-11-09, W-118, merged into 159, mine):** open issue 159 states the case of a function stored before its signature is known in the current references, and `ByRow` is its second case there. Reading the function a `Dynamic` holds is `unwrap(d)` ([traits §7.7](reference/traits.md#77-dynamicx)). *As it stood:* **`ByRow(f)`: a function kept before its signature is known, inside plain code that reads it** (W-88, open issue 102's second case; [the note](plans/2026-10-29-w88-102-kept-function.md)). `Tables.ByRow{F}` keeps the user's function at its own type, so `ByRow`'s method and DataFrames' mini-language are compiled per closure (E-216: a new closure's whole `transform` 24–25 ms against the kernel's 6.1–6.8 ms). Kept as `Dynamic{Callable}` ([traits §7.5](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#75-functions-kept)), they would be compiled once. Blocked by: (a) DataFrames' plain pipeline reads the function: column names (`funname` by `nameof`, `f_g` for a `ComposedFunction`), the `ComposedFunction{…, typeof(collect)}` fast path, upstream's test `ByRow(+) isa ByRow{typeof(+)}`; plain code has no public way to read the function a kept value holds ([traits §7.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#71-the-three-forms); `rewrap` returns a cast); (b) `ByRow` is a plain struct, and a struct parameter bounded by `Callable` without a signature is not defined ([traits §7.4](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#74-a-struct-parameter-bounded-by-a-trait)). *Proposal:* `struct ByRow{F <: Callable} <: Function` in `ColumnCore`: a function of the trait world (Base's `+`, `sum ∘ collect`) is kept as itself where the code knows its type, a user's function as `Dynamic{Callable}`; and a public read of a kept value's object for plain code (`object(k)`), used by the fork's `funname` and fast paths. Undecided: a singleton function of the trait world typed at the prompt (`ByRow(+)`), `Dynamic` by the first-seen rule there.

113. **Closed (2026-11-09, W-118, moot under the current references, mine):** the entry and its unspecialized trait positions are gone. A trait argument is compiled per type, or once, by its attribute ([traits §7.1](reference/traits.md#71-three-ways-to-compile-an-argument)); a call of a `Callable` known only by its trait has the declared result `S` ([traits §9](reference/traits.md#9-functions-callable)). TDE's `::Nothing` on `_ad_jacobian!` is a declared result, as the design has it. *As it stood:* **Facts inference loses a bounded function's result where a function kept at another signature enters it** (W-34, E-229). An `ODEProblem` whose function is `Dynamic`, kept in a `Kept{ODEProblemTrait}`, was refused as a family member: `jacobian!` inferred `Any`, declared `Nothing`. Plain inference of the same body gives `Nothing`. Under facts, the generated entry of `_ad_jacobian!(fd::Callable{CoreDualSig{N}, Nothing}, …)` is inferred at `fd::Any` (`enter_callable(…, ::Any, ::Any, …)`), then a dynamic `checked_call` (`TraitWorld.factscode`). TDE declares `_ad_jacobian!`'s result `::Nothing` (bc68501): the design's declared result, used here where inference should have found it. Blocked by the entry's lowering ([lowering §8.2](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#82-the-entry)): trait positions are entered unspecialized. *Proposal:* the facts entry infers at the argument's kept type, or passes on the bounded method's inferred result, so the declaration is not needed for inference.

114. **Closed (2026-11-09, W-118, moot under the current references, mine):** a value carries an attribute only as a `Dynamic{X}`, a type of Julia's `Core`: plain code asks `x isa Dynamic` and reads the object with `unwrap(x)` ([traits §7.5](reference/traits.md#75-what-decides), [§7.7](reference/traits.md#77-dynamicx)). *As it stood:* **Plain code cannot ask a kept value for its attribute or its object.** TraitReport's session check (09d7f9b) could test only that a sorted column is a `Kept{Column}`, not whether it is `Dynamic`: `TraitWorld.is_dynamic(::HeldValue)` exists but is neither exported nor in the reference; TraitTables' own test reads `getfield(c, :compiled)`. Open issue 111 (`ByRow`) needs the other half: plain code reading the function a kept value holds (`nameof`, composed functions). Blocked by [traits §7](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#7-kept-values-dynamicx-compiledx-keptx): it states how kept values are made, cast and passed, not how plain code inspects them. *Proposal:* two public functions for plain code, `isdynamic(k)` (the attribute) and `object(k)` (the user's object, or a `Callable`'s function), stated in traits §7 beside the constructors.

132. **Closed (2026-11-09, W-118, answered by the current references, mine):** a trait is an abstract type and its parameters are type parameters, so `getter(ip::IndexProvider{I}, idx::J) where {I, J <: I}` is Julia's own `where` ([traits §3.2](reference/traits.md#32-parameters), [§6](reference/traits.md#6-methods-at-traits)). TraitIndexing's own `idx isa I` goes when the SciML port is rebuilt on the unified build. *As it stood:* **A trait parameter as an upper bound for an ordinary argument** (W-32, E-245). By [traits §5.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#51-bounds), `getter(ip::IndexProvider{I}, idx::I) where {I}` requires the index's type to equal `I`, so it refuses a `VIndex` where `I = SymbolicIndex`; TraitIndexing's `getter` takes the index untyped and checks `idx isa I` itself. *Proposal:* `where {I, J <: I}` with `idx::J`, as plain Julia writes it; check whether §5.1 already allows it and the lowering refuses it.

133. **Closed (2026-11-09, W-118, moot under the current references, mine):** `@trait_module` is a declaration in an ordinary module whose statements are Julia's, so `public` stands where Julia parses it ([traits §13](reference/traits.md#13-scopes-trait-modules)). *As it stood:* **`public` inside a trait module** (W-32, E-247). Julia parses `public` only at a module's top level, so it cannot stand inside `@trait_module begin … end`; [traits §6.3](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#63-defaults-a-traits-default-and-annotations) relies on functions being declared `public`. NetworkDynamics declares them after the block. *Proposal:* the Planned plain-module form ([traits §2](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#2-trait-modules), T-81) allows it; until then a T- row or the porting guide says to declare `public` after the block.

109. **Closed (2026-11-07, mine, for the user to confirm):** built as proposed: `textbound(T::Type)` returns a type's bound or `nothing`, a constant for the types of fixed width, and `Solvable` is LinearAlgebra's `ldiv!`, so `Diagonal`, the triangular types and `Bidiagonal` join by their own `ldiv!` (TraitWorld `unified` 4be0097; [traits §10.1](reference/traits.md#101-basetraits); [decisions](record/decisions.md) §9). *As it stood:* **`BoundedText` gives a bound per value; a writer needs one per type.** CSV's writer sizes its row blocks by a byte bound per column; for cell types it has no width for, it renders 64 sampled cells. With `textbound` over every cell (`forks/scripts/bt_write.jl`, `bt_bounds.jl`, `forks/logs/bt/`; forks a6d0716): the bytes written equal upstream's; for bits types (`Char`, `Rational{Int64}`, `ComplexF64`) the loop folds to a constant and costs nothing; for variable-width types it is a pass over every cell (`BigInt` 1.9 ms per 10⁴ cells against the sample's 19 µs, `Symbol` 31 against 2.5 µs). The writer therefore keeps the sample. Blocked by [traits §8.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#81-basetraits-traits-for-bases-concepts): `textbound(x)` takes a value. *Proposal:* a bound per type where the type has one (fixed-width types: integers, floats, `Bool`, `Char`, dates, and types built from them, such as `Rational{Int64}`), for example `textbound(::Type{T})` returning an `Int` or `nothing`, with the bound per value for the rest; a writer uses the type's bound without visiting cells, and samples or visits only where there is none. Related: LinearAlgebra's `factorize` returns, besides factorizations, `Diagonal`, triangular and `Bidiagonal` matrices, which are not `Solvable` though each has `ldiv!` (a prompt solver in `experiments/w34-sciml/move3/stageC.jl` was refused at its declaration for that, and uses `lu`).

   - **Recommendation:** (a): it is the design's own mechanism, and the boundary's second policy is planned anyway; (b) costs a second system image per build; (c) is a special case in every lookup.
   - **Tried:** (c) on the run paths only (2026-11-07, not kept): the session gets past start-up, then crashes staging a generated function; the test bed has a further fault behind this one (E-274).

A model kept as `Dynamic{Callable{…}}` therefore shows no mass matrix to TDE's optional traits and is solved as an ODE. *Proposal:* a kept value whose held object conforms to none of the bounds' traits reaches the fallback, as its plain object would.
124. **Closed (2026-10-29, built: TraitWorld branch `w93` d749ec9, W-93):** a trait with supertraits is a `Subtrait{U}` of all of them, transitively, and a supertrait's declaration defines one method per operation function that covers the kept forms of every subtrait, so a later subtrait adds no method to its supertraits' operations ([lowering §3](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#3-trait-declarations); the choice of the encoding mine). A function two supertraits of one subtrait declare is chosen by the subtrait, its one method on a supertrait's function. Measured (E-244, the forks' loads): DataFrames' image code rejected by CSV's subtraits' methods 176 CodeInstances before, 0 after; test `test/runtests.jl`. *As it stood:* **Subtraits add methods to their supertrait's operation functions, and so reject plain callers' image code** (W-92, E-238). TraitWorld lowers a subtrait (CSV's `CSVColumn`, `TextColumn <: Column`) to methods of `Column`'s operations, `op(dyn::HeldValue{X}, …) where X<:Sub` (`TraitWorld/src/trait.jl:540`; lowering §3 does not describe it). A trait package invalidating plain code. *Proposal:* the supertrait's declaration defines one method per operation that covers the kept forms of all its subtraits, so a later subtrait adds none. Ask whether unification (W-83) removes the case: the methods exist because a subtrait is not a Julia subtype.

Keying by the bound does not help here: `Callable{A, S}` with `S` open has no hash either. *For the user:* take `A` as data with `@nospecialize`, as `Core.Compiler.return_type` does (an escape hatch by the user's rule), or leave it.

A subtrait's implementation changes nothing a member fold depends on: [facts §2.2](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#g2), "What invalidates nothing". *As it stood:* **Facts code rejected at load by another package's implementation** (W-92, E-238). 120. **Closed (2026-10-29, the user (2026-10-29): "Issue 120 approved", the recommendation: keep the check):** a declared result of `Nothing` still refuses an in-place function that returns its array ([traits §5.8](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#58-callable-functions-at-a-declared-signature)); the error names the declared and the actual result type and hints to end the function with `nothing` (W-93). Built: TraitWorld branch `w93` a405cc1, 4579236 ([traits §5.8](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#58-callable-functions-at-a-declared-signature); test `test/runtests.jl`). *As it stood:* **In-place functions whose result is discarded** (W-32, E-237). [Traits §5.8](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#58-callable-functions-at-a-declared-signature) makes a result of the wrong type a `TypeError`: TDE declares `rhs!(…)::Nothing`, so a SciML-valid closure that ends by returning its array (`du .= …` as the last line returns `du`) is refused. The declaration catches a common mistake (porting guide B2), and SciML ignores the result. *For the user:* keep the check (an error with a hint to end with `nothing`), or declare such operations' result as discarded (any result accepted, none used).

SciML reads a function's mass matrix, system and Jacobian prototype by duck typing; TDE's front end refuses a mass matrix only in its own `ODEFunction`, and ignores it on a callable such as NetworkDynamics' network. NetworkDynamics' test `diffusion:129` is then solved as if it had none: error 1.6 against 1e-6, no error raised. A G1-class fault for the port: a wrong result, not a refusal. *Proposal:* optional traits on the function's type (`HasMassMatrix`, `HasSystem`, `HasJacobianPrototype`), checked at the front end, refused where the solver cannot take the property. Until then the front end refuses a callable that has a `mass_matrix` property other than the identity.

98. **Closed (2026-10-28, mine, flagged; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §1; built 2026-10-29, TraitWorld e99851b, W-88):** decided as proposed, [traits §6.4](reference/traits.md). *As it stood:* **Opening a stored `Dynamic` at the callee** (audit candidate 1). DataFrames' kernels and TraitMakie's drawing loops need code per key, but their values arrive as stored `Dynamic` columns or kept fields; every caller writes `compiled_for` (9 sites in the forks, 17 in PlotCore), and a caller that forgets pays about 2× (E-214). Blocked by [traits §6.5](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#65-values-carry): a value keeps its attribute at every bound. *Proposal:* a bound that opens the value at the entry (`c::Opened{Column}`): one dispatch per call, then code per key; the callee knows its cost model, the caller does not.
100. **Closed (2026-10-28, mine, by the user's rule "Making the trait/type system more expressive is a principled change"; [decisions](record/decisions.md) §1; the plan [2026-10-28-annotation-audit-decisions](plans/2026-10-28-annotation-audit-decisions.md)):** a bounded function's declared result that is a plain type or a `Dynamic{X}` is asserted at the entry's returns and reaches every caller ([lowering §8.1](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#81-the-rewrite), [traits §7.6](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#76-equality-and-inference): "declare the result"; TraitWorld 254bcd8, efc8645). `tocolumn` declares `Dynamic{Column}`, and the four sites that asserted it are plain calls (forks, W-87). *As it stood:* **Declared results that do not reach the caller** (candidate 3). `tocolumn(::Vector{Int})` infers `Any` (four sites assert `::Dynamic{Column}`); PlotCore asserts 17 kernel results; MakieCompat asserts an operation's result on an untyped item. Blocked by [lowering §8.1](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#81-the-rewrite) (only a plain declared result goes on the entry) and [traits §7.6](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#76-equality-and-inference), which tells users to assert. *Proposal:* put a kept-form result (`Dynamic{X}`, a concrete type) on the entry too; traits §7.6 says "declare the result" instead of "assert it".
14. **Closed (2026-10-28):** taken up as §1 item 89 (the fresh-ideas round). *As it stood:* The joint-method rule for wide families such as `plot(x)` over data traits (makie-rewrite.md §8.9). *Revisit once experience with large-scale ports is at hand (W-65; §6): the Makie port.*
13. **Closed (2026-10-28):** taken up as §1 item 88 (the fresh-ideas round). *As it stood:* "Live" bounds: an argument that is a value or a source of values (`Live{X}`, makie-rewrite.md §8.3). *Revisit once experience with large-scale ports is at hand (W-65; §6): the Makie port (Observables).*
6. **Closed (2026-10-28):** taken up as §1 item 85 (the fresh-ideas round). *As it stood:* Input parameters (several values per type), associated type constructors beyond one family, and results bounded by their own trait (`view`, Swift's `SubSequence`). A case: `ODERHS{P}` for SciML's `ODEFunction`, which works at every `P` (open issue 54's first occurrence; form B avoids it). *Revisit once experience with large-scale ports is at hand (W-65; §6): the SciML and Makie ports show whether conformance at every value of a parameter or `view`-like results are needed.*
30. **Closed (2026-10-28, mine, E-190; for W-71):** one answer, one rule: `return_type` at run time is plain inference's in `PlainRTInterp`, the fold's, in both settings (julia-1.13 3e3da4e584; facts F-20 and compiler C-10 retired). The corpus of the sessions' own facts methods: run-time answers differing from plain inference's 26 of 907 in D and 414 of 6,554 in C before, none after; the sessions unchanged within 2%. *As it stood:* **To revisit: the exception for `return_type` in the product setting** (recorded as an exception, the user, 2026-10-21). The compile-time fold infers a facts method plainly (`PlainRTInterp`); the run-time route, a `NativeInterpreter` of owner `nothing`, hands it to facts inference, which can answer wider ([facts §7.1](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#return-type), the exception; [compiler §1.4](reference/compiler.md#14-return_type)). The intended answer is plain inference's ([facts §7.1](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#return-type), rule 3). To decide how: `PlainRTInterp` for the run-time route in both settings, accepting a second cache's inference for plain methods ([compiler C-10](reference/compiler.md#deviations)), or another route to the same answer. **To settle by E-190** (W-65; in W-22): the run-time query routed through `PlainRTInterp` in both settings; every answer checked `===` the folded one (E-44's corpus); the second cache's inference time measured in the demonstrators' sessions and E-168's. No measurable cost: adopt (one answer, one rule; F-20 and C-10 retire). A cost of 20% or more on a session: keep the exception, stated with its cost. **Measured beside it (E-200):** the compile-time route's plain inference (`facts_plain_return_type`) is 65% of the inference samples of C's session (upstream SciML compiled by facts as trait modules: calling into ordinary code), and none of D's (idiomatic trait code). In C the answers are for facts methods, so no plain cache can be shared; the cost is the fold rule itself.

Closed by the clean-up of 2026-10-27 (W-65; [the proposal](plans/2026-10-27-cleanup-issues-and-queue.md)):

73. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** not a choice; each option has its home: facts images without edge validation are W-66's Phase B ([the note](plans/2026-10-27-facts-keyed-cache.md)); a smaller tree is W-60's memo and the ports (W-34, W-70); strict mode's check at load goes with W-66's Phase B; remembered validation is PE-1 and an environment image PE-2, in [the performance explorations log](record/performance-explorations.md); the facts images' own load is fixed (W-59). *As it stood:* **The load of a large trait world** (W-58; E-169, E-174 to E-176). With a cache across sessions, every setting's work after the load is about a second or less; the load remains: stock SciML and CairoMakie 3.3–3.7 s, the same compiled by facts 3.85 s, against Python's 0.45 s. Where it goes (profiles): restoring 381 package images about 1.6–1.8 s, of which activating their methods (the search for what each insertion invalidates) 0.8–0.9 s; validating about a million edges 1.3–1.4 s; `__init__` and finding files about 0.4–0.6 s. Facts code is 11% of what C loads. A system image of the stack, which needs neither activation nor validation, could not be built on this machine (more than 38 GB of memory and swap; E-170). The load follows the stack's dependency tree (E-176). Options; the plan takes the first as the lever within reach, the loader changes as a second (a Julia change):
    - **A smaller tree:** the load follows the methods the stack adds (E-176: stock's 80,263 against the demonstrators' 9,196), and Makie's tree brings most of them (Distributions, SparseArrays, DataStructures, Unitful). The Makie rewrite's narrow waist ([explorations/makie-rewrite.md](explorations/makie-rewrite.md) §5) is where it can shrink.
    - **Validation and activation remembered for an unchanged environment:** a session that loads the same images as the previous one, with nothing defined before, reaches the same results; a loader that remembers them per image and predecessor set saves up to about 2.2 s of stock's 3.3 s. A Julia change, not trait-specific. *(Measured, W-64: E-186 saves 0.6–0.8 s of the load, the session about 0.5 s; set aside by the rule for performance work, [decisions](record/decisions.md) §5: not enabled by facts or traits, moderate gain for its complexity.)*
    - **Facts images without edge validation under strict mode** (the principled lever under the rule for performance work: enabled by facts): with its dependency closure's images unchanged (build ids), an image's facts code can be broken only by a method no image in that closure defined (pirates, extensions activated by others, standard libraries loaded later, evals from outside, definitions at run time) or a deletion; so a session index of those candidates leaves only the facts they intersect to walk (W-66's note, revision 2; [its review](reviews/2026-10-27-facts-keyed-cache.md) B4). It scales with the facts share: little in C today, nearly all of a whole trait world.
    - **Strict mode's check at load** (6.6 s for C, E-168) moved to image validation, its intended design ([facts §10.3](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#strict-intended)).
    - **An environment image** built in the background, as a system image: too large to build here for SciML and Makie.
    - **The facts images' own load** (W-59, fixed): under `all` the load was 8.2 s against 3.6 s, from specializations without a hash kept in images (the documentation system's `push!` of signature types); images no longer keep them (julia-1.13 6800be7839), and the load under `all` is 3.8 s.
72. **Closed (2026-10-27, the user):** the direction is the cache keyed on facts and their origins, W-66; the environment-keyed cache is retired. *As it stood:* **A cache that builds up across sessions** (the user, 2026-10-27: "traits gradually built up cache that lasts from session to session is exactly what should help here. The fact that all slowness can be fixed by the right precompilation doesn't really matter."). Code compiled in a session is lost at exit; package images keep only what precompile workloads ran. The design states only images built at precompile time ([design §4](design.md), "compiled once, not once per session"). **Direction (the user, 2026-10-27): a cache keyed on facts and their origins (W-66, [the exploration](explorations/facts-keyed-cache.md)); the environment-keyed cache (W-62) is retired.**
    - **Evidence:** E-172, an emulation: a perturbed session's compiled statements kept for the next session. What can be kept is decided by the code's key. Code keyed on library types can be kept: recorded at the level of CodeInstances (E-175), a cache takes stock SciML and CairoMakie's perturbed session from 12.9 to 4.9 s (SciML's `AutoSpecialize` already erases the function below the entry), the plain twins to 0.64 s, the trait demonstrators to 0.97 s (with the facts owner's code, exact types and the checks kept). A cache recorded from other call shapes reuses the plotting code but not SciML's solver code, which is keyed on the call's shape (E-178); the load remains (3.5–3.7 s for stock). Built as a mechanism in TraitWorld (E-180, branch `session-cache`): the demonstrators' off-workload session 2.4 → 1.07 s, the facts-compiled SciML core with CairoMakie 19.4 → 6.1 s; it holds in order B with a cache recorded in order A; the parts cost 0.15–0.45 s at load ([evidence §3](design/evidence.md)).
    - **Built before, for plain code, and removed:** combination caches (session 20) and view caches (sessions 21–29). Both kept code whose validity depended on the load order, so they were keyed by it ([alternatives](design/alternatives.md#caches-across-sessions-combination-caches-and-view-caches-removed)).
    - **What facts change (the design's claim, untested here):** code compiled by facts stays valid in any load order of unrelated packages (facts §11), so a kept CodeInstance need not be keyed by the order; plain code is cacheable too (E-175) but may be discarded at load in another order. Whether this difference shows on the workload's path is E-177 ([the direction review](reviews/2026-10-27-w58-direction.md) 1).
    - **To design (W-58 phase 4; [the cache note](plans/2026-10-27-session-cache.md), revision 2):** where the cache lives (per environment and dependency set); when it is written (at exit, built in the background); what it keeps (both owners' code with exact types and the first-call checks; not code keyed on `Main`); how it is validated at load (the facts edges; strict mode's check); whether it needs a Julia change (writing session-compiled code into an incremental image) or a generated package suffices first, as the combination caches did. Earlier thinking: [`explorations/fast-loads.md`](explorations/fast-loads.md) §3 item 5.

From "the references (2026-10-19)":

- **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; mine, adopted by the clean-up rule):** the setting is part of a package image's key, as Julia keys images by its code-generation flags ([facts §11](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#package-images), Planned; F-7; W-69). *As it stood:* **The setting as part of a package image's key.** Images are not keyed by the setting (product or test bed), nor by `--trim` ([facts F-7](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#deviations)).
- **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; mine, accepted as is by the clean-up rule; for the review session W-71):** `verify_main` reports the run-time sites of the code as it runs in the session, so under `all` the facts mode's sites in Base count; it is conservative, since a trimmed build compiles plainly ([facts §12.3](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#trimmed-binaries); F-8 retired; the check marked as expected under `all`, W-67). *As it stood:* **`verify_main` under the test bed** reports a run-time site in Base (`convert(Type{AbstractString}, ::Any)` inside `DomainError`) as a hole. Whether Base's own run-time sites under `all` count as holes ([facts F-8](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#deviations)).

2. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; accepted as is):** read through `getfield`; C-7. *As it stood:* Fields stored inline that are not isbits (an immutable struct holding references): read through `getfield`, or loaded inline by a runtime.
3. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; accepted as is):** Optional until a port targets isbits-only devices ([lowering §6](reference/lowering.md)). Item 21 with it. *As it stood:* A layout that copies the fields, opt-in per trait, for GPU kernels and other isbits-only targets; valid only for traits without writable fields.
4. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** a duplicate of [traits §4.7](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#47-families) (Planned) and T-7; built when a port needs it. *As it stood:* Forwarding for a whole family of user types, `@implement X Series{T} by data where {T}`: planned ([traits §4.7](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#47-families)), not prototyped.
