A field's constness does not depend on the family ([traits §3.3](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#33-fields); constness per family is not taken, [alternatives](design/alternatives.md)). *As it stood:* Mutable and immutable state families: `u` writable in one and `const` in the other. Either two traits, or field constness that depends on the family (solvers.md §11.7).
38. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; accepted as is):** values fixed per implementation are operations with declared results; values fixed per instance and needed in types are explicit parameters ([traits §3.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#31-operations)). *As it stood:* **Associated constants:** values fixed per implementation (`alg_order`, `isinplace`; the SciML exploration's PB-B), today operations with declared results; and values fixed per instance lifted into the type (`FunctionDynamics{…, PDIM, EXTDIM}`, the traits plan's PB-1), which are explicit parameters as in Julia (open issue 54's second occurrence).
7. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** resolved: BaseTraits' `iterate` declares a small `Union` result ([traits §8.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#81-basetraits-traits-for-bases-concepts)); the reporting half is closed item §1.18. *As it stood:* Whether an operation may return a small `Union` of concrete types, and what fully typed mode reports for it (makie-rewrite.md §8.4). The reporting half is decided: a small union is fully typed, and fully typed mode is silent about it (closed §1.18).
11. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; mine, adopted by the clean-up rule; for the review session W-71):** a trait inside a Base container's type in an argument annotation is refused at the definition, with a message naming the collection bound, through which a user's vector crosses as it is ([traits §5.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#51-bounds), §8.3; T-78; W-67; copying element by element is not taken, [alternatives](design/alternatives.md)). *As it stood:* How `Vector{<:ColTable}` takes a user's vector: element by element into a new vector of trait objects, or as a whole. The copy costs one pass and loses aliasing (writes no longer reach the user's vector); collection traits (item 31) would let a vector cross as a whole, through a view.
16. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; accepted as is):** a plain assertion on the held object ([traits §7.1](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#71-the-three-forms)). *As it stood:* Opening a `Dynamic` at the conforming value's own concrete type, a checked downcast, for callbacks that change parameters (solvers.md §11.2). `compiled_for(d)` opens it at its key; a downcast to the user's type is a plain assertion. Related: a trait-typed assertion in a body (item 36).
17. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** resolved: by pointer, an `Erased{A, S}` with a `:caller_world` `cfunction` for any function ([lowering §12](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#12-callable-and-dynamiccallable)). *As it stood:* How a closure created inside a trait module is stored as a dynamic function: by dispatch, or by a pointer (makie-rewrite.md §8.5).
18. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** resolved by [traits §7.3](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#73-assigning-from-plain-code) (closed items §1.40, §1.47). *As it stood:* Assignment from above into a field declared by a trait name, `plot.color = x` at the prompt: whether `setproperty!` from ordinary code there is an entry (makie-rewrite.md §8.7).
19. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** a check: E-63, in W-9. *As it stood:* Tasks and concurrency: trait objects and `Dynamic` values across tasks; spawning a trait-world closure (makie-rewrite.md §8.6).
20. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; accepted as is):** a function without a method at `A` raises `MethodError` when called ([traits §5.8](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#58-callable-functions-at-a-declared-signature)); the misleading warning in compiled mode is fixed in W-67. The solvers agent's item 4 (closed items §3) with it. *As it stood:* Reporting a function from above that always throws when its pointer is made (`Union{}` result at the declared signature; solvers.md §11.10).
21. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** with item 3. *As it stood:* Checking held parts after each call upward: obsolete under the reference layout, kept only for the opt-in copying layout of item 3.
22. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** a test for the conformance suite, E-47. *As it stood:* An example and a test that a value crossing upward behaves as it does inside (C++0x's `late_check`, 2-design-choices, prediction 3).
23. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** with strict mode's intended design, W-66's Phase B. *As it stood:* Indexes for strict mode's attribution of methods to packages, so that it never scans all methods (prediction 7). Sealing and the dispatch check, for which this was first written, are gone.
24. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** resolved: [facts §2.1](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#g1), the exception for values decided by inference. *As it stood:* Inference-dependent element types (`promote_op`): the declared exception to "identical or error"; solvers must declare them instead (solvers.md §11.8).
25. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** a measurement: E-110; the views part is moot (views are deleted). *As it stood:* The compile cost of recipes and models defined at the prompt; views exclude modules under `Main` (findings §15, makie-rewrite.md §8.8).
27. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; mine, accepted as is by the clean-up rule; for the review session W-71):** the edges go to plain code, consistent with the decision "the compiler's own code is plain whoever calls it"; the loss is cacheability only, measured at 0 ([facts §6.4](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#the-compilers-own-code); L-17 retired; calls in the compiler's world are not taken, [alternatives](design/alternatives.md)). *As it stood:* **The B1 boundary: facts code that calls compiler functions directly** (the performance review of 2026-10-19, B1). TraitWorld's machinery calls `argextype`, `widenconst` and `sptypes_from_meth_instance`. Such calls hold edges to plain compiler code, whose validity is plain Julia's. Documented, not fixed ([facts §6.4](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#the-compilers-own-code); [lowering L-17](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#18-deviations)). The reason: a rule that ran such calls in the compiler's world would break packages that extend the compiler for their own interpreter types. The loss is cacheability, not correctness: the control's nine-package load lost no facts code in the modules it counted (TraitMakie 0; TraitWorld's own code was not counted separately).
28. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** the forks' workload extensions are W-14's; the gaps are L-15. *As it stood:* **AOT from declared targets: the forks' workload extensions** ([traits §13](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#13-compiling-ahead); [lowering §15.1](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#151-compiling-ahead-from-declarations); [state §6.3](state.md#63-the-order-of-work)). Compiling ahead from declarations is built (TraitWorld `src/ahead.jl`): while a package that declares an implementation is precompiled, the workers for its key, the pattern methods for untyped callers and their first-call checks go into its image. Still open: replacing hand-written workload extensions such as the forks' `CSVDataFramesInlineStringsExt` (CSV + DataFrames + InlineStrings, forks 9d512a1), which the performance review calls a workaround, by it; what it leaves out, functions with several trait positions or a vararg ([lowering L-15](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#18-deviations)); and what its entry methods invalidate (item 29).
31. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** decided (closed items §1.21–25), built, measured (E-164). Its last open points: which traits and algorithms come next, and two traits declaring one function at one arity, revisited with the ports (§6); a generic algorithm takes its function as an ordinary argument, as `retain!` does (accepted as is); items of one concrete conforming type wait for traits in the type system (W-27); a `Dynamic` argument boxed in a `Dynamic` `Callable` is W-17's; `Dynamic{X}(x)` at a run-time site in plain code is PE-6 ([the performance explorations log](record/performance-explorations.md)). *As it stood:* **Collection traits in TraitWorld** (the user, 2026-10-20: "TraitWorld needing collections sounds right to me. Vector{AbstractType} is a poor substitute for an Iterable trait"). Trait code that loops over heterogeneous data should be bounded by a collection trait with a declared item bound (an `Iterable` whose items are `Dynamic{X}`), not take a `Vector{AbstractFoo}` or a `Vector{Any}`. Then the item type is known from the bound, each operation on an item is the `Dynamic` split rather than a call at `Any`, and a user's existing vector crosses through a lazy view that wraps each element when read: no copy, and the user's vector stays the one read and written (item 11). This is the shape of Rust's `impl Iterator<Item = &dyn Shape>` and Swift's `some Sequence<any Shape>`. To decide:
    - which traits: iterable, indexable, mutable indexable, keyed (for `Dict{String,Any}`), and their associated types;
    - when an element that does not implement `X` fails: eagerly when the view is made (a pass over the data), or lazily when it is read (after part of the work is done). Rust checks at the producer; Swift, at its boundary with Objective-C, checks an `NSArray` of class elements bridged to `[T]` lazily, on read ([other languages §11](design/other-languages.md));
    - who provides the view: an explicit adaptor in TraitWorld (sketch name `DynamicView{X}(v)`), or conformance of Base's containers to the traits, which raises who owns that declaration;
    - what wrapping on read costs: making a `Dynamic` must become cheap (157 ns and 32 B in the review, a scan of the candidate registry; 5 ns since the branch `basetraits`, below);
    - the measurement: the review's experiments rewritten as idiomatic trait code against such a bound, compared with plain Julia, Rust's `dyn` and Swift's `any`: measured, below.
    - **Decided (2026-10-20, the user; open issues §1, closed items 21–25):** BaseTraits as a module of TraitWorld, its names exported; the traits `HasLength`, `Iterable{T}`, `Indexable{T}`, `MutableIndexable{T}`, `Resizable`, `Keyed{K, V}`, Base's containers conforming by Base's methods; `Dynamic` holds the user's object, the split's candidates in declaration order; a user's container crosses implicitly, by the bound, and a non-conforming item fails when read ([traits §8.3](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#83-a-users-container-taken-as-it-is)). The crossing's finer rules are the BaseTraits agent's, to confirm (§1, item 26).
    - **Measured (E-134, E-143, E-144; `experiments/hetero-idiomatic/README.md`):** making a `Dynamic` 5 ns (was 157); wrapping on read allocates nothing for the split's candidates. Within four types the idiomatic code recovers the tails in both settings: `filter!` 3.3–3.8 ns over the view (1.6–1.8 over a copy) against 41–43 interop and 2.2–2.4 plain; the `Dict{String,Any}` loop 4–5 ns against 274–276 interop and 2.4 plain. Beyond four the view costs about twice the copy (64 types: 162–181 against 78–84 ns, plain 99–106): two run-time sites per item outside the split. Item 32's question applies to both.
    - **Still open:**
      - which traits and algorithms come next (numbers, strings, IO; `count`, `map`, `sum` under their own names, since a bounded method of a Base function ranks below Base's typed methods and never runs for Base's containers, traits §5.5);
      - two traits that declare the same function at the same arity (`Indexable` and `Keyed` both declare `getindex`): a value carrying both is ambiguous at that operation;
      - generic algorithms over any item type cannot take a `Callable` at the item type (its argument types are constant); `retain!` takes `f` as an ordinary argument;
      - items of one concrete conforming type (`Iterable{T}` with `T` conforming, compiled per item type), Rust's `impl Iterator<Item = T> where T: Shape`: needs `where` variables in bounds;
      - a `Callable{Tuple{Dynamic{X}}, S}` in `DynamicCall` mode boxes the `Dynamic` per call (14–18 ns, 16 B); passing the held object instead is a TraitWorld change, not made;
      - `Dynamic{X}(x)` at a run-time site in plain code (a comprehension over a `Vector{Any}`) costs 102–108 ns, because Julia's per-site cache refuses calls whose function is a type (`src/gf.c`, `call_cache_exact`; the per-site cache's loop in `jl_lookup_generic_` would need entries matched on the function object itself). `collect(DynamicItems{X}(v))` costs 2 ns per element.
37. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** PE-3 in [the performance explorations log](record/performance-explorations.md): beyond the split trait code beats plain (E-164), so no gap motivates it; compiler §1.6 and §3.3 keep it Optional. *As it stood:* **Recompiling in the background with the observed types** (Optional, [compiler §1.6, §3.3](../attic/writeup/reference/compiler.md#33-further-work-on-mechanism-3)). The user (2026-10-20): "Guarded devirtualization was mostly problematic because it causes a permanent slowdown. If we can have a slow fallback but then recompile in parallel and swap compiled code back in once it is available, then that would of course be amazing."
    - **What E-145 found** ([`experiments/fallback/README.md`](../experiments/fallback/README.md)): with the table behind the split, a split over the observed hot types is 3× faster only for one multiply on data in the cache, in skewed or phase-changing mixes; 1.4× to 2.1× for a heavier body; 0.8× to 1.8× on large data, where a split over random types costs. To revisit when a demonstrator shows a hot site whose types are outside the declared four.
    - **Why recompiling in the background removes the objection to guarded devirtualization.** The objection (postponed 2026-10-19, [the speed plan](plans/2026-10-19-principled-speed-plan.md) §2) was the permanent cost: a failed guard leaves the site on the slow path until its caller happens to compile again. With recompilation in the background the cost is bounded: the fallback's cost until the new code is ready, then the observed types at inlined speed.
    - **Why it keeps the guarantees** (reasoning). The swap is not an invalidation: the old and the new code are valid in the same worlds and compute the same results. Each new branch is a fact with its edge, and the choice of types records nothing, as the split's candidate list records nothing today. So "identical or error" and G2 hold as for the split.
    - **What it needs from Julia** (reasoning, to verify):
      - a counter at the fallback;
      - a compile request for the caller's MethodInstance with extra candidates, on a background thread (whether Julia's code generation can run there alongside the program is to verify);
      - a way to make the new code the one that runs: a newer CodeInstance for the same MethodInstance and worlds that dispatch prefers, and an atomic update of the entry that callers go through. Callers that call the old code directly, or inlined it, keep it until they compile again;
      - for package images: what was learned is lost at exit unless it is recorded, for example as a workload for the next build;
      - a closed (`--trim`) build knows every implementation and needs none of this.
    - **What it costs:**
      - speed depends on what the session has run, as in every tiered JIT;
      - the code that runs changes after the start, so reflection must say which version runs;
      - a background compile needs a thread, in programs that may not otherwise use one.
    - **For ordinary run-time sites** (trait code calling plain Julia, [the review](reviews/2026-10-20-heterogeneous-collections.md) F6): the same mechanism, fed by the types a site sees, is type feedback. It is the remedy for callees whose methods sit only at `Any`, where a split over the method table has nothing to split on. Its limit there: the fallback's result is `Any`, so code after the call stays untyped unless the observed branches are the whole story.

36. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** with W-27: once `x isa X` means conformance, `v::X` in a body is where the value is first seen through `X`; until then, the constructors of [traits §6.2](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#62-how-plain-values-enter-the-trait-world). *As it stood:* **A trait-typed assertion in a body** (the user, 2026-10-20: "say a non-trait module defines a value v of a type that implements T and we introduce v_as_T = module.v::T in a function body. Then I guess this would be a compiled way to enter the trait world..."). Not in the design today. Under the `Dynamic`/`Compiled` rule it would be a place where a value is first seen through `T`: `Compiled` where the code knows the value's concrete type (a `const` or typed global), `Dynamic` for an ordinary global. To decide with W-3 (what `::` means for a trait against an abstract type); not built in W-4.

- **`dynamic by`** (the design and the code disagree, item 3; closed 2026-10-20, the user): not part of the language ([traits §4](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#4-implementations)); TraitWorld refuses it (7fc229c).

- **Facts code behind `jl_apply`** (closed 2026-10-20, decided): a dispatch through the runtime's `jl_apply` (splats of unknown length, `jl_call`, `@cfunction`, finalizers, task entry) runs the callee's default owner's code. A trait module's method's default owner is the facts owner, so trait code reached this way is code compiled by facts; a neutral method runs its plain code, which is not trait code, so trait code is never invalidated through this path. This needs no reliance on the runtime's internals. Implemented as built; facts F-3 is retired ([facts §5](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#run-time-sites); [compiler §2.5](../attic/writeup/reference/compiler.md#25-dispatch-from-facts-code)). Was: "Dispatch through the runtime's `jl_apply` … runs the default owner's code, so plain code for neutral methods in the product setting. Whether every way facts code dispatches should run facts code."

26. **Closed (s29):** both questions are answered by part (D) (julia-1.13 f6838f96fc, TraitWorld 703f814). The machinery is not on the path of a `Dynamic` operation, and `sealed_call` is gone. The `isa` branches come from the call-site split: an operation on a `Dynamic` value branches over the registered conformances (at most four) and falls back to a dynamic call. Witness tables are deleted. Recorded in [traits §7](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#7-kept-values-dynamicx-compiledx-keptx), [§10.4](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#104-no-sealing) and [facts §9](../attic/writeup/reference/facts.md#dynamic-split). **No sealing, of abstract types or functions** (user, session 28; to think about later). The facts-mode recovery test (`plans/2026-10-17-policy-in-compiler-plan.md` §3) suggested sealed abstract types and sealed functions, so that calls at abstract types become facts. The user's view: this notion should leave the project entirely. One should not seal an abstract type but use a trait instead: the trait's declared signatures and result types, checked for every conformance, are the closed contract. To settle:
    - *(Answered in session 29 by `Dynamic` in the compiler: with the call-site split (D) in the caller, the machinery is not on the path of a `Dynamic` operation, and `sealed_call` goes.)* how TraitWorld's own machinery is exempted under the facts mode in the compiler without a "sealed" notion (the prototype's `sealed_call` names TraitWorld's functions);
    - what replaces the `isa` branches plain Julia emits over a known set of methods, once the call goes through `Dynamic` with witness tables.

A container typed by a trait holds the members' own values; `keep` stores a value with its attribute ([traits §7.8](reference/traits.md#78-keeping-an-argument-with-its-attribute)). *As it stood:* **A trait applied to `where` variables in a trait module's body** (audit candidate 10): `Callback{U, T, P}[]` is not rewritten to `Kept{…}[]`, as `Column[]` is (`_dyn_body`, `trait_module.jl`, within lowering L-6). *Proposal:* rewrite it, or a deviation row under L-6.

108. **Closed (2026-11-09, W-118, moot under the current references, mine):** the certificate is gone with the entry and the untyped table it counted. Fully typed mode is the compiler's check, which the library explains ([compiler §2.7](reference/compiler.md#27-fully-typed-mode); [traits §14](reference/traits.md#14-concreteness-and-fully-typed-mode)). *As it stood:* **`fully_typed` on trait-world code** (candidate 11): it crashes with an `AssertionError` on DataFrames' `_group_rows!` and on `joinkeys` without its assertions. Where kept values are passed, it reports 8–30 sites of TraitWorld's own machinery (the per-parameter dispatch of a `Dynamic{X{T}}`, the untyped table's miss path, locks, `Task` code). Blocked by `TraitWorld/src/certificate.jl`, which counts only operations on `Dynamic` values as declared. *Proposal:* count the entry's dispatch and the table as declared sites, fix the assertion, add a test per case. Until then the porting guideline's advice to keep reports clean cannot hold. *In part done (TraitWorld 254bcd8):* the crash (an argument of `cglobal`, an expression, read as a value), the untyped table's miss path skipped as machinery, and two real costs the report showed in the entry (a `convert` of the worker's result per result part, a branch for a result like the input, both gone for a plain declared result; `_group_rows!` 51 → 8 sites). Left: a value opened from a stored `Dynamic` (`compiled_for`) entering a kernel is reported (`_assemble`, `checked_call`: the one dispatch per call that chooses the worker), not counted as declared.

128. **Closed (2026-10-29, built: TraitWorld branch `w93` e6c1678, W-93):** the machinery chooses the untyped fallback among the tagged methods alone (by `invoke` where the call by dispatch is ambiguous), so the module's end reports no ambiguity between a tagged fallback and another method ([lowering §8.1](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#81-the-rewrite); a closed build calls by dispatch, L-47); test `test/runtests.jl`. Not yet enough for NetworkDynamics' `NWState(integrator)`: a call with keywords to a function whose bounded method takes none reaches no fallback, a separate case. *As it stood:* **A spurious ambiguity between a bounded method and a typed one** (W-32, E-239). `NWState(int::(IntegratorView, HasSystem))` is reported ambiguous with `NWState(thing, uflat::AbstractVector)`, an intersection no call can reach. It blocks `NWState(ctx.integrator)` and `NWParameter(integrator)` in callbacks, which NetworkDynamics' documentation uses. *Proposal:* the module's-end check reports only ambiguities a call can reach; a test with this pair.
131. **Closed (2026-10-29, built: TraitWorld branch `w131` ea897b1, W-95; decided, mine):** a bounded method of a type's constructor works as any bounded function: everything the machinery keys by the function is keyed by `Core.Typeof(f)` (`Type{NWState}`), and the function is read back by one helper ([lowering §8.1](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#81-the-rewrite)); the type's ordinary constructors are the fallback, with and without keywords ([traits §5.5](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#55-dispatch-types-first-then-traits)); an untyped constructor of the entry's shape, Julia's default ones included, is refused when the bounded method is defined ([lowering L-50](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#18-deviations), mine); test `test/constructors.jl`; `experiments/w131/`. *As it stood:* **A bounded method on a type's constructor fails at its call** (W-95; `experiments/w130/repro.jl`). `NWState(int::(IntegratorView, HasSystem))` raises `UndefRefError` on main and on `w130`: TraitWorld keys entries by `typeof(f)`, which is `UnionAll` for `NWState`, and reads the function as `F.instance` in about ten places (`entry.jl:203`, `:479`, `:624`, `ahead.jl:104`, `concrete.jl:533`, …). Julia allows methods on constructors. *Decided (2026-10-29, mine):* key entries by `Core.Typeof(f)` (`Type{NWState}`) and get the function through one helper; a bounded constructor works as any bounded function.
123. **Closed (2026-10-29, built: TraitWorld branch `w93` 68239d6, W-93):** compiling ahead for an implementation reads the entry positions from TraitWorld's records (the methods of `needed`), not every binding of every loaded module, which read Cairo's deprecated bindings ([lowering §15.1](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#151-compiling-ahead-from-declarations)); test `test/depwarn.jl` (an image build under `--depwarn=yes`). *As it stood:* **`@implement` reads a module's deprecated bindings** (W-32, E-240). Under `Pkg.test`'s `--depwarn=yes`, each `@implement` in TraitMakie's PlotCore prints Cairo's deprecation warnings while precompiling (208 in all): TraitWorld seems to read all of a module's bindings at each implementation. *Proposal:* find the read and resolve only the names the implementation uses; a test under `--depwarn=yes`.
2. **Closed (2026-10-28, W-67's probe 2):** a parameter-bounded type constructor at an archetype raises no false problem: `edge1(g::Graph{T}) where {T} = SimpleEdge{T}(nv(g), nv(g))` with `SimpleEdge{T <: Integer}` passes definition checking and runs (`experiments/checks/w67_probes.jl`, `.log`). *As it stood:* a probe was to settle whether an open parameter at an archetype (`ParamArchetype{X,:p}`) is refused by such a constructor.

**Performance**

9. **Closed (s29)** as a measurement: re-measured on the facts compiler. Typed calls 36–38 ns against 33.8–34.5 ns for the same loop with `len` an argument. The old 30 ns used a literal `16`, which unrolls the loop. Untyped calls 115–129 ns against about 55 ns for a plain untyped call (`design/evidence.md` §2). The untyped cost is accepted: type-unstable code was never claimed fast (`design/performance.md`, "Costs that remain"). Typed calls cost 33 ns against 30 ns for plain Julia (`total(t, 16)`), without allocation. Untyped calls (the caller does not know the argument's type) cost 105–117 ns and 64 bytes (270–295 ns before TraitWorld 5efbb4c: the entry built the tuples for a result like the input on every call). What remains is two dynamic calls (`enter`, `checked_call`), the boxed trait object and the boxed result; removing them would compile part of the entry per user type.
10. **Closed (s29):** the piracy guard was removed in session 25, and rule 4 is gone ([design §3](design.md#3-the-design-in-brief)). The first-call check (`worker_problems`) now reflects a worker by facts inference, the code that runs, shared with its compilation and kept in images (TraitWorld 93c8a08). The forks' first `String15` read went from 1.74 s to 0.04 s. That gain is shared with a workload extension (forks 9d512a1); the two were not separated (`design/evidence.md` §3). A worker that has not been called in a session runs `_check_once` and the piracy guard at its first call; after any world change the guard audits the signature again (the PoC's stand-in for rule 4), which costs tens to hundreds of milliseconds after large loads.
11. **Closed (s29):** measured. TraitMakie's forwarding `@implement` in a fresh process: 77 ms without loads, 96–97 ms with them (`design/evidence.md` §3, *n = 1*). This is slower than the 36 ms measured earlier in session 29: see §4.12. A fresh session's `@implement` of a forwarding type: not re-measured since the rewrite (it was about 470 ms).

**Session 27, prototype gap:** **Closed (s29):** the policy's `invoke` of a CodeInstance is gone with the policy (TraitWorld c533f3a). `verify_main` now scans `:facts` code by owner, and code reached from facts code as facts code (TraitWorld 3db6592). TraitMakie's row 5 (dispatch targets) has not been measured since. `verify_main` in a session does not scan the workers behind the policy's `invoke` of a CodeInstance (it counts them as static calls), so it lists no trait sites or targets there; only a trimmed build, which compiles the plain calls, shows them. TraitMakie row 5 waits for it.

TraitDifferentialEquations' two broken tests for this pass (TraitDifferentialEquations 62e9fa4). `ImplementError` messages: a missing operation or a wrong argument type names neither the operation nor its declared signature; the text still says `project(t) = …` and "representation". Wanted: "`rhs!` has no method for Tuple{…}; existing: …".

**Session 29, found under (B):** **Closed (s29), except the limitation in the last sentence:** fixed in TraitWorld 3db6592. TraitWorld's machinery read the user's object as `Any` through generic helpers (`_loadpart(::Type, ::Any, ::Slot)`): run-time sites under facts, hidden before by `sealed_call`. Fixed: the helpers take the view. `verify_main` now scans code reached from facts code as facts code; an instance reached in both modes is scanned in the first mode met (a limitation).

### §4

Closed by W-118 (2026-11-09): items that named the earlier design's mechanisms ([todo](record/todo.md) W-118).

67. **Closed (2026-11-09, W-118, moot under the current references, mine):** a member's value is passed unwrapped ([traits §3.5](reference/traits.md#35-values)), so a frame prints the value's own type, inlined or not; no trait object's printing is lost to inlining. Julia's own trade-off for inlined frames stays. *As it stood:* **To revisit: readable stack traces against inlining** (found while testing W-40, 2026-10-27; the user: "Did you identify a trade-off between printing in stack traces and performance? If so note this as a revisit."). Trait objects print by bound and key in stack traces (`PVec{Float64} at Vector{Float64}`, [traits §12](../attic/writeup/reference/traits.md#12-printing)), but only in a frame of their own: an inlined frame prints no argument types. With W-40 a family member's typed call costs what a declared type's does, so the worker inlines into its caller and the frame that named the trait object disappears (`TraitWorld/test/printing.jl` keeps one with `@noinline`). The faster the code, the fewer frames name a trait. Options to weigh: keep it so (Julia's own trade-off for inlined frames); print the trait object's type for inlined frames from the line table's method instance; a debug setting that keeps workers out of line. Related: `verify_main` reports the crossing's now-static call as a trait argument of unknown type (`:trait_arg`) where it reported a dispatch (`test/basetraits.jl`). *Revisit once experience with large-scale ports is at hand (W-65; §6): the ports show how often debugging needs the trait object in an inlined frame.*

68. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65; mine, adopted by the clean-up rule; for the review session W-71):** a type conforms only where each of its values meets the trait's contract. `Symbol` does not implement `ColorSpec` or `MarkerShape`; TraitMakie implements them for validated name types, and `convert(ColorSpec, ::Symbol)` translates and refuses an unknown name at the store, by decision 47's path (W-67; [the porting guide](intro/porting.md); a validation hook at stores is not taken, [alternatives](design/alternatives.md)). *As it stood:* **Pushback on decision 47 (W-44): a conforming value is stored untranslated, so its library cannot refuse a bad one at the assignment** (TraitMakie, 2026-10-27). `Symbol` implements `ColorSpec` and `MarkerShape`, so `p.color = :magenta` stores the name as it is; TraitWorld calls the library's `convert(ColorSpec, x)` only for a value that does not conform. TraitMakie's own conversion used to refuse an unknown name at the assignment; now the `Symbol` implementations refuse it where it is used, at the render (TraitMakie 9436157). The error comes later and further from its cause. Options when revisited: a library's validation hook for conforming values at a store; or implement the trait for a validated name type instead of `Symbol`, and translate symbols to it.

10. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** PE-5 in [the performance explorations log](record/performance-explorations.md): by design; the remedy is upstream. *As it stood:* **Upstream CSV + DataFrames pay 369–386 ms after the nine loads under `all`** (plain Julia 4.0 s; diagnosed 2026-10-20, E-142, `experiments/loadcosts/README.md`). Two parts, both by design under today's rules:
    - **`CSV._parse` compiled again by facts, 320–334 ms (86–89 %).** SparseArrays' `vcat(::Union{Number, AbstractVecOrMat{<:Number}}, …)` (`sparsevector.jl:1275`) wins at `vcat(::Int64, ::Vector{Int64})`, which CSV writes as `[0; v]`. StatsBase, Distributions, Interpolations or CairoMakie alone each cause it, since each loads SparseArrays. A standard library counts as Julia's own code, so this is a stakeholder's change, followed (§1.19 decides whether it should be). Plain mode compiles the same function again in 675 ms. Remedies: decide §1.19, or upstream (SparseArrays' concatenation methods only for sparse arguments; CSV not concatenating Base's types this way).
    - **The plain inference behind run-time `return_type`, 45–63 ms (12–16 %).** Its cached answers at abstract signatures (`quantile!(::AbstractVector, …)`, sort internals) are invalidated by the loads and computed again in `describe` (the diagnosis of 2026-10-19, `experiments/factscompiler/step0/`, when it was 70 % of about 990 ms). The planned revalidation after loads would remove it; keeping the plain answers in package images is another idea.

35. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** W-17: the gaps where trait code should be as fast as plain code, each kept at 20% or more (the rule for performance work, [decisions](record/decisions.md) §5). *As it stood:* **Performance issues to revisit after W-4** (the user, 2026-10-20: "Where you make a choice that you judge performance relevant, record it as a possible performance issue to revisit."): P1 and P3–P10 of [the implementation plan §1a] (P2, `compiled_for` from an untyped caller, takes the table: [lowering §9.3](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#93-untyped-callers-of-compiled-values-the-table))(plans/2026-10-20-dynamic-compiled-implementation.md#1a-scope-semantics-not-performance): untyped callers on the erased worker (a split per operation, P1); an erased worker in an image splits over its package's candidates only (P3; a user's type then takes the split's table, one probe per operation: [compiler §4.3](../attic/writeup/reference/compiler.md#43-what-follows-the-split-the-table-of-a-calls-targets)); `Dynamic{Callable}`'s extra load (P4); the prompt `Dynamic` (P5); `with_dynamic` boxing isbits values (P6); sites relying on opening that `compiled_for` misses (P7); an erased worker per parameter value (P8); a call dispatched at run time passes `Dynamic` values, also through measurement harnesses such as `@allocated f(x)` (P9); the `Dynamic` path's small machinery compiled per new type (P10). Found with E-145: an operation whose result is `Self` is not split at all; it opens the value and dispatches twice (`_dyn_like`, P11). Found in the same checks: under the test bed `all`, entering a container that crosses into a view (`DynamicItems` from a `Vector{Any}`) costs about 300 B per call, a family's `conformance` and a run-time call of the worker; constant, not per item; `test/basetraits.jl:122` fails its bound under `all` only (P12). Each is revisited in W-17, after the idiomatic rewrites and E-145's work; W-4 adds the sites E-147 finds.
    - **A non-bits immutable argument of a `Dynamic` `Callable` is boxed per call** (E-157, 2026-10-22): a `NamedTuple` `p` holding a vector, 32 B and about 7 ns more per call, 6 boxes per Tsit5 step; SciML's `p` is often such a value. Bits values and mutable arrays are not boxed. Source: `TraitWorld/src/core.jl:1118` (`_ctype` maps non-bits types to `Any`), the `ccall` at `:1146` (lowering §12). Ways round it today: `compiled_for(f)`, or a mutable `p`.
43. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** W-17, by E-192; the reference promises plain speed (L-36). *As it stood:* **A `Callable` argument at a typed entry is entered by an untyped call** (found while building W-25, 2026-10-21; not measured; [lowering L-36](../attic/writeup/reference/lowering.md#18-deviations)). `enter_callable` is `@noinline` with the function and the attribute unspecialized (`entry.jl`, `enter_callable`), so its result is inferred `Any` even where the caller knows the function's type, and the worker's call after it is a dynamic dispatch with an `Any` result. Seen in `code_typed` of `fm(x::LMeas) = colmap(abs, x)` (`TraitWorld/test/like.jl`'s module). To measure, then decide: a typed form of the entry's decision where the function's type and the attribute are known (as the trait positions' `enter`), or the cost accepted. Related, also not measured: a `Dynamic` function seen at another signature is wrapped afresh at each crossing (`_erased_at`, `entry.jl`: a new `Erased` with its own pointer cell, the pointer fetched at its first call); the entry's cache per (signature, attribute, type) serves plain functions only. A crossing inside a hot loop allocates per call; TraitDifferentialEquations crosses once, at the problem's construction. The crossing itself goes with W-31 (traits T-54): the design refuses it, and a function kept at several signatures is a tuple.
78. **Closed (2026-10-27, W-65):** W-7: about 30% of the first render, worth investigating. *As it stood:* **Facts inference through a `Dynamic{Callable}`'s target** (E-182 (iii)): a call of a `Dynamic{Callable}` from code compiled by facts runs the target's plain code ([compiler §3.1](../attic/writeup/reference/compiler.md#31-the-cfunction-calling-convention-caller_world)), but inference by facts still passes into the target and its callees, making CodeInstances of owner `:facts` without native code. At small scale it costs about 65 ms at a first render (the hand-over form 262–269 ms against 199–201 ms). Not diagnosed: whether the call site's inference or the callable's first-call check infers it. A candidate fix: the facts interpreter treats a `Dynamic{Callable}` call as opaque beyond its declared result. *(Under the rule for performance work as refined ([decisions](record/decisions.md) §5): worth investigating: about 30% of the first render, and a slowdown where the hand-over should cost nothing. Candidates for the cause: TraitWorld's pointer made at the first call (`_erased_ptr_slow`, reached from facts code), or the `:caller_world` thunk's emission.)*

Closed before the clean-up:

1. **Closed (s29):** superseded. View caches are deleted (`viewcache.jl`, TraitWorld c533f3a), and stock support is dropped. With a view cache on stock 1.13.2, `summarize` recompiles for 71–74 ms once CSV and Makie are loaded (3.3 ms in session 21 on 1.12). It is unclear whether 1.13 or TraitWorld causes this (`experiments/baseline-1.13.md`).
2. *(Resolved by wrapping by facts, session 24: with nothing skipped, 0 facts-owned CodeInstances are lost to any of twelve loads, in any order; `wrapping-by-facts.md` §Validation. Its filter protects code from a definition only if the definer owns no type in the code's signature (review F1, fixed in 280612ed2f; before, a user's redefinition of its own implementation left the worker stale). Measured with it (session note 23): CSV and DataFrames loaded after TraitMakie invalidate no PlotCore code and 14 machinery methods (285 on stock). CSV, DataFrames and CairoMakie loaded after TraitDifferentialEquations still invalidate 2–4 ODECore methods and 323 machinery methods: these loads bring pirates of Base (OffsetArrays, StatsBase, Durations), which invalidate by design, and owned methods (InlineStrings, Unitful) that reach Base methods compiled for trait-world callers, which the insertion half does not protect (the load half's callee rule has no insertion counterpart). A pirate-free load set is needed to separate the two.
5. **Closed (s29):** both causes are gone, and new trait code costs about the same in every load order (§3, "Open from session 25"). Cause (i): the policy's compiler code is deleted; the checks run Julia's native compiler in the compiler's world (julia-1.13 bb82a82bbf, TraitWorld f79727c). Cause (ii): the machinery's slow paths are compiled by facts (TraitWorld 624f742), and the compiler's own code stays plain (julia-1.13 c4f7e76a2f). Numbers: `design/evidence.md` §3. *(Resolved in session 24: TraitDifferentialEquations' first solve 55–60 ms and TraitMakie's first PNG 89–97 ms in every load order. Open in its place: **new** trait code defined after big loads, 2.7 s to define a small module and 0.8 s for its first call, against 1.0 s and 0.09 s; the definition-time checks, Julia pieces 7 and 12.)* **Session 26, two causes found:**
   - **(i) The policy's compiler code** fails validation at load when an interpreter-defining package, REPL in every interactive session, loads before TraitWorld: a new key's first call 5.9 s instead of 0.13 s (decision §1.3).
   - **(ii) The machinery's internal functions** are native code that large loads invalidate: `@implement` 1.2–1.7 s after loads, 0.6 s without (decision §1.4). Moving `@implement`'s expansion and checks under the policy (TraitWorld a5e4778) gained little, because their run-time sites reach native internal functions.

   Earlier measurements: Before: TraitDifferentialEquations' first solve takes 0.7–1.05 s with CSV, DataFrames and CairoMakie loaded (73 ms without); TraitMakie's first PNG takes 0.31–0.37 s with CSV and DataFrames before (0.1–0.2 s without; 0.09 s after, patched).
6. *(Fixed in session 26: Julia's type-printer registry, db82407fa6; TraitWorld 6239b1e.)* **TraitWorld invalidates ordinary code** (session 26, TraitReport row 4). `Base.show(io::IO, T::Type{<:CObj})` and `Base.show(io::IO, T::Type{<:TObj})` (`TraitWorld/src/core.jl`) are no pirates, but they invalidate every caller compiled for `show(::IO, ::Type)`: CSV's `parsecolchunk!`, Base's `temp_cleanup_atexit` and others. That costs 1.0 s on CSV's first read with DataFrames loaded. Without the two methods: 2.64 s against 3.6 s. Fix: print trait objects' types another way. The general point: a trait package's own methods of Base functions at abstract signatures widen what plain code compiled, so they need the same care as any package's.
7. *(Fixed in session 26, TraitWorld 29f0a8e. The cause was not piece 5: TraitWorld's load hooks and package callback were native code, invalidated by the loads they watch and compiled again four times per load. They run under the policy now. `using TraitReport`: 0.79–0.82 s patched, 0.71–0.72 s stock.)* **The patched build's images load slowly** (session 26, TraitReport row 5): `using TraitMakie` takes 0.69 s patched against 0.23 s stock, and CSV, DataFrames, Statistics and TraitMakie together take 1.40 s against 0.82 s. It is still on by default (`JULIA_TW_VIEWCACHE`).

Needs a decision on how asserted effects meet run-time sites.

15. **Closed (2026-10-20):** diagnosed (E-141) and fixed (E-146). Plain helpers of TraitWorld (`_tw_type`) and of the demonstrator (`solve`'s front end) were compiled again after the loads, and a generator's code in TraitWorld's image was made invalid by a package loaded before it (Unitful's `Colon(::Any, ::Quantity)`). Fixed in TraitWorld 226db6a (`_tw_type` is machinery; the generators' code fully typed) and TraitDifferentialEquations 99393b9 (a function barrier). Measured with first calls timed whole: every row is the same in every load order but the first gradient, 82–89 against 68–70 ms (open; [evidence §3](design/evidence.md#3-first-calls-and-sessions); `experiments/loadcosts/README.md`).

5. **Closed (s29):** superseded. `design.md` is the consolidated current design (design repository 562c06f, which also marked the register's retired rows as history). It states the policy in the compiler and strict mode as a prototype. Notes 1–4 go to the attic rather than being fixed line by line (`plans/2026-10-19-notes-cleanup-plan.md`). The fresh-eye review's minor findings (`reviews/2026-10-17-consistency-fresh-eye.md`, "Minor"): stale lines in `2-design-choices.md` outside its banner, register rows 19, 20, 69, 91, numbers that disagree between documents, "the policy is package code" (1-overview, 3-wrapping) against the adopted "policy in the platform", strict mode "not built" against its prototype.
4. **Closed (s29):** superseded. [`record/decisions.md`](record/decisions.md) states the adopted rule (tags labelled by the primal signature, implemented in TraitDifferentialEquations), and `notes/1-overview.md` goes to the attic (`plans/2026-10-19-notes-cleanup-plan.md`). The gradient rows are measured: a second loss 17–18 ms in every load order (`design/evidence.md` §3). `notes/1-overview.md` ("Fields as the key") says a new loss under automatic differentiation "compiles the step again, as today". The adopted decision labels tags by the loss's primal signature (`Tag{Sig{A, S}, V}`), so that a second loss of the same signature compiles nothing; the sentence holds only without it. Fix when TraitDifferentialEquations row 5 is built (review of session 26).
163. **Closed (2026-11-11, the user (2026-11-11): "I meant 2 not 1. So 2, 3, 4 with Kept is agreed. We can think about syntax sugar for this later."):** a `where` variable bounded by a trait is the value's type under either attribute; code compiled once reads it from the value ([compiler §6.2](reference/compiler.md#62-compilation-signatures); decisions §3; to build: W-148, C-54). The proposal of the issue (a used variable makes the method open) is in [the alternatives](design/alternatives.md). *The case:* `f(x::C) where {C <: X} = C`, compiled once at the erased `X`, would return `X`.
175. **Closed (2026-11-11, the user (2026-11-11): "I meant 2 not 1. So 2, 3, 4 with Kept is agreed. We can think about syntax sugar for this later."):** what is stored is decided by the storage's type: storage typed by the trait holds the object, `Kept{X}` holds a member with its attribute ([traits §7.8](reference/traits.md#78-keeping-an-argument-with-its-attribute), [§8.1](reference/traits.md#81-fields-and-containers-typed-by-a-trait); decisions §3; to build: W-149, traits T-111). *The case:* the tutorial's `add!` with `keep` stored a `Dynamic{Shape}` at the prompt and the circle in compiled code, so `sc.shapes[1] isa Scalable`, `==` and a `count` answered differently (`experiments/docs-examples/keep_at_prompt.jl`).
